## Resuming

To continue after an interruption: read `docs/dev/plan.md`, this file, and the work order of the first chunk that is not done. Work orders are written one at a time, just before the chunk starts; a missing work order means the chunk has not been specified yet.

## Chunk notes

(Each chunk adds a section below: what exists, deviations from the work order, how to exercise it.)

### C01 Foundation

**Toolchain.** `./dev` runs `docker.io/library/golang:1.27.4` (Go 1.27.4, pinned in `GO_IMAGE` at the top of the script) with `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, `-buildvcs=false`; caches in `${DEVC_DEV_CACHE:-~/.cache/devc-dev}`. `go.mod` says `go 1.27.4`. Dependencies: `spf13/cobra` v1.10.2 (pulls `spf13/pflag`, `inconshreveable/mousetrap` indirectly), `BurntSushi/toml` v1.6.0, `golang.org/x/sys` v0.50.0. Nothing outside the approved list.

**What exists.**

- `cmd/devc`: dispatch on argv[0]; `devc` and `devc-*` run the CLI, `git-remote-devc` and any other name print "not implemented" and exit 2.
- `internal/cli`: cobra root with `--config-dir`, `--state-dir`, `-v` (count: warn, info, debug), hidden empty `box` group; `version`, `doctor [--json]`, `config check`, `config show [ref] [--json]`, `realm ls`, `project ls [--realm r]`, `env ls` (all listings take `--json`). Exit codes 0/1/2; flag errors, unknown subcommands and wrong argument counts exit 2.
- `internal/names`: name rule, `Check` with a precise reason, `ProjectRef`, `BoxName`, `BoxID`, session IDs.
- `internal/paths`: every layout path of spec 5 and plan 3.2, in-box constants and UIDs, `Ensure` (0700 below the state, runtime or config root only).
- `internal/safefs`: `OpenRoot`, `Open`, `ReadFile`, `ReadFrom`, `List` via openat2 with `RESOLVE_BENEATH|NO_SYMLINKS|NO_MAGICLINKS`; directory listing parses getdents itself so `DT_UNKNOWN` never triggers a path-based lstat.
- `internal/execx`: `Runner` (`Run`, `Interactive`), `Real`, `Fake`, typed `*ExitError`, `Redact`, output cap.
- `internal/config`: all schemas of the work order, strict decoding (unknown keys and type errors reported with file and key), loader-applied defaults, all validation rules, lookups, `ResolveProject`, `Resolved`, `View`, `ProblemsFor`. Built-in manifests `assets/harness/{claude,codex}.toml` embedded through package `assets`.
- `internal/engine`: `Engine` interface (`Binary`, `Version`, `Info`, `Ps`, `Inspect`, `Exists`), `Podman` over execx, `Fake`; parsing tested against JSON recorded on this host (`internal/engine/testdata`, user paths sanitised).
- `internal/doctor`: the 14 checks of work order section 9, table and JSON output; all branches unit-tested with a fake root filesystem, fake runner and fake engine.
- `examples/config`: realms `demo` (`hello`, `analysis` with all three zones, savepoints `auto`, a mount of `hello`, a data remote) and `other` (`tools`, plain image); env `base` (Debian trixie-slim Containerfile).
- `test/integration`: helper package `itest` (temporary config/state/runtime/projects dirs from `examples/config`, runs `DEVC_BIN`) and two tests (smoke; broken config rejected).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build && bin/devc doctor`, `bin/devc --config-dir examples/config config check`, `… project ls`, `… config show demo/analysis`; `./dev test`, `./dev itest -v`, `./dev vet`.

**`devc doctor` on this host:** ok engine (5.8.3 from `~/.local/opt/workstation-tools/bin`), rootless, subids, runtime-dir, tools, storage (fuse-overlayfs), selinux (disabled), cgroups, gpu (`/etc/cdi/nvidia.yaml`); warn linger (off), netbackend (slirp4netns), apparmor-userns (restriction on and the conda-installed podman is not covered by any profile in `/etc/apparmor.d`, although rootless podman evidently works), projects-fs (ext4, no reflink); skip kvm.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. `./dev itest` always rebuilds `bin/devc` and `bin/itest` (the work order says build if missing): a stale binary would silently test old code, and the build cache makes it cheap. It translates `go test` flags (`-v`, `-run X`) to test-binary flags and runs from `test/integration`. Added `./dev go …` as a passthrough; `./dev vet` includes `-tags integration`; `./dev test` passes extra flags to `go test` and adds `./...` when no package is named.
2. Session IDs use lower-case base32hex (`0-9a-v`), because only that alphabet sorts lexically in numeric order: 50-bit millisecond time plus 50 random bits, strictly increasing within a process.
3. `config show` without an argument prints the global view (effective directories, engine, box defaults, hubs); it does not infer a project from the working directory, so its output schema does not depend on where it runs. With an argument it accepts `realm/project` or a unique bare name.
4. Read-only commands (`ls`, `show`, `doctor`) run despite configuration problems and print a one-line warning. `Config.ProblemsFor(ref)` returns the problems affecting one project (global, realm, project, its env, its manifests) so lifecycle commands from C02 on can refuse to act. Files with syntax or type errors drop their entity; unknown keys are reported but keep it. A realm directory without `realm.toml` (or a project directory without `project.toml`) is a problem and is skipped. `config check` fails if the config directory does not exist.
5. State directory precedence: `--state-dir` > `DEVC_STATE_DIR` > `state_dir` in `config.toml` > default. `projects_root` and `state_dir` must be absolute or start with `~/`.
6. Validation beyond the work order, all for argument safety or obvious mistakes: `upstream` refuses `<transport>::` URLs (`ext::` runs commands) and unknown schemes; image references, SSH aliases, branch names and globs cannot start with `-`; data paths have no component starting with `-`; `data.root` is one name (name rules) other than `main`; harness `bin` values are unique and not `devc`, `devc-*` or `git-remote-devc` (argv[0] dispatch); a user manifest's file name must equal its `id`; a realm override to `env-token`/`api-key` needs `auth.env` in the manifest; `home.shared` only with `private`, `home.per_box` only with `shared`; `launch.resume` must contain `{id}`; duplicates in harnesses, grants, ports, mounts and zones are errors.
7. Types and defaults not fixed by the work order: `box.ports` is a list of integers; `[[mounts]] mode` defaults to `ro`; `savepoints.keep = 0` means keep all; project `box.pids = 0` inherits; manifest defaults are `install.method = "none"`, `auth.method = "none"`, `home.strategy = "private"`, `input.adapter = "paste"`, `submit_key = "Enter"`, `seed.when = "missing"`, `display = id`.
8. `paths` getters panic on a name that did not pass `names` (a programming error, never user input), rather than returning errors from every getter.
9. `execx.Interactive` has no default timeout (attached sessions last hours); `Run` defaults to 2 minutes, sends SIGTERM on timeout and SIGKILL 5 s later. The fake picks the longest matching prefix, the latest registration among equals.
10. Doctor details: `subids` also accepts the engine's actual ID mapping (covers subordinate IDs from LDAP/sssd); `cgroups` reads delegated controllers from `podman info` when rootless, else from `user@<uid>.service`; `linger` falls back to `/var/lib/systemd/linger/<user>`; "distribution path" for AppArmor means a profile in `/etc/apparmor.d` that attaches to the resolved engine binary and grants `userns`; `projects-fs` probes the nearest existing ancestor when the projects root does not exist yet (and says so); a missing `ssh` is a warning; `vfs` storage and CNI also warn; SELinux permissive counts as enabled.

**Notes for later chunks.**

- Box names `devc-<realm>-<project>` can collide (realm `a-b` + project `c` vs realm `a` + project `b-c`). C02 should verify the `devc.realm`/`devc.project` labels whenever it finds a container by name, and refuse to create a box whose name is taken by another project.
- `Config.ProblemsFor` exists for fail-closed lifecycle commands; nothing uses it yet.
- `engine.Engine` and `engine.Fake` must grow together when methods are added.

### C02 Boxes

**What exists.**

- **C01 amendments.** `[[root_log]]` is gone from `env.toml` (an old entry is now an "unknown key" problem); `devc root` appends `{"time","project","argv"}` lines to `<config>/envs/<env>/root-log.jsonl` (`paths.EnvRootLog`), the only file devc writes below the config directory. Global `[selinux] mode = "labels" | "disable"` (default `labels`, shown by `config show`). Realm names are `^[a-z0-9]{1,32}$` (`names.ValidRealm`, `names.Check(KindRealm)`, `paths` getters, config loading); the example realms already complied. Every container found by name is checked for `devc.realm`, `devc.project` and `devc.role=box` (`box.Manager.Find`); a foreign one is never touched and shows as `conflict` in `ls`. `up`, `rebuild`, `sh`, `run` and `root` refuse while `Config.ProblemsFor(ref)` is non-empty and print the problems.
- **`internal/engine`.** Image inspect/exists, build, pull (both stream progress), create, init, start, stop, remove, exec (captured and interactive; user, workdir, env, privileged, `-i`/`-t` with `--detach-keys=`), unshare; inspect now parses mounts, `HostConfig` (security options, caps, memory, pids, network, init), effective/bounding caps, user, env. Every engine command runs with working directory `/`. `engine.Fake` implements all of it in memory (create parses `--name`, `--label`, `--mount`, …). `execx.Cmd` gained `Stdout`/`Stderr` for `Interactive`.
- **`internal/toolbox`.** `Sync(exe, version)` (flock-protected, idempotent): `devc/<version>-<sha12>/devc`, `devc/current`, `bin/devc -> ../devc/current/devc`, `helper-rootfs/bin/devc` (hard link, else copy; anything else in the helper rootfs is removed), `seccomp.json`; everything 0755/0644 because in-box root without capabilities maps to a subordinate UID. `List()`. Commands `devc toolbox sync|ls [--json]`.
- **Seccomp.** `assets/seccomp/default.json` is containers/common `common/v0.67.1` (the version Podman 5.8.3 uses), source and licence in `assets/seccomp/SOURCE.txt`. `toolbox.DeriveProfile` removes `clone`, `clone3`, `unshare` from the unconditional allow rule, adds `clone`/`unshare` allowed only with `(flags & CLONE_NEWUSER) == 0` (s390 variant on argument 1), leaves `clone3` to the default action (ENOSYS), replaces each "allow socket if domain != x" rule by range rules, and adds `socket` with `(domain & 0xffffffff) == 38` → EAFNOSUPPORT. Strict decoding and self-checks make the derivation fail loudly if a future base changes shape. Unit tests evaluate the rules for both capability sets and three architectures and check that undoing the documented changes gives the base back.
- **Env images.** `localhost/devc-env/<env>:latest` built from the env directory with labels `devc.role=env`, `devc.env=<env>` and the build args; base-only envs and `image =` projects are pulled when missing; `build_from_repo` fails with "not supported yet". `devc env build <env> [--no-cache] [--pull]`; `devc env ls` shows whether the image exists and when it was built.
- **`internal/box`.** `BuildSpec` (golden-file tests in `internal/box/testdata`, regenerate with `./dev test ./internal/box/ -run Golden -args -update`), `Contributor`/`Spec.AddMount`/`AddEnv`/`AddLabel` for later chunks, host directories, `/etc/devc` (`box.json` + a copy of `<config>/dotfiles/`), image selection, `Manager` with `Up`, `Down`, `Remove`, `Rebuild`, `List`, `Shell`, `Run`, `Root`. After every start: `podman exec --user 0 … devc box fix-identity`, then a wait of up to 20 s for `<runtime>/<box-id>/ready` (removed before each start; the wait also notices a box that stopped).
- **`internal/netpolicy`.** `BoxArgs(level)`: `none` → `--network none`; `open` → engine default; `internet`/`allowlist` → engine default plus a "not enforced yet" warning.
- **`internal/boxside`.** `box init` (home skeleton from `/etc/skel` when the home holds only mount points, dotfile links, `~/.local/bin`, `/run/devc/spool`, `ready`, waits for SIGTERM/SIGINT, ignores SIGHUP), `box fix-identity`, `box shell`, `box selftest [--json]` (12 items, see below).
- **Commands.** `devc up [ref] [--recreate] [--build]`, `down`, `rm [--home]`, `rebuild`, `ls [--json]`, `sh`, `run [ref] -- cmd…`, `root [ref] -- cmd…`, `env build`, `toolbox sync|ls`, hidden `box init|fix-identity|shell|selftest`.
- **Integration tests** (`test/integration/box_test.go`): `TestBoxLifecycle` (items 1–6), `TestBoxDrift` (7), `TestIdentityAndNetwork` (8 and 10), `TestMountsAndRealms` (9). The whole suite takes about 80 s here; the base env image is built once per run and all test images are removed at the end.

**Box arguments**, in this order: `--name --hostname --init --tz local --userns=keep-id:uid=1000,gid=1000 --user 1000:1000 --passwd-entry dev:*:1000:1000:dev:/home/dev:/bin/sh --http-proxy=false --cap-drop=ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges --security-opt seccomp=<toolbox>/seccomp.json [label=level:… | label=disable] [--pids-limit] [--memory] [--network none] --workdir --env… --mount… --label… <image> /opt/devc/bin/devc box init`. `devc.confighash` is SHA-256 over the arguments (without the hash label), the image reference and command, the image ID and the SHA-256 of the seccomp profile.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Extra creation flags.** `podman create` has no `--passwd=false` (only `run` does), so boxes get `--passwd-entry` as in spec 6.2; it covers the moment before `fix-identity`, which then makes the files canonical (Podman adds nothing once UID 1000 exists). `--http-proxy=false`: the host's proxy variables are host environment, not policy, and may carry credentials. Engine-default network levels pass no `--network` at all.
2. **Seccomp beyond the work order.** The kernel truncates the socket domain to 32 bits while seccomp compares 64, so `socket(0x100000026, …)` creates an AF_ALG socket under the base profile (verified here). Every allow range therefore requires the upper 32 bits to be zero (aligned power-of-two blocks with `SCMP_CMP_MASKED_EQ`, about 70 rules), and the AF_ALG refusal matches the low 32 bits. The no-`CAP_AUDIT_WRITE` ranges also exclude 40, which removes the base profile's overlap of "allow domain != 16" with "refuse domain == 40". No rule overlaps another with a different action, so nothing depends on libseccomp's tie-breaking.
3. **Hash inputs** include the seccomp digest (the engine reads the profile only at creation), so a devc upgrade that changes the profile shows as drift.
4. **`up --recreate`** always recreates; `rebuild` builds the env (if it has a Containerfile) and recreates; image projects are not re-pulled. On an already running box, `sh`, `run` and `root` exec even if it has drifted; a stopped box is started through `up`, so drift stops them.
5. **Fail-closed scope.** `down`, `rm` and `ls` still work with configuration problems (they only stop or remove, or read); all commands that create, start or exec refuse.
6. **Nested mount points.** Before every create/start, each mount that lies inside another mount (today `/home/dev/.cache` in the home) must be a real directory at that place in the parent's host source; a symlink planted by the box (for example `.cache -> /opt/devc`) would otherwise redirect the mount inside the box and shadow the toolbox. Missing mount points are created; anything else is refused.
7. **`devc box shell`** (not in the work order) chooses the shell inside the box (`box.shell` from `box.json`, else zsh, bash, sh in `/bin`, `/usr/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`) and execs it as a login shell; `devc sh` runs it with `-i` and `-t` only when stdin is a terminal. All `devc box` commands refuse unless `DEVC_BOX=1` and `/etc/devc/box.json` exist, so `fix-identity` can never rewrite a host's `/etc/passwd`.
8. **`fix-identity`** replaces entries matching UID/GID 1000 or the name `dev` (and 2000/`vault`) with canonical lines at the position of the first match, keeps a replaced group's member list, keeps every other line byte for byte, writes by rename (falling back to in-place on `EBUSY`/`EXDEV`), and leaves `/etc/shadow` alone. `vault` has home `/nonexistent` and a nologin shell.
9. **`box init` and dotfiles.** Dotfile links replace existing symlinks and skip real files; links to removed dotfiles are deleted. The host copies only regular files and directories from `<config>/dotfiles/` (symlinks and special files are skipped with a log line, `.git` is skipped), so a symlink in the dotfiles repository cannot copy an arbitrary host file into a box.
10. **`box selftest`** items: identity, capabilities (CapEff), no-new-privileges, `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` and `clone(CLONE_NEWUSER|CLONE_FS)` must fail with ENOSYS (the kernel alone would say EINVAL, so ENOSYS proves the seccomp rule), `clone3` ENOSYS, AF_ALG EAFNOSUPPORT with and without upper bits, ordinary sockets still work, `/opt/devc` and `/etc/devc` read-only, project directory writable. Under the unmodified base profile all six seccomp items fail.
11. **Integration-test naming** deviates from README ("containers named `devc-it-…`, labelled `devc.test=1`"): box names and labels are fixed by the plan, so the harness renames the copied example configuration instead: realms `itdemo`/`itother`, env `itbase` (boxes `devc-itdemo-hello` etc.). Containers labelled with these realms are removed before and after every test; `localhost/devc-env/it*` images are removed at the end of the run, pulled Debian images only if they were absent at the start.
12. **Host bug worked around in tests.** On this host, `statx()` asking for the birth time on a file in an image layer hangs forever in fuse-overlayfs 1.17 with libfuse 3.18.2 (the process sits in `request_wait_answer`). Debian trixie's `apt-get update` verifies signatures with `sqv`, which does exactly that, so the example `base` env cannot be built here; `stat FILE` hangs too, while `ls -l` and `stat -c %s` work. The test harness builds the base env from `debian:bookworm-slim` (apt uses gpgv); the example configuration stays on trixie. Not a devc problem; see LEFTOVER.
13. **AppArmor.** Nothing box-specific: rootless Podman applies no AppArmor profile (`podman info` reports it disabled), and devc never passes `apparmor=…`. The host-side user-namespace restriction remains a `doctor` check (unchanged; it warns here, as expected).
14. **SELinux.** Mode `labels`: `label=level:<realm level>` when the realm sets one, never `:z`/`:Z`, and `restorecon -R -- <project dir>` (from `/`) during `up` when the tool exists, otherwise a warning. Mode `disable`: `label=disable` plus a one-line warning. Both only when `/sys/fs/selinux/enforce` exists; tested with fakes only.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`, then with a config directory: `bin/devc up demo/hello`, `bin/devc sh demo/hello`, `bin/devc run demo/hello -- /opt/devc/bin/devc box selftest`, `bin/devc root demo/hello -- apt-get install -y …`, `bin/devc ls`, `bin/devc down|rm [--home] demo/hello`, `bin/devc toolbox ls`, `bin/devc env ls`. Tests: `./dev test`, `./dev itest -v`.

**Notes for later chunks.**

- C03: add mounts and environment through a `box.Contributor` appended to `Manager.Contributors` (the CLI builds the manager in `internal/cli/boxcmds.go`); nested mounts below the home get the mount-point check automatically. `/etc/devc/harness/…` can be written next to `box.json` in `writeEtc` (`internal/box/dirs.go`). `boxside.Info` is the box's description of itself (schema 1).
- C07: the place for firewall rules is marked in `createAndStart` (between create and start; use `Engine.Init`); `netpolicy.BoxArgs` is the single decision point and its warning text must go.
- C06: data-zone mounts nested inside a project directory are checked like `.cache`, but that directory may be mounted read-write into another running box (`[[mounts]] mode = "rw"`), which could race the check; consider refusing nested zones under rw-shared projects or checking inside the helper container.
- `Manager.Find` is the only way to get a box container; use it.

### C03 Harnesses

**What exists.**

- **Static tmux.** `devc toolbox build tmux` builds tmux 3.8 (tarball SHA-256 pinned in `internal/toolbox/tmux.go`, verified in the container before unpacking) as a static-pie musl binary in a throwaway `docker.io/library/alpine:3.24.4` container, and installs `tmux/3.8/tmux`, `tmux/current`, `bin/tmux -> ../tmux/current/tmux` and `terminfo/` (Alpine's `ncurses-terminfo` plus `kitty-terminfo`, about 8 MB). `toolbox ls [--json]` shows the tmux version, the pinned version, a recipe id and the shims. `devc up` warns once per state directory (marker `local/warned-tmux-missing`) while tmux is missing.
- **Installer image.** `localhost/devc-aux/installer:latest`, built on first use from the embedded `assets/installer/Containerfile` (Debian bookworm-slim with bash, curl, ca-certificates, tar, xz-utils, unzip, git); a `devc.aux.hash` label rebuilds it when the Containerfile changes. Build context: `<state>/cache/aux/installer/`.
- **Harness installs** (`internal/toolbox/harness.go`). `devc harness ls [--json]`, `devc harness install <id> [--version v]`, `devc update [<id>…]`, `devc harness gc`. Layout `harness/<id>/v/<stamp>/` (stamp `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ`, `-N` appended on a collision), `harness/<id>/current -> v/<stamp>`, `harness/<id>/installed.json`, per-harness flock. `script`: the version directory is mounted read-write at `/opt/devc/harness/<id>/v/<stamp>` with `HOME=<that>/home`, a constant bash script downloads `url` and runs it with the arguments as positional parameters. `local`: the host copies `install.path` to `exe`. Then a second container (network none, version directory read-only) checks `exe` and runs `version_cmd`; `current` switches only after that succeeds, otherwise the new directory is deleted. `none`: nothing is installed, `exe` is an absolute path in the image. Auxiliary containers run with `--security-opt no-new-privileges`, the box seccomp profile and the labels `devc.role=aux`, `devc.aux=<purpose>`. Built-in manifests: Claude `install.sh [{version}]`, exe `home/.local/bin/claude`; Codex `install.sh` with `CODEX_HOME={dir}/home/.codex`, exe `home/.local/bin/codex`.
- **Shims and launcher.** `shims/<bin> -> ../bin/devc` for every installed harness (created by `install` and again by `up`). `cmd/devc` routes every name other than `devc`, `devc-*` and `git-remote-devc` to the launcher, which looks the name up among the `bin` fields of `/etc/devc/harness/*.json`. `devc box launch <id> [-- args]` does the same by id. `internal/boxside/launch.go`: validate the description, refuse an executable that is not executable or resolves to the running devc (`os.SameFile`), settle the session ID, export `DEVC_SESSION`, `DEVC_HARNESS`, `DEVC_LAUNCH_DEPTH`, call the `registerSession` placeholder, add the manifest `[env]` and `home.env`, granted secrets and the credential from `/run/devc-secrets`, then `exec` with argv `[bin, launch.args…, user args…]`.
- **Per-box files at `devc up`** (`internal/harness`, wired as `box.Contributor`, `box.Preparer` and `box.Cleaner` in `internal/cli/boxcmds.go`). `/etc/devc/harness/<id>.json` (schema 1, type `boxside.Launch`) and `/etc/devc/harness/<id>/seed/…` for every listed and installed harness; a listed but uninstalled one gives a warning with the install command. Baselines are rendered for every listed harness (installed or not) into `local/boxes/<r>/<p>/baseline/<id>/` and mounted read-only at the directory of the manifest's baseline paths. Stale descriptions and baselines are removed.
- **Baseline.** User file `<config>/baseline/<id>/settings.json` or `settings.toml` (either format for either harness; both at once is an error), required keys from the template merged over it (`harness.Merge`: devc wins on scalars, objects merge, lists concatenate with the user's entries first and without repeating devc's). Claude `managed-settings.json`: `cleanupPeriodDays` 3650, `permissions.defaultMode` `bypassPermissions`, `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt`, `autoMemoryDirectory` `/ctx/memory`, `env.DISABLE_UPDATES`/`DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER`, command hooks for `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, `Notification`, `SessionEnd`. Codex `/etc/codex/config.toml`: `sandbox_mode`, `approval_policy`, `check_for_update_on_startup = false`, `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"`, `developer_instructions`, inline `[[hooks.<Event>]]` for `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, `PermissionRequest`, `SessionEnd`. Instructions: the generated sandbox description (template `sandbox.md.tmpl`; network sentence per level; data paragraph when the project has zones), then `baseline/instructions.md`, `baseline/<id>/instructions.md`, `realms/<realm>/instructions.md`.
- **State mounts.** private: each `shared` subdirectory from `durable/realms/<r>/harness/<id>/<sub>`; shared: `durable/realms/<r>/harness/<id>/home` at the harness home and each `per_box` subdirectory from `local/boxes/<r>/<p>/harness/<id>/<sub>`. The mounts depend only on the configuration, never on what is installed, so installing does not cause drift (tested). `box.json` gained `home_mounts` (top-level home entries that are mount points), so `box init` still treats a home holding only `.cache` and harness mount points as fresh; `box init` then applies the seeds.
- **Secrets and logins.** `internal/secrets`: `Store` interface, `FileStore` (`durable/realms/<realm>/secrets/<name>`, 0600/0700, no symlinks followed, 64 KiB cap), `Deliver` (0400 files by rename, so a running box sees updates) and `Remove`. Secret names may use upper-case letters and `_` (`names.ValidSecret`). `devc secret set|ls|rm`, `devc login <realm> <harness> [--token-stdin]`, `devc login status <realm> [harness]` (exit 1 when something is missing). Delivery at every `up` into `<runtime>/<box-id>-secrets/`, mounted read-only at `/run/devc-secrets` (always, even when empty); removed on `down` and `rm`.
- **Fake harness.** `test/fakeharness` (built by `./dev build` and `./dev itest` as `bin/fakeagent`), manifests `fake` (private, shared `state`, a `missing` and an `always` seed, env-token, baseline) and `fakeshared` (shared, per-box `perbox`, shared-home login) in `test/fakeharness/manifests/`. C04 places are marked `// C04:`.
- **Placeholder** `devc box hook <kind> <event>`: reads and discards stdin, prints nothing, exits 0, because the baselines already call it.
- **Amendments from C02.** Seccomp: `socketcall` refused (see deviation 1), unit test and a new self-test item `socketcall` (int 0x80 probe in a child process, amd64 only). The example env and the installer image use Debian bookworm-slim; the itest harness no longer rewrites trixie. The Alpine tmux build is not affected by the statx hang (musl and busybox do not ask for the birth time); nothing needed.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **socketcall gets EPERM, not ENOSYS.** runc drops rules whose action equals the default (ERRNO ENOSYS). Without an explicit unconditional socketcall rule, libseccomp rewrites every `socket` rule on the x86 sub-architecture into a socketcall rule (the domain comparison replaced by the call number), and rules with different actions then collide: containers fail with "error adding seccomp rule for syscall socket: file exists" (verified). An explicit EPERM rule placed before the socket rules makes those rewritten rules redundant. Consequence: 32-bit programs that use socketcall (32-bit glibc does) have no sockets at all; a libc using the direct calls is unaffected and meets the AF_ALG rule. The derivation fails if a future base profile's default is EPERM.
2. **Secrets mount at `/run/devc-secrets`** (work order) instead of `/run/devc/secrets` (plan 3.10): `/run/devc` is box-writable, and a mount nested in it could be redirected by a planted symlink.
3. **Session ID reuse.** `DEVC_SESSION` is kept only when valid and `DEVC_HARNESS` is unset; a harness started from inside another harness's session gets a new ID instead of sharing its parent's.
4. **Method `none`** counts as installed when `install.exe` is set; `harness install` only creates the shim; without `exe` the manifest loads but `up` warns. Validation: `local` needs `path` (absolute or `~/`), `exe` relative; `script` URL must be https without user info; baseline settings and instructions must share one dedicated directory (at least two levels deep, not under `/etc/devc`, `/opt/devc`, `/home`, `/w`, `/ctx`, `/run`, `/usr`, …), because that directory is mounted over the image.
5. **Installer details.** Containers from the installer image get empty tmpfs mounts at `/home`, `/opt` and `/tmp`, so the directories above devc's bind mounts are not image-layer directories. On this host that is also required: Claude Code's installer (Bun) `lstat`s every component of its target path with the birth-time mask, and `statx("/opt")` on the image layer hung in fuse-overlayfs exactly as in C02. `{version}`: an argument containing it is dropped when no version is requested; `--version` is refused for `local`. The installer container runs as container root (the host user) with podman's default capabilities and the host proxy variables (podman's default; boxes keep `--http-proxy=false`), so vendor installers that untar as root work. The check container gets `HOME=/tmp` and no network. `harness gc` keeps the union of current and the two newest directories (two when current is the newest).
6. **Templates** for seeds and settings come from `<config>/harness/<name>` for user manifests, else from the embedded `assets/harness/templates/`; `sandbox.md.tmpl` may be overridden the same way. Template functions: `json`, `toml`, `join`, `hook`.
7. **Logins** run as container root with a temporary home at `/home/dev` (below `local/tmp/`, removed afterwards), the toolbox read-only and network on; a login command whose first word is the harness's `bin` runs the real executable. `shared-home` mounts the realm's `harness/<id>/home` at the harness home. `--token-stdin` is refused for `shared-home`; `api-key` prompts (or reads stdin). Granted secrets are exported only when their name is a valid environment variable name; others are files only.
8. **Hidden prompts** use termios from `golang.org/x/sys` (echo off, restored on SIGINT) rather than adding `golang.org/x/term`.
9. **Integration-test caches.** The installer image stays between runs; the tmux build is cached in `${DEVC_DEV_CACHE:-~/.cache/devc-dev}/itest/tmux-<version>-<recipe>/` and copied into each test toolbox (`DEVC_TEST_REBUILD_TMUX=1` forces a build). `alpine:3.24.4` is in the list of pulled images removed at the end when it was absent at the start. `./dev itest` passes `DEVC_FAKEAGENT` and `DEVC_ITEST_CACHE`.

**Optional network test** (`DEVC_TEST_NETWORK_HARNESS=1`, run once by hand). With the host's default storage (fuse-overlayfs 1.17) the Claude Code installer hangs: its Bun binary `lstat`s `/opt` and then `/etc` with the birth-time mask, and `statx()` on an image-layer directory never returns (the C02 bug). The tmpfs mounts fixed `/opt`; `/etc` cannot be moved off the image. Run instead against a temporary podman store with native rootless overlay (`CONTAINERS_STORAGE_CONF` with `mount_program = ""`; kernel 6.8 supports it, and `stat` there does not hang): pass. `claude` installed as `2.1.325 (Claude Code)`, `codex` as `codex-cli 0.169.0`; `claude --version` and `codex --version` in a box through the shims both exit 0 (Claude prints the missing-credential line first). The temporary store was deleted afterwards. Expect Claude Code itself to hang in boxes on this host's default storage for the same reason; see LEFTOVER.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; `bin/devc toolbox build tmux`; `bin/devc harness install claude` (or a manifest with `method = "local"`); `bin/devc login <realm> claude` / `--token-stdin`; `bin/devc secret set <realm> NAME`; `bin/devc up <ref>`; `bin/devc run <ref> -- claude --version`; `bin/devc harness ls`, `bin/devc update`, `bin/devc harness gc`. Tests: `./dev test`, `./dev itest -v` (harness tests in `test/integration/harness_test.go`), `DEVC_TEST_NETWORK_HARNESS=1 ./dev itest -run TestHarnessNetwork -v`.

**Notes for later chunks.**

- C04: fill `registerSession` in `internal/boxside/launch.go` (it has the description and the prepared argv/env/session) and replace the `box hook` placeholder in `internal/cli/inbox.go`; the fake agent's `// C04:` markers show where hook calls go. `boxside.Launch` already carries `resume`, `hooks`, `status`, `input`, `memory` and `transcripts`. tmux: `/opt/devc/bin/tmux`, `TERMINFO_DIRS=/opt/devc/terminfo:`. The installer image has git for the context repository (`toolbox.InstallerImage`, `toolbox.EnsureInstallerImage`).
- Harness installs never remove a version a running box uses except through `harness gc`; the spec's "only when no box uses them" needs a reference check (with the old `toolbox/devc/<id>` builds from C02).

### C03r Fixes after review A

All 21 findings of `docs/dev/reviews/A-review.md` are addressed; `docs/dev/reviews/A-response.md` has one paragraph per finding. Summary of what changed:

- **Box lifecycle** (`internal/box`). `up` decides from the existing container first (no-op, finish a start, drift refusal, recreate) and writes `/etc/devc`, baselines, launch descriptions, secrets, mount sources and mount points only on a path that creates or starts the box. A failed create/start is rolled back (created container removed, started container stopped, secrets removed). A running box without the readiness marker is not "already running": `up`, `sh`, `run` and `root` finish its start. Every lifecycle operation holds `local/boxes/<r>/<p>/lock` (new package `internal/flock`, bounded wait of 10 minutes). Root execs (`devc root`, `fix-identity`) run with `PATH=/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin`, `HOME=/root` and working directory `/`. `rm --home` empties the home in the helper container (`devc box rm-worker`, `--rootfs <toolbox>/helper-rootfs:O`, network none, only `DAC_OVERRIDE`/`FOWNER`) and then removes the empty directory.
- **Box arguments.** `--entrypoint /opt/devc/bin/devc` with command `box init`; `TERMINFO_DIRS=/opt/devc/terminfo:` (the trailing empty entry keeps the image's database; verified on bookworm, whose ncurses searches its compiled-in directories anyway); `gpu` → `--device nvidia.com/gpu=all`, `kvm` → `--device /dev/kvm --group-add keep-groups`, `nested` refused. A configured memory or PID limit whose controller is not delegated fails `up`; `memory = "none"` and `pids = -1` switch a limit off (global `pids = 0` is now a problem). The arguments changed, so every box created before C03r shows as drifted once (`devc up --recreate`).
- **Network.** `internet` and `allowlist` are refused until C07; only `none` and `open` create boxes. **The example realm `demo` sets `network.default = "open"` explicitly for now; C07 must switch it back to `internet`.** The default for a realm without the key stays `internet` (refused). The sandbox description only describes levels in force.
- **Filesystem.** `safefs.Root.MkdirAll`/`RequireDirs` (mkdirat plus openat2 without symlinks, per component); `paths.Ensure` and the new `paths.EnsureBeneath` (projects root) use them, as does the mount-point preparation. Manifests whose `home.shared`/`home.per_box` entries nest are refused, and so are projects whose harness homes overlap.
- **Configuration.** A user manifest that exists but fails to load (or a manifest directory that cannot be listed) blocks every project using that id; the built-in manifest is never used instead (`Config.HarnessProblems`). `auth.method = "shared-home"` requires `home.strategy = "shared"` (manifests and realm overrides). Manifests gained `baseline.files` (template-only files in the baseline directory) and `baseline.settings.also` (more paths for the same settings).
- **Codex baseline.** `/etc/codex/requirements.toml` (allowed approval policy `never`, sandbox mode `danger-full-access`, `[features] hooks = true`, the five managed hooks) plus the settings in both `/etc/codex/config.toml` and `/etc/codex/managed_config.toml`; hooks are no longer in `config.toml`. `CODEX_RELEASE = "{version}"` passes `--version`; `{version}` works in `install.env`; `--version` is refused for manifests without the placeholder.
- **Harness installs** (`internal/toolbox`). Publication writes `installed.json` first and switches `current` last under a short publish lock (readers take it shared), restoring the old metadata on failure; switches are logged in `harness/<id>/history.jsonl`. Launch descriptions name the concrete version directory; `devc update` and `harness install` rewrite the descriptions of all boxes that have one (running boxes start the new version at the next launch). `harness gc` keeps current, the two newest, every version a description names and every version current since the oldest running box started; it refuses to remove anything when it cannot list the running boxes.
- **Secrets.** Delivery fails unless the secrets directory is on tmpfs or ramfs, or `DEVC_ALLOW_PERSISTENT_SECRETS=1`.
- **Containers.** Every auxiliary container and every image build runs with `--http-proxy=false` (C03 deviation 5 is reversed). `execx` runs every command from `/` unless a directory is given.
- **Integration tests.** Each run draws a nonce: realms `itdemo<nonce>`/`itother<nonce>`, envs `itbase<nonce>`/`ituid<nonce>`. devc honours `DEVC_TEST_RUN=<nonce>` by labelling every container and image it creates with `devc.test=1` and `devc.test.run=<nonce>` (outside the box definition, so the hash is unchanged); cleanup removes exactly what carries the run's label (the shared installer image excepted). Runtime directories live below `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`.

**Deviations from the work order.**

1. **socketcall stays EPERM.** Removing `socketcall` from the allow lists (as ordered) was tried: every container fails with "error adding seccomp rule for syscall socket: file exists" under runc, as C03 had found. An explicit ENOSYS rule is dropped by runc (equal to the default action) and `SCMP_ACT_TRACE` (ENOSYS without a tracer) could be bypassed by a tracer in the box. The C03 rule and self-test are unchanged; the reasons are in `internal/toolbox/seccomp.go`.
2. **`harness install` also rewrites launch descriptions**, not only `devc update` (E1): otherwise a new installation would no longer reach running boxes at their next launch, as it did through `current` before.
3. **Running-but-not-ready boxes** that this invocation did not start are not stopped when finishing their start fails; `up` reports the error and says to run `down` and `up`. Rollback only undoes what the invocation itself did.
4. **`up` on a running box writes nothing** (consequence of A3): to deliver a new secret or baseline to a running box, restart it.
5. **Test-run labels in production code.** `DEVC_TEST_RUN` is read by the CLI (`app.engine`) and only adds labels; it is the mechanism D1 needed for "every object carries a label with that run's nonce".

**How to exercise.** `./dev test`; `./dev itest -v`. By hand: plant `~/.local/bin/apt-get` in a box and run `devc root -- apt-get --version`; set a realm to `network.default = "internet"` and see `up` refuse; `DEVC_RUNTIME_DIR=/some/disk/dir devc up` with a granted secret fails unless `DEVC_ALLOW_PERSISTENT_SECRETS=1`; `devc harness gc` while a box runs keeps the versions it may use.

### C04 Sessions

**What exists.**

- **`internal/proto`** (new). The event format of plan 3.6 with the extra type `harness` (the harness's own session ID, transcript path, working directory, source). One data struct per type; `ParseSpoolLine` decodes a spool line strictly (no unknown fields, no `seq`/`at`, nothing after the object, version 1, session equal to the file's, RFC 3339 timestamp, known type) and validates the data field by field with limits (line 64 KiB, prompt text 2,000 and message/question text 4,000 characters plus the marker ` …[truncated]`, paths absolute without NUL, names valid, enums checked); the journal stores the re-encoded data. `Printable`/`PrintableText` neutralise control characters before box-written text reaches a terminal.
- **`internal/tmux`** (new). Every tmux command line: `NewSession`, `NewWindow`, `TagWindow`, `Attach`, `KillPane`, `KillWindow`, `ListPanes`/`ParsePanes`, the pane-died hook, the environment cleaning (`Env`: no `DEVC_SESSION`, `DEVC_HARNESS`, `DEVC_LAUNCH_DEPTH`, `DEVC_WORKTREE`, `TMUX*`; `TERMINFO_DIRS` set) and `Config()`, the content of `/etc/devc/tmux.conf` (written by `writeEtc` at every start through devc). All invocations are `/opt/devc/bin/tmux -L devc -f /etc/devc/tmux.conf …`.
- **In the box** (`internal/boxside`). `devc box hook <kind> <event>` (kinds `claude`, `codex`, `fake`; 1 MiB input cap; never prints, always exits 0, flags are not parsed; malformed or oversized input becomes a `note`). `devc box session start --id --harness --worktree [--detach] [-- args]` (checks the launch description, the executable, tmux and the configuration before creating anything; creates the session `devc` or a window in it; tags it; without `--detach` execs a tmux client attached through its own grouped session), `session ended` (run by the pane-died hook: writes `session.end` with exit status or signal unless the spool already has one, then removes the pane), `session ps [--json]` (tagged live panes plus processes of the user whose initial environment has `DEVC_SESSION`), `session stop <id>`. The launcher's `registerSession` writes `session.start` (harness, worktree from `DEVC_WORKTREE` or the working directory, cwd, PID). `AppendEvents` validates every event as the host would and appends all lines in one `O_APPEND|O_NOFOLLOW` write.
- **Host** (`internal/session`, new). `Store.Ingest` reads `<runtime>/<box-id>/spool/` through safefs (the directory beneath the host-made runtime directory, so a symlinked `spool` is refused; only regular files named `<session ID>.jsonl`), complete lines only from the offsets in `local/boxes/<r>/<p>/spool-offsets.json`, assigns `seq` per session continuing from the journal, adds `at`, appends to `durable/realms/<r>/journal/<p>/<session>.jsonl` (0600), writes a `note` for a spool file shorter than its offset, removes a fully ingested file of an ended session (`safefs.RemoveFile`, new, with `safefs.Size`). `Store.Refresh` adds reconciliation, `Derive`/`Sessions` the listing.
- **Context repository** (`internal/ctxrepo` host side, `boxside.ContextWorker` in the container). A bare repository at `durable/realms/<r>/context/.git`; every operation runs `devc box context-worker commit|diff|log` in a throwaway installer-image container (network none, no capabilities, no new privileges, box seccomp profile, the context directory at `/ctx` and the toolbox read-only).
- **Commands.** `devc <bin> [ref] [-w worktree] [--detach] [-- args]` for every manifest (`devc claude`, `devc codex`, `devc fake` …), `devc attach [ref] [session]`, `devc ps [ref] [--all] [--json]`, `devc stop <session>`, `devc context commit|log|diff [realm]`; `devc ls` has a SESSIONS column (`2, 1 needs you`; JSON `sessions: {live, needs_you}`). `box.Manager` gained `StopHooks` (run around stopping in `down` and `rm`; failures are warnings) and `State`.
- **Fake harness.** Reports through `devc box hook fake` (receiver `FAKEAGENT_HOOK`, default `/opt/devc/bin/devc`): `harness` + `idle` at start, `prompt` + `working` and `message` + `done` per line, `question` + `blocked` for `ask <text>` (the next line is the answer), `session.end` at `exit` or end of input; `--no-end` suppresses the end, `die [code]` exits at once without one. `-p` reports one turn and the end.
- **Tests.** Unit: tmux command construction; hook mappings from recorded inputs (including malformed, oversized, NUL-carrying); event validation; spool writer; session start/ended/ps/stop with a fake tmux; ingestion (offsets, incomplete lines, an oversized line across chunks and runs, truncation, removal, hostile spool entries, symlinked spool), derivation, reconciliation, live-report parsing; context scan and an end-to-end worker run with git; CLI refusals, registration, chooser, JSON escaping; box stop hooks. Integration (`test/integration/session_test.go`): `TestSessions` (items 1–10 and 12, plus interactive `devc fake`, the tmux hook path, `stop`) and `TestContextRepository` (item 11).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Window command.** The window runs `/opt/devc/bin/devc box launch <id> -- args…` instead of the shim path: tmux runs a one-argument command through `sh -c`, and only an argument vector of two or more is executed directly. It does exactly what the shim does.
2. **End of a pane.** `pane-exited` fires after the pane is gone, so its exit status is lost; devc sets `remain-on-exit on` and uses `pane-died`, whose `run-shell` sees `#{pane_dead_status}`/`#{pane_dead_signal}`, and the helper removes the dead pane afterwards. Both are set globally in `tmux.conf` (before `~/.tmux.conf`) and again per window. A harness that exits before its window is tagged (milliseconds) gets no `session.end` from tmux; reconciliation marks it `lost`. tmux turns tabs in format output into `_`, so `list-panes` output is space-separated with the box-controlled session option last.
3. **Start and attach.** `devc <harness>` without `--detach` is one interactive `podman exec -it --detach-keys= -e TERM -e COLORTERM -e TERMINFO_DIRS=…` of `box session start`, which creates the window and then execs the tmux client; with stdin not a terminal it starts detached and says so. The client's grouped session is targeted as `=devc-<hex>:` (with the colon; `set-option` resolves a bare name as a pane or window first). `devc attach` refuses without a terminal; with one argument it is a project if it names one, else a session prefix searched in every project; prefixes may omit the `s`; "most recent" is the highest session ID.
4. **Reasons devc writes for `session.end`:** `exited` (+ status), `killed` (+ signal), `stopped` (`devc stop`), `lost` (reconciliation), `box-down`. Ingestion drops every event of a session after its first end (counted, never journaled), which also absorbs the second end when both the harness and the tmux hook write one; that is why `session ended` writes an end even when the spool file is gone.
5. **Reconciliation race.** Refresh ingests, records the sessions open at that point, asks the box, ingests again, and ends only those recorded sessions that the box does not report; a session that starts or ends meanwhile is never marked lost by mistake. Dead panes do not count as alive. A box report that fails strict parsing changes nothing (a warning). A box that is not running ends all open sessions as `lost`; a running box that is not ready is only ingested.
6. **`devc down`/`rm`.** Before the stop: ingest and reconcile (as ordered). After it: ingest again and end every open session with `box-down`, then commit the realm's context. A `down`/`rm` of a box that was not running ends open sessions as `lost` and commits nothing (so no-op downs start no container).
7. **Ingestion bounds and locking.** Per run and file at most 8 MiB and 20,000 events; at most 10,000 spool entries (more fails the run for that box). The offsets file keeps `{offset, discard}` per file; `discard` skips the rest of an oversized line across runs. A separate `local/boxes/<r>/<p>/ingest.lock` serialises ingestion (the box lock can be held for minutes by `up`). Projects with neither journals nor a runtime directory are not touched (`ls` creates no state).
8. **Listing times** are the host's ingestion times (`at`), which a box cannot choose: AGE is since the session's start, the 24-hour window counts from its end. Old ended journals are skipped by mtime plus a last-line check, so `ps` does not parse every journal ever written.
9. **Context repository.** Bare, so git never treats the box-written tree as a work tree. The worker scans with safefs (no symlink followed anywhere), builds the commit with `git fast-import` (`deleteall` plus one `M` line per accepted file, C-quoted paths), and moves `main` only when the tree changed; `diff` compares `main` with such a snapshot (tree to tree, `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`), `log` is `--stat`. Left out and reported: entries that are git metadata on any filesystem (`.git` in any case, NTFS trailing dots/spaces, `git~1`, HFS-ignored characters) — only the entry, so a planted `.git` cannot hide its siblings; symlinks; special files; unreadable directories; files over 10 MiB. Over 50,000 files or 512 MiB the snapshot is refused rather than committed in part (a partial tree would record deletions). The container runs as its root, which is the host user, without capabilities, so files of subordinate UIDs are unreadable and skipped. Fixed identity `devc <devc@localhost>`.
10. **Terminal safety beyond the work order.** Error messages printed by the CLI pass through `PrintableText` (`execx.ExitError` embeds the first stderr line of a command, which the box controls for in-box execs); JSON output escapes DEL and C1 controls, which `encoding/json` leaves raw.
11. **Harness commands** are registered before flag parsing, so `--config-dir` is pre-scanned from the raw arguments; nothing is registered in a box (`DEVC_BOX=1`, which also keeps devc from reading a box home's `~/.config/devc`), and a manifest `bin` that equals a devc command is skipped.
12. **`devc stop`** of a session started from a shell sends SIGTERM to its processes (there is no window to kill).
13. **Stale boxes.** A box started before C04 has no `tmux.conf`; `box session start` tells the user to restart it. A harness installed after its box started has no launch description in it; the host says so before starting.
14. **Dependency:** `github.com/creack/pty` v1.1.24 (approved list), used by the integration tests only.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; with a configuration whose project lists `fake` (or `claude`): `bin/devc toolbox build tmux`, `bin/devc harness install fake`, `bin/devc fake <ref>` (attaches; detach with C-b d), `bin/devc fake <ref> --detach`, `bin/devc ps`, `bin/devc ls`, `bin/devc attach <ref> [session]` from another terminal or `ssh -t <host> devc attach <ref>`, `bin/devc stop <session>`, `bin/devc context diff|commit|log <realm>`, `bin/devc down <ref>`. Tests: `./dev test`; `./dev itest -run 'TestSessions|TestContextRepository' -v`.

**Notes for later chunks.**

- C05: `box session start` uses `/w/<r>/<p>/<worktree>` when it exists; `-w` does not create worktrees. The spec's periodic context commits are not scheduled yet: the banking timer should call `ctxrepo.Repo.Commit` too.
- C08: continuous ingestion should use `session.Store` (`Ingest`, `Refresh`) under the same ingestion lock; the box channel can deliver `box session ps` data and events directly. The journal is the source of truth; `seq` is per session.
- C09: the PTY wrapper goes into the window command built in `boxside.StartSession`; OSC 7501 state can be written with `AppendEvents` as `state` events.

### C05 Git

**What exists.**

- **`internal/gitx`** (new). `Host` is the only way devc runs host git: every command is `git --git-dir=<relay> -c core.hooksPath=<state>/local/git/no-hooks -c core.fsmonitor=false <sub> …` from `/` (the hooks directory is created on first use and must be empty). Three modes: *Internal* (all `GIT_*` removed, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, no prompts, `LC_ALL=C`), *Upstream* (only repository-locating variables removed, the user's global and system configuration kept for credentials; attached to the terminal when stdin is one, else `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`), *Display* (isolated like Internal, output captured and sanitised). `log`, `diff` and `show` always get `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`. Box transport: `ext::<engine> exec -i -u 1000:1000 <box> %S /w/<r>/<p>/.repo` with `-c protocol.allow=never -c protocol.ext.allow=always` on that invocation only. `Relay` (create via a temporary directory and rename, configuration, upstream remote `github` with `tagOpt = --tags`, ref listing, `update-ref --stdin` transactions with compare-and-swap, HEAD). `PlanBank` is the pure bank policy; `Service` has `InitRelay`, `InitBox`, `Adopt`, `Bank`, `Pull`, `Push`, `Log`, `Diff`, `WorktreeNew/List/Remove`, `MainBranch`, `DefaultBranch`.
- **In the box** (`internal/boxside`). `devc box snapshot --all|--worktree n` (JSON report, `refs/devc/wip/<worktree>`), `devc box worktree add|rm|list`, `devc box worktree-hook create|remove`, `devc box repo-state`, and the stub remote helper `git-remote-devc` (`cmd/devc` dispatches it to `boxside.RemoteHelper`; the toolbox has `bin/git-remote-devc -> ../devc/current/devc`). `box.json` gained `branch_prefix` and `data_root` for the worktree hook.
- **Commands.** `devc realm add <name> [--network level] [--description d]`; `devc project add <realm>/<name> [--from url] [--env e|--image i] [--harness h]… [--default-branch b]`; `devc project init [ref]`; `devc project adopt <realm>/<name> --path <clone> [--from url] …`; `devc bank [ref] [--all] [--quiet] [--json]`; `devc pull [ref]`; `devc push [ref] [branch…] [--force] [--tag t]… [--yes]`; `devc log|diff [ref] [-- git-args]`; `devc wt new <name> [ref] [--from start]`, `devc wt ls [ref] [--json]`, `devc wt rm <name> [ref] [--force]`; `devc service install|uninstall|status` (`internal/service`: `devc-bank.service` runs `bank --all --quiet` (failure ignored) then `context commit --all`; `devc-bank.timer` every 5 minutes); `devc context commit --all`. `devc down`/`rm` bank before stopping (a `box.StopHook`); `devc up` refuses a project with an upstream and no relay; `devc <harness> -w <name>` creates the worktree first.
- **Claude baseline.** `WorktreeCreate`/`WorktreeRemove` hooks call `devc box worktree-hook create|remove` (unverified, see LEFTOVER).
- **Tests.** Unit: host argument construction for every mode, ext URL escaping, bank policy (names, protected, non-fast-forward, kept deletions, snapshots, tags, stamps, limits), ref sorting, sanitising, porcelain and symref parsing, `names.CheckRef`; an end-to-end run with real git where a fake engine plays the box (`internal/gitx/gitx_test.go`: init, bank, rewrite, protected and hostile names, snapshot, forward, push with and without `--force`, pull, log, diff) that records every host git command and asserts `--git-dir=<relay>`, the hooks path, `/` as working directory, no box path in any argument and ext only with the box transport; remote-helper protocol exchange; snapshots (tracked, staged, untracked, 10 MiB, ignored, symlink, nested repository, unborn branch, reuse, clean); worktrees and the hook; systemd unit text and install logic against a temporary directory; `realm add`/`project add` refusals; `execx` `Unset`. Integration (`test/integration/git_test.go`): `TestGit` (items 1–12), `TestGitAdopt` (13), `TestBankAll` (14, plus banking at `down`, an empty project and `up` without a relay).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Remote helper and deletions.** The stub reports `ok` for every branch update but answers a deletion (`push :ref`) with `error`, because the relay never deletes a branch; reporting success would make the box drop its tracking ref until the next bank.
2. **Ref names.** devc's limits on top of git's: printable ASCII only, at most 200 bytes, not `HEAD`. Unicode branch names from a box are therefore skipped and reported. `git check-ref-format --branch` runs for every changed branch (in addition to the Go check). A bank applies at most 1000 changed branches and 1000 new tags; the rest wait for the next bank.
3. **Bank details.** A new bank ref is recorded when the tip differs from the branch's newest bank ref (so A→B→A records A again). Timestamps are host seconds; a collision takes the newest existing stamp plus one, so refs never clash and sort in order. Each branch update (head plus bank ref) is its own transaction with the old value as a check, so one refused update (a directory/file conflict such as `x` kept in the relay and `x/y` in the box) does not stop the others. Snapshots count as "differing" when tree or parents differ; the box also reuses its own `refs/devc/wip/<worktree>` commit when nothing changed, and deletes it when the worktree is clean. Tags equal to the relay's `refs/tags` (handed in from the upstream) are not recorded as box tags. Untracked files are skipped when over 10 MiB, nested repositories and special files are skipped too; all are listed in the commit message and the summary (not with `--quiet`). `--quiet` prints refused refs, failures and forward failures. `devc bank <ref>` on a stopped box prints a note and exits 0; `bank --all` waits at most a minute for a busy box and exits 1 only when a bank failed.
4. **Pull.** Relay branches ahead of the upstream are left alone silently; only true divergence is reported. Deleted upstream branches are pruned from `refs/remotes/github/*`, tags are not. On a stopped box the relay is updated and a note says the box gets the refs later. Tags are pushed into the box without force; a box tag of the same name is kept and reported.
5. **Push.** The first argument is the project when it names a configured project, otherwise all arguments are branches. Without branches and tags it pushes the branch checked out in `main` (the relay's HEAD when the box is not running). It pushes the tip object that was previewed (not the ref name), with `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<last fetched tip>` whenever `--force` is given. Without a terminal it needs `--yes`. `--tag` alone pushes only the tag.
6. **Matching repository.** `project init` treats an existing box repository as matching when `.repo` is a repository whose `remote.origin.url` is `devc::<realm>/<project>`; anything else (another origin, an invalid `.repo`, a `main/.git` without `.repo`) is refused. On a match it re-applies the box configuration, reflects all refs and checks out the default branch only if nothing is checked out. Checkout uses `git checkout -b <b> --track origin/<b>`, which refuses to overwrite untracked files rather than resetting.
7. **Adopt.** The upstream defaults to the clone's `remote.origin.url`, read with `git config --file <clone>/.git/config` (one file, no includes, nothing runs); a URL devc would refuse is dropped with a warning. Local branches enter the relay with bank refs, `origin/*` (except `origin/HEAD`) become `refs/remotes/github/*`, tags `refs/tags/*`; then relay branches are created from the upstream refs as in `init`. Default branch: `default_branch`, else the clone's `origin/HEAD` target, else its checked-out branch, else `main` (no network needed). Clones inside the projects root or the state directory are refused (they are box-writable).
8. **`-w` without a relay.** `devc <harness> -w <name>` creates the worktree only for projects that have a relay; projects never initialised keep C04's behaviour (the session starts in the project directory). C04's session tests rely on that.
9. **Realms.** `realm add` writes the network level only when `--network` is given; the default (`internet`) is refused until C07, so new realms for now need `--network open` or `none`.
10. **Log and diff output** is captured, control characters other than git's own colour sequences are replaced (commit messages and file contents are box-written), and the result is paged through `$GIT_PAGER`, `$PAGER` or `less` (with `LESS=FRX` unless set) when stdout is a terminal. `--ext-diff` and `--textconv` are refused in user arguments. Log and diff take no lock (read-only).
11. **Service.** The units carry the current `PATH` and, when set by flag or environment, `DEVC_CONFIG_DIR`, `DEVC_STATE_DIR` and `DEVC_RUNTIME_DIR`; `DEVC_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` overrides the unit directory (for tests). `context commit --all` (new flag) commits every realm that has a context directory and prints only commits and problems.
12. **Extra in-box commands** beyond plan 3.3's table: `box worktree …`, `box worktree-hook …`, `box repo-state`. `execx.Cmd` gained `Unset` (variables removed from the inherited environment).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; with a configuration (realm with `network.default = "open"`): `bin/devc project add <realm>/<name> --from <url>` (or `project adopt … --path <clone>`), work in the box (`bin/devc sh <ref>`), `bin/devc bank <ref>`, `bin/devc log <ref>`, `bin/devc diff <ref>`, `bin/devc pull <ref>`, `bin/devc push <ref>`, `bin/devc wt new|ls|rm …`, `bin/devc fake <ref> -w t1`, `bin/devc service install`. Inspect a relay with `git --git-dir=<state>/durable/realms/<realm>/relay/<project>.git for-each-ref`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/gitx/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/service/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestGit|TestBankAll' -v`.

**Notes for later chunks.**

- C07: the example realm must switch back to `internet` (C03r); `realm add` without `--network` then gives working realms. The user documentation should explain banking, `refs/devc/*`, `devc push` and the stub remote helper.
- C06: `wt new` should add the `data` symlink to new worktrees (plan 3.8); `boxside.CheckWorktreeName` already refuses the data root as a worktree name.
- C08: the daemon can run `Service.Bank` after turns (under the box lock, as the CLI does) and replace the timer.
- C10: the real `git-remote-devc` replaces `boxside.RemoteHelper`; the bank policy (`PlanBank`) and the relay's update path stay the same.

### C06 Data

**What exists.**

- **Ownership model** (work order section 1), applied by the setup worker at every `devc up` that creates or starts the box, and before every mutating data command: project directory `2000:1000 1770`, realm directory above it `0700` (host, for every project), `.devc/{savepoints,trash}` `2000:2000 0700`, data root and intermediate directories of multi-component zone paths `2000:1000 1770`, input zones `1000:1000 0755`, work zones `2000:1000 0775` (deviation 1), sealed zones `2000:1000 1770`; sealed entries `2000:2000`, directories `0555`, files without write bits and readable by all. Projects without `[data]` are untouched (no worker, no `.devc`).
- **Helper workers** (`internal/boxside`: `datalayout.go`, `dataproto.go`, `dataworker.go`). `devc box data-worker` reads one JSON `DataRequest` on stdin and prints one `DataResult`; operations `setup`, `seal`, `unseal`, `rm`, `trash-restore`, `trash-empty`, `ls`, `reflink-save`, `savepoint-drop`, `restore`. They run as `podman run --rm --network none --userns=keep-id:uid=1000,gid=1000 --cap-drop=ALL --cap-add … --security-opt no-new-privileges --security-opt seccomp=<box profile> [label=…] --user 0:0 … --rootfs <toolbox>/helper-rootfs:O /bin/devc box data-worker` with only the project directory at `/target` (plus, for restic restores, the staging directory read-only at `/staging`). Capabilities: `CHOWN FOWNER DAC_OVERRIDE` for ownership work, `DAC_READ_SEARCH` only for `ls`, `DAC_OVERRIDE FOWNER` for deletions. `internal/safefs/dir.go` (`safefs.Dir`) is the write side: every operation takes one path component relative to a directory descriptor, never follows a symlink (`openat O_NOFOLLOW`, `fchownat AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW`, `fchmod` on descriptors, `renameat2 RENAME_NOREPLACE`, `mkdirat`, `unlinkat`, descriptor-based `RemoveAll`). Sealing hands each directory to the vault and makes it read-only *before* listing it, so the box cannot add or swap entries in a directory being walked; afterwards the worker checks that the entry's name still refers to the sealed inode. Symlinks are re-owned and copied as links; FIFOs, sockets and devices are skipped and reported.
- **Host side** (`internal/data`, new). `Service` (setup, seal, unseal, rm, trash restore/empty, scan/ls, links, save, restore, gc, before-session), `Normalize` (paths relative to the data root, or absolute in-box or host paths inside it; `..` refused anywhere), `DecodeResult` (strict, 8 MiB cap, every path/ID/count validated, text made printable), the savepoint index `durable/realms/<realm>/savepoints/<project>.jsonl` (`save` and `drop` records: id, name, time, driver, paths, exclude, files, size, relay commit, restic snapshot), `Retention`, `NewID`. `BoxHooks` is a `box.Contributor` (one read-only nested mount per input zone, part of the hash), a `box.Preparer` (setup worker before start) and a new `box.StartHook` (`Manager.StartHooks`, run once a box this command started is ready: data links).
- **Savepoints.** IDs `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ[-name]` (a taken ID moves on by a second). `reflink`: probe clone in `.devc/savepoints/`, copy of the data root minus input zones and sealed entries into `<id>.partial/` with FICLONE, read-only and vault-owned, renamed to `<id>/`; "not supported" removes the partial copy. `restic`: pinned 0.19.1 (`devc toolbox install restic`, SHA-256 of the release `.bz2` pinned for amd64 and arm64, cached by checksum in `${DEVC_DOWNLOAD_CACHE:-~/.cache/devc/downloads}`, installed as `toolbox/restic/<version>/restic` + `current`, checked by `restic version` in the helper container); repository `local/savepoints/<realm>/<project>/restic` (`--insecure-no-password`), cache `cache/restic/<realm>/<project>`; restic runs in the helper container as `1000:1000` without capabilities or network, toolbox read-only at `/opt/devc`, tmpfs `/tmp`, tagged with the savepoint ID, `--host devc`, excludes escaped for restic's glob syntax. `auto` = reflink if the probe succeeds, else restic if installed, else an error saying what to do. `none` refuses, `borg` refuses (LEFTOVER). Retention after every save (`keep`, unnamed only; restic `forget` by snapshot ID); `devc data gc [--drop id]…` applies retention, drops, and prunes restic.
- **Restore.** Copy mode: `restored-<id>[-N]/` in the first work zone, keeping paths relative to the data root, owned by the box user, nothing overwritten. `--in-place`: a dry pass first (refusals before any change), then each entry goes back to its place; what is in the way is moved into one trash entry (`<stamp>-restore-<id>`); entries already identical (same type, symlink target, or mode and bytes — not times, which the box controls) are left alone; a full restore skips entries sealed since the savepoint (reported), an explicit path into a sealed entry or an input zone is refused. restic restores into `local/tmp/restore-*` first; the worker copies from there; the staging tree is emptied by `rm-worker` in the helper container and removed.
- **Trash.** `.devc/trash/<stamp>-<label>[-N]/meta.json` (id, time, reason `rm`/`restore`, paths) and `payload/<path>`; entries are moved by rename and keep their owners (a sealed entry comes back sealed). Restore checks every path first and refuses occupied places; missing parents are recreated for the box user (never layout directories). `trash empty [--older-than 30d] [--yes]` asks on a terminal and refuses without one unless `--yes`.
- **Data links.** `devc box data-links` (in the box, as dev) gives every worktree a relative `data -> ../<root>` link unless the index tracks `data` or something occupies it, and adds `/data` to `.repo/info/exclude`. Run after every start through devc (StartHook), after `project init`, and by `box worktree add` / the worktree hook for new worktrees (`box.json` gained `data_links`).
- **Commands.** `devc seal <path> [ref]`, `devc save [name] [ref]` (one argument containing `/` is the project), `devc restore <savepoint> [path…] [--in-place] [-p ref]` (savepoint by ID or unique name), `devc data ls [ref] [--json]`, `devc data rm|unseal <path> [ref]`, `devc data gc [ref] [--drop id]…`, `devc data trash ls|restore <id>|empty`, `devc toolbox install restic`; `toolbox ls` shows restic. Without a ref, an absolute path argument names the project, else the current directory. Every mutating data command holds the box lock and refuses while the project's configuration has problems.
- **Automation.** `before_session = true`: `devc <harness>` takes an unnamed savepoint first unless one is younger than 30 minutes; a failure stops the session (fail closed).
- **Sandbox description.** The data paragraph names the data root and the links, read-only inputs, where results go, one run directory per run in sealed zones, `devc seal <path>`, and that sealed directories cannot be changed.
- **Tests.** Unit: layout validation and classification, seal targets, IDs, trash labels (`boxside`); every worker operation against a real temporary tree as namespace root (setup owners and modes and refusals, sealing a hostile tree, unseal, records, trash round trip, ls, reflink save and restore with a substitute clone, unsupported clone, staging restore, in-place restore with displacement, sealed and input refusals); data links with real git; `safefs.Dir`; host service end to end with a fake engine that runs the real worker in-process and a fake restic (helper arguments, capabilities, restic container, excludes, retention, staging cleanup, gc, auto fallback, before-session, box hooks for rw mounts); index, retention, IDs, trash age, `DecodeResult`; restic installer with a fake download and the cache; `--userns` rendering; CLI usage errors. Integration (`test/integration/data_test.go`): `TestData` (items 1–7, 9–12; item 8 skips on ext4) and `TestDataNone` (item 13). About 2 minutes.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Work zones are `2000:1000 0775`, not `1000:1000 0755`.** A box-owned directory inside the sticky, vault-owned data root can still be renamed or removed by its owner, which contradicts "zone directories cannot be renamed or removed by the box" (section 1) and test item 1. Vault-owned with group write keeps the directory itself immovable while everything inside stays ordinary for the box and the host user. Intermediate directories of zone paths (`a` in `a/b`) are `2000:1000 1770` like the data root, so a zone cannot be moved by moving its parent.
2. **restic sees the whole project directory read-only at `/project`, not just the data root.** Podman resolves bind sources on the host and follows symlinks; the project directory's path is host-made, so nothing a box planted can change what is mounted (`.devc` is 0700 vault and unreadable to restic's user). Before every restic save the setup worker re-checks the layout and lists the excludes.
3. **Every mutating data command runs the setup worker's layout step first** (inside the same worker), so seal, rm, save and restore also work on a project whose box has not been started since C06, and always on a checked layout. A `.devc` that the box created (owner 1000) is refused; one left by a crashed worker (owner 0) is adopted.
4. **rw-mounted data projects.** A box that mounts another data project read-write (`[[mounts]] mode = "rw"`) gets that project's input zones as read-only nested mounts too, and its `up` runs that project's setup. This closes the C02 note: no running box can then write another project's inputs or swap its zone directories while that project's box starts.
5. **Seal records** go to `.devc/seals.jsonl` with `op` = `seal` or `unseal` (unseal records are an addition); `data ls` takes sealed entries from the tree (vault-owned children of sealed zones) and time/size/commit from the last seal record. Sealing an entry that is sealed already re-walks it (completing an interrupted seal) but writes no record and reports "sealed already". A regular file directly in a sealed zone can be sealed too.
6. **Restore granularity** is per entry (see above), with identical entries left in place; the work order's "anything in the way" is everything that differs.
7. **Savepoint paths** are recorded as `paths: ["."]` plus `exclude` (input zones and the sealed entries at save time); a restore of an excluded path is refused with that reason.
8. **The realm directory is made `0700` for every project**, data or not (it is shared by the realm's projects).
9. **Before-session savepoints fail the session** when they cannot be taken (README rule 6); turn `before_session` off to start anyway.
10. **In-box command names.** Plan 3.3 lists `box seal-worker` and `box copy-worker`; there is one `devc box data-worker` taking a JSON request with an operation, plus `devc box data-links` (run in the box itself).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; a project with `[data]` (for example the example `analysis`), `bin/devc up <ref>`, `bin/devc toolbox install restic`, in the box create `data/results/run1/…`, `bin/devc seal results/run1 <ref>`, `bin/devc data ls <ref>`, `bin/devc save before <ref>`, change files, `bin/devc restore <id> -p <ref>` or `--in-place`, `bin/devc data rm …`, `bin/devc data trash ls|restore|empty`, `bin/devc data unseal …`, `bin/devc data gc <ref>`. Inspect `.devc` with `podman unshare ls -la <project>/.devc`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/data/ ./internal/safefs/ ./internal/toolbox/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestData' -v`.

**Notes for later chunks.**

- C07: the escape suite should include sealed data (item list of plan 3.13): from the box, try `rm`, `mv`, `chmod`, truncation, `ln` over sealed entries and the zone directories, listing `.devc`, and writing an input zone. User documentation: sealing limits (LEFTOVER), trash, savepoint drivers, `toolbox install restic`.
- C08: the daemon could run `data gc` (restic prune) on the banking timer; requests to seal (phase 2) call `data.Service.Seal` under the box lock — never unseal, rm, trash or restore.
- C14 (sync): moves from staging into the project should use the same worker pattern (`restore` from `/staging` already does the copy part).

### C07 Network and finish

**What exists.**

- **`internal/netpolicy`** (rewritten). `BoxArgs`: `none` → `--network none`; `open` → engine default; `internet` → engine default plus `--sysctl net.ipv6.conf.{all,default}.disable_ipv6=1` and `Decision.Rules`; `allowlist` → refused ("not available yet"). `Rules(resolvers, hostAddrs)` renders the nft script (one `inet` table `devc`, set `allow4` of `ipv4_addr . inet_service` with `flags timeout`, output chain with default accept: `oifname "lo"` accept, IPv6 dropped, `@allow4` accept, UDP/TCP 53 to the resolvers, `192.0.2.1` TCP 9 `reject with tcp reset`, the nine ranges `reject with icmp type admin-prohibited`, then the host's own non-private IPv4 addresses rejected the same way; it starts with `table inet devc` + `delete table inet devc` so a second application replaces the first). `Gather` reads the namespace path (`NetworkSettings.SandboxKey`) and, through `ReadEngineFile` (absolute path, `O_NOFOLLOW`, regular file, 64 KiB cap), the box's `ResolvConfPath` and `HostsPath`, parsed strictly (`ParseResolvConf`: IPv4 nameservers, at most 8; `ParseHosts`: the engine's host aliases). `Installer.Install` runs `podman unshare -- <nsenter> --net=<ns> <nft> -f -` with the rules on stdin (absolute tool paths from `execx.LookPath`, which also searches the sbin directories) and `Verify` lists the table and checks set, chain and sentinel. Canary: `OpenCanary` (listener on `0.0.0.0:0` counting accepts), `RunCanary`/`CheckSentinel`/`Classify` (shared with the box side), `CanaryAddrs`, `EvaluateCanary` (fails on any accepted connection, a `connected` probe, an unparsable report or a missing destination). Forwarding: `Forwarder` (accept loop; per connection `nsenter -t <pid> -U -n --preserve-credentials <devc> box dial 127.0.0.1:<port>` with the connection's socket as stdin and stdout, `DEVC_BOX=1`, from `/`; polls the box every 2 s and ends with `ErrBoxGone` when it stops or its PID changes), `Listen` (same port, else a free one unless `--host-port` was explicit), `Registry` of detached forwards in `<runtime>/forwards/<id>.json` (0600, strict decoding, validated; stale records and their logs are removed when listed; `ProcessAlive` requires `forward serve --id <id>` in the process's command line).
- **Start sequence** (`internal/box`). `Spec.NetRules`; `startBox` = `init` → inspect → `Gather` → `Install` (+ verify) → `start` for level internet, plain `start` otherwise. Used for a new box (failure removes the container), a stopped box (failure stops it again) and a running box that is not ready (its rules are verified from the host; without them it is stopped and started through the full sequence). After the readiness marker appears, `checkNetwork` runs the canary: the host's IPv4 addresses (`netpolicy.HostAddrs`) and the host alias from the engine's hosts file (read before start) are passed to `devc box net-canary --port <listener port>`; a failure stops/removes the box like any failed start. `Manager.Net` and `Manager.HostAddrs` are injectable for tests.
- **In the box** (`internal/boxside`). `box init` removes a stale readiness marker first and then, for level internet (or when `box.json` cannot be read), requires the sentinel to answer "connection refused" within 5 s before anything else; otherwise it exits non-zero and the box stops. `devc box net-canary` (JSON probes, exit 1 when anything connected), `devc box dial <ip:port>` (no box check: it runs in the host's mount namespace; relays with half-closes, `shutdown(SHUT_WR)` on a socket stdout), `devc box statx-worker` (helper container only). Self-test item `nftables`: a netfilter netlink "list tables" dump and a "create table" request must both be answered with EPERM.
- **Commands.** `devc forward <port> [ref] [--host-port N] [--bind ADDR] [--detach]`, `devc forward ls [--json]`, `devc forward stop <id>`, hidden `devc forward serve`; `devc init <git-url> | --example`; `devc doctor --fix`; `devc up --all`, `devc down --all`; `devc cursor [ref]`, `devc code [ref]`.
- **Doctor.** Check `overlay-statx` (after `storage`): creates `devc-doctor-statx-<random>` from the helper rootfs (`--rootfs …:O`, network none, no capabilities, aux security options, tmpfs `/target`, labels `devc.role=aux`, `devc.aux=doctor`), starts it, polls for 10 s; on a timeout it kills the container's own fuse-overlayfs process (owned by the user, command line naming `/<container id>/` or `upperdir=<UpperDir>`) and removes the container; skip without a synced toolbox. Checks carry `RootFix` lines; `doctor.RootScript` turns them into one `sh` script (linger, subordinate IDs, SELinux rule and `restorecon`, AppArmor profile, cgroup delegation). `--fix` creates the state, runtime and projects directories and syncs the toolbox first. On this host the check reports the hang in about 13 s and leaves nothing behind.
- **Engine.** `ContainerInfo` gained `NetworkSettings.SandboxKey`, `ResolvConfPath`, `HostsPath`, `GraphDriver`; `Unshare(ctx, args, stdin)`; `RunOptions.CreateArgs(name)`; the fake engine creates a namespace path and the engine files at `Init`, records unshare input, parses `--sysctl`/`--rootfs`/`--network`, and has `OnStart`.
- **Configuration.** The example realm `demo` uses the default level again (`internet`, written explicitly with a comment); `other/tools` overrides the realm's `none` with `internet`. `realm add` without `--network` gives a working realm. The sandbox description states the level in force (internet mentions DNS, IPv6 off and `devc forward`).
- **Repository.** v1 moved with `git mv` to `legacy/` (`devc/`, `podman-dev-images/`, `specs.md`, `podman_cheatsheet.md`; staged, not committed). Example envs `r-base` (rocker/r-ver with the system libraries, LaTeX, Miniforge, HyperQueue, git-delta and `rcmdcheck`) and `python-base` (Ubuntu 24.04 tools and uv), not built by tests. `examples/config/dotfiles/` with v1's `.zshrc`, `.gitignore_global`, `.gitconfig` without the credential helpers, `.tmux.conf` without the terminal type and clipboard lines devc sets itself. Package `examples` embeds `examples/config` (`all:`) for `init --example`.
- **Documentation.** `README.md` (what devc is, quick start, document map), `docs/guide.md` (concepts, configuration, boxes, sessions, git, data, network and forwarding, logins and secrets, notes, editors, what is and is not protected), `docs/host-setup.md` (requirements, root commands, known problems).
