- **Tests.** Unit: rules text and order, private ranges, resolv.conf and hosts parsing, engine-file reading, install/verify with a fake engine, gather, sentinel and canary evaluation, the canary listener, registry bookkeeping (save, list, stale records, foreign records, removal by PID), `Listen` fallback, the forwarder's accept loop with a socket-passing fake runner (dial arguments, socket as stdin/stdout, end on PID change); box: the internet start sequence (create args, rules on stdin, canary), canary failure, nft failure, init failure, box started outside devc, allowlist, none; boxside: init network check and stale marker, check selection, canary output, dial relay; doctor: `overlay-statx` success, hang (timeout, fuse kill, removal), removal failure, worker failure, skip, probe arguments, `fuseServes`, root script; CLI: usage errors of the new commands, `init --example`, `init <url>`, editor URI and help. Integration (`test/integration/network_test.go`, `escape_test.go`): `TestNetworkInternet` (items 1, 3), `TestNetworkPlainStart` (2), `TestForward` (4, 5), `TestNetworkLevels` (6), `TestInitExample` (7), `TestDoctorStatx` (8), `TestEscape` (9, eight groups), `TestRecreateKeepsState` (phase 1 "done when").

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Restart without recreation.** `podman init` works on a stopped (exited) container and creates a fresh network namespace, so a stopped box of level internet is started with init → rules → start; it is not removed and recreated (the container layer, including `devc root` changes, survives). A failed restart stops the box again instead of removing it (rollback semantics of C03r).
2. **Host addresses in the rules.** Besides the nine ranges, the rules reject the host's own IPv4 addresses outside them (a server's public address would otherwise reach host services through the engine's NAT). The canary tries every host address (private or not) plus the host alias, not only private ones.
3. **Canary from both sides.** The host counts connections its listener accepts as well as reading the box's report, so a box cannot pass the check by lying.
4. **Resolvers.** All IPv4 nameservers of the box's resolv.conf are allowed on port 53. On this host that is `10.0.2.3` (slirp4netns) and `192.168.122.1`, the LAN router (see LEFTOVER).
5. **`up --all` starts existing boxes only** (running or stopped) and creates none; `down --all` stops every running box of the configuration. Both continue after a failure and exit 1 if any box failed.
6. **Forward details.** Box gone = not running or a different PID (a restart ends a forward). A detached forward is devc itself (`forward serve`), started directly with the listener as file descriptor 3 in its own session, logging to `<runtime>/forwards/<id>.log`; the parent opens the listener so the port and errors are reported synchronously. Forward IDs are `f` plus six hex digits. `forward` starts the box like `sh` does.
7. **Box init removes a stale readiness marker** (not in the work order): a plain `podman start` that fails left the marker of the previous run behind.
8. **Self-test item `nftables`** (not in the work order) gives the escape suite its "rules cannot be listed or changed" proof without `nft` in the image.
9. **Escape suite reuse.** The data layout and sealed-entry attempts of `TestData` moved into `layoutEscapes`/`sealedEscapes` (extended by overwrite, rename inside, replacing with a decoy and a symlink) and are called from both tests; the git host-side traps reuse `hostileRepository` from `TestGit`. A box of another realm runs during the suite; the second box of the same realm serves on all its addresses.
10. **Doctor statx probe** uses `create`/`start`/`inspect`/`rm` instead of `run --rm`, because a hung worker cannot be killed while its fuse request is pending; devc ends the container's own fuse-overlayfs process (it is the user's process; nothing else is touched).
11. **`init <url>`** validates the URL like a project upstream (no `ext::` or other transport helpers) and clones with `-c protocol.ext.allow=never`, the user's own git configuration and credentials, attached to the terminal.
12. **Example dotfiles** are now copied into every test box (they are part of `examples/config`); nothing in the suite depends on them.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; with a configuration whose realm uses the default level: `bin/devc up <ref>` (look for the rules with `podman unshare nsenter --net=$(podman inspect -f '{{.NetworkSettings.SandboxKey}}' <box>) nft list table inet devc`), `bin/devc run <ref> -- bash -c 'exec 3<>/dev/tcp/192.0.2.1/9'` (refused), `podman stop <box>; podman start <box>` (the box stops again; see `podman logs`), `bin/devc forward 8888 <ref> [--detach]`, `bin/devc forward ls|stop`, `bin/devc doctor [--fix]`, `bin/devc init --example --config-dir /tmp/x`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/netpolicy/ ./internal/box/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/doctor/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestNetwork|TestForward|TestInitExample|TestDoctorStatx|TestEscape|TestRecreateKeepsState' -v`.

**Notes for later chunks.**

- C13: the table `inet devc`, the set `allow4` (`ipv4_addr . inet_service`, `flags timeout`) and the chain `output` are stable; exceptions are set elements added with `podman unshare nsenter --net=<ns> nft add element inet devc allow4 '{ <ip> . <port> timeout <d> }'` (the namespace path comes from `Engine.Inspect`). The rules are rendered only by `netpolicy.Rules`.
- C08: the daemon could own detached forwards (the registry format is in `netpolicy.Record`) and serve `http://<port>.<project>.<realm>.localhost`.
- Phase 1 is complete as far as this host can show it; Review B should look at `internal/netpolicy`, the start sequence in `internal/box/lifecycle.go` and the escape suite.

### C07r Fixes after review B

All twelve findings and the unconfirmed B13 of `docs/dev/reviews/B-review.md` are resolved as the work order's decisions table directs; `docs/dev/reviews/B-response.md` has one paragraph per ID. What changed:

- **Network binding (B1).** `internal/netpolicy/binding.go`: `Binding` (network namespace inode, start time of the box's PID 1, boot ID) written by `startBox` to `<state>/…/etc/<box>/netns.json` (in the box `/etc/devc/netns.json`, read-only) after the rules are installed and verified and before `start`; every devc start removes the old record first. `devc box init` checks it (`netpolicy.CheckBinding`) before the sentinel. The namespace inode is read with `podman unshare stat -L -c %i -- <SandboxKey>` (the path the rules went into) and compared with `/proc/<pid>/ns/net` of the engine's main PID when the host can see it. `up` and `EnsureRunning` (so `sh`, `run`, `root`, sessions, forwards) verify a running box of level internet from the host (`Manager.verifyRunning`: record matches, `nft list table` succeeds) and stop and restart it otherwise. The fake engine's `init` now creates the main PID that `start` keeps, as runc does.
- **Git (B2, B4, B8).** Git mutations refuse while `ProblemsFor` reports anything (`withGit`, `push`, `wt new`; `bank --all` skips and exits 1; the bank hook at down/rm says nothing was banked and lets the box stop). `gitx.Protected` fails closed. `Bank` returns `*gitx.NotPreservedError` for preservation failures; `gitx.Preserved(err, worktree)`. `box.ErrKeepRunning` from a stop hook keeps `down`, `rm` and a recreation from stopping the box; `up --recreate`/`rebuild` on a running box now run the stop hooks (bank, sessions, context commit) like `rm`. New flags: `wt rm --discard`, `down|rm|rebuild|up --no-bank`. `refs/devc/boxtip/<branch>` records the box's tip; a bank moves a relay head only when the box moved the branch; `InitBox` records the tip of the branch it checks out.
- **Sessions (B3, B6, B7, B12).** `devc ingest [ref] [--all] [--quiet]`; `devc bank` and `bank --all` ingest first, relay or not; the timer unit runs `ingest --all --quiet`, then `bank --all --quiet`, then `context commit --all` (`devc service install` again to get the new unit). Journal appends are fsynced before offsets are saved, cut back on failure, and start on a new line after a torn one; journal events carry the host-only `spool` offset (proto.Event.Spool) and offsets the journal's `seq`, so a retry resumes exactly. `Store.Refresh` takes an `Observe` function called under the ingestion lock after ingesting. Session prefixes shown by `ps`, the chooser and `wt ls` are at least 12 characters and unique among the listed sessions.
- **Forwarding (B5).** `netpolicy.Pin` opens the box main process's user and network namespaces after checking PID and start time against the engine and `/proc`; `nsenter --user=/proc/self/fd/3 --net=/proc/self/fd/4` per connection (`execx.Cmd.ExtraFiles`); the forward ends when PID or start time change.
- **Savepoint modes (B9).** Reflink saves write a mode record `.devc/savepoints/.modes/<id>` (non-default modes only, vault-owned 0444); restores use it; drops remove it; restic staging restores keep restic's modes exactly.
- **Resolvers (B13).** `netpolicy.TrustedResolvers` (host `/etc/resolv.conf`, systemd-resolved's upstream file behind a loopback stub, the backend's built-in resolver from `HostConfig.NetworkMode`/network gateways); `Gather(ci, trusted)` keeps only trusted nameservers and reports the rest, which `up` warns about. `engine.NetworkSettings.Networks` (gateways) parsed; fake engine `DefaultNetwork`.
- **Tests.** Unit: `TestBinding`, `TestBind`, `TestPin`, `TestTrustedResolvers`, `TestGather`, `TestForwarder`, `TestNetworkCheckSelection`, `TestInternetLevel` (plain restart, ready but unfiltered, namespace mismatch, untrusted resolver), `TestStopRefused`, `TestPlanBankBoxTips`, `TestBankNotPreserved`, `TestEndToEnd` (bank after pull), `TestGitRefusesBrokenConfig`, `TestRefreshObservesUnderLock`, `TestIngestAppendFailure`, `TestShortIDs`, `TestDataReflinkModes`, `TestServiceText`. Integration: `TestNetworkPlainStart` (binding stops a plain start; flushed rules restart the box through `up` and `run`), new `TestNetworkResolvers`, `TestGit` (bank after pull keeps it; unbankable work blocks `wt rm --force` and `down`, `--discard`/`--no-bank`), `TestBankAll` (ingestion without a relay, `ingest`), `TestEscape` (valid hostile spool targets, every host command must succeed with its effect).

**Deviations from the work order.**

1. **The binding holds more than the namespace inode.** On this host the engine reuses network namespace inodes across restarts (the same number for four stop/start cycles in a row), so an inode-only record would accept a plain `podman stop; podman start`. The record also holds the start time of the box's PID 1 (field 22 of `/proc/1/stat`, the same from host and box, new for every start) and the boot ID.
2. **`--no-bank`** on `down`, `rm`, `rebuild` and `up` (not in the decision): without it a box whose work cannot be banked (a broken box repository, a full disk) could not be stopped through devc at all. `wt rm --discard` implies `--force` (it is the more destructive of the two).
3. **`EnsureRunning` verifies too**, not only `up`: `sh`, `run`, `root`, sessions and forwards on a running box of level internet would otherwise still trust the readiness marker.
4. **`up --recreate` and `rebuild` run the stop hooks** on a running box (they ran none before), so they bank and end sessions like `rm`; that is what "rebuild proceeds only if the work was preserved" requires.
5. **Restic restores keep exact modes** (the old code added owner write and owner rwx to every restored entry, like the reflink path did).

**Upgrade notes.** A running box of level internet started by C07 has no binding record: the first `devc up`, `sh`, `run` or session restarts it once. Journals written before C07r have no `spool` offsets; ingestion falls back to the offsets file for them. The timer unit needs `devc service install` again to gain the ingest step.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes, and `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 27 tests, with `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (it needs `DEVC_TEST_NETWORK_HARNESS=1`). No test containers or images were left behind.

**How to exercise.** `./dev test`; `./dev itest -run 'TestNetworkPlainStart|TestNetworkResolvers|TestForward|TestGit$|TestBankAll|TestEscape' -v`. By hand: `devc up <ref>`, then `podman unshare nsenter --net=$(podman inspect -f '{{.NetworkSettings.SandboxKey}}' <box>) nft delete table inet devc` and `devc up <ref>` (restarts it); `podman stop <box>; podman start <box>` (stops again; `podman logs` names the binding); `devc root <ref> -- chmod 0555 /tmp` and `devc wt rm <wt> <ref> --force` (refused) / `--discard`; `devc ingest --all`.

### C08 Daemon core

**What exists.**

- **`internal/proto`.** The event types of C04 were already here; added: the JSON-RPC 2.0 codec (`rpc.go`): `ReadLine` (refuses lines above 1 MiB, which ends the connection), `ParseMessage` (strict decoding, version, id of bounded length, request/notification/response classification), `Conn` (symmetric: a method table with per-connection values for the serving side, `Call` with timeouts and `Notify` for the calling side, notification handlers run in arrival order, requests served in order on a worker, panics answered as internal errors, results above the limit answered with `CodeTooLarge`, handler errors hidden from the peer unless `ExposeErrors`, write and idle deadlines, queued requests still answered after the peer half-closes), `DecodeParams`/`DecodeResult` (strict), error codes (the JSON-RPC ones plus host unavailable, not found, refused, too large, busy, version). `views.go`: `SessionView` (C04's summary plus `machine` and `box_state`, with `Apply` folding one event; `session.Summary` is now an alias), `BoxView`, `Cursor`, `Command` and `Receipt` (types and validation only), `DaemonInfo`, the parameters and results of every method, the notification types, `ChannelVersion = 1`.
- **Box agent** (`internal/boxside/agent.go`, `agentclient.go`). `devc box init` starts it in a goroutine after the readiness marker (and so after the network and binding checks of C07r); a panic in it is logged and never ends init. It logs to `/run/devc/agent.log` (1 MiB, then starts over; a planted symlink or FIFO makes it discard). It listens on `/run/devc/agent.sock` (re-created after a listener failure, with backoff) and accepts a connection there only when `SO_PEERCRED` reports PID 0 (a peer outside the box's PID namespace, the host); a new host connection replaces the old one. It runs a yamux server on it and serves `box.hello` (protocol, devc version, realm, project, box name from `box.json`) and `box.sessions` (`LiveSessions`, C04's `box session ps`). It listens on the abstract socket `@devc-agent` for in-box clients (at most 32 at once): it reads the first line (1 MiB, 10 s), opens a stream to the host and relays both ways, or, without a host, answers a request with `CodeHostUnavailable` at once and drops a notification. `SendHint` (200 ms budget, never fails visibly) is called by `AppendEvents` after every write to the box's own spool, so hooks, the launcher's `session.start` and the tmux end hook all hint. `devc box agent-status` pings the host through the agent (exit 1 when not connected or no agent).
- **Daemon** (`internal/daemon`). `devc daemon [--log-level]` runs in the foreground from `/`; one per runtime directory (`daemon.lock`, flock); API socket `<runtime>/daemon.sock` (0600, directory 0700; the path length is checked). Jobs: **box watch** every 3 s (`podman ps -a` with `devc.role=box`, only configured projects whose container carries the project's labels; configuration reloaded when a fingerprint of the TOML files and their directories changes, also before every API answer); per running box a **worker** with the channel and the ingester; **ingestion** with C04's `session.Store.Ingest` under the per-box ingestion lock shared with the CLI, polled every 2 s, every 250 ms for 5 s after events arrived, at once on `host.hint`, skipped when a cheap spool snapshot (names and sizes through safefs) did not change, repeated at once when a run hit C04's per-run limits (`IngestReport.More`, new); **reconciliation** every 30 s for projects with a running box or open sessions (C04's `Refresh`, with the box's live sessions asked over the channel when connected, `session.BoxState.Live`, new); **housekeeping** every 5 minutes: `devc bank --all --quiet` and `devc context commit --all` as child processes of the same binary with absolute directories; **the hub**, which follows the journals themselves (startup scan, then per project directory mtime plus the size of every open session's journal, every second and right after each ingestion), so events appended by anyone (daemon, CLI, `down`) reach subscribers, and keeps the derived view of every session (journals of sessions that ended before the window are read only when asked for).
- **Channel, host side.** `DialBoxSocket`: `open(<runtime>/<box-id>, O_PATH|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW)`, `openat(agent.sock, O_PATH|O_NOFOLLOW)`, `fstat` must say socket, connect to `/proc/self/fd/<n>`. `CheckPeer`: the listener's PID from `SO_PEERCRED` must be visible, its `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` must have a path component `libpod-<id>` or `libpod-<id>.scope` of the box's container ID (from `ps`; `libpod-conmon-<id>.scope` does not match), and when both can be read its PID namespace must equal that of the container's main process. Then a yamux client, a control stream, `box.hello` (10 s; reply decoded strictly and validated; another protocol version or another realm/project refuses the helper), streams from the box served with `host.ping` and `host.hint` (at most 32 at once, 10 minutes idle, errors never expose host details). Channel states `none`, `connecting`, `connected`, `no-agent`, `refused`, `error`, with a printable detail; retries back off from 1 s to 30 s (4 s while the socket is missing).
- **API.** `daemon.info`, `daemon.stop`, `boxes.list`, `sessions.list` (realm, project, live, since, `sync`, pages of at most 1000 sessions and 512 KiB with `after`), `sessions.get` (view and the last 50–1000 events), `events.read` (seq above `from`, at most 1000 events and 512 KiB per call), `events.subscribe` (cursor, realm, project, sessions, window in seconds, `from_now`). A subscription replays per session the journal's events after the cursor (all for unknown sessions active within the window, default 30 days), then `caught_up`, then live events and a `heartbeat` every 15 s; registration and the snapshot happen under the hub's lock and each subscriber sends every seq of a session at most once and in order, so live events the replay covered are dropped. A cursor beyond the journal's end, or for a session without a journal, gets `missed`. A subscriber more than 4096 batches behind is disconnected (it resumes from its cursor). At most 64 API connections.
- **CLI.** `devc ps` and `devc ls` ask the daemon when one answers on the socket and serves the same configuration and state directories (`sessions.list` with `sync`, so the daemon ingests and reconciles first, as the phase 1 path does); otherwise, or with `DEVC_NO_DAEMON=1`, silently the phase 1 path. `devc ps --watch` (needs the daemon): the list from the daemon kept current by a subscription, redrawn on every change and heartbeat, resubscribing with its cursor when the daemon restarts. `devc events <session> [--follow] [--json]` (by ID or prefix, ended sessions too; without the daemon from the journal; `--follow` needs the daemon and ends with the session's end); `devc events --follow` follows every session from now. `devc daemon status [--json]`, `devc daemon stop`. `devc service install` writes `devc.service` (`ExecStart=<devc> daemon`, `Restart=on-failure`, the current PATH and directories), removes the banking timer, enables and (re)starts it; `--timer-only` installs the timer of C05 instead and removes the daemon unit; `uninstall` removes either; `status` says which is installed.
- **Tests.** Unit: codec (`TestReadLine`, `TestParseMessage`, `TestCalls`, `TestMalformedInput`, `TestHalfClose`, `TestClosePending`, `TestExposeErrors`), `TestSessionViewApply`; daemon: `TestDialBoxSocket` (socket, symlink to a listening host socket never contacted, regular file, directory, FIFO, missing, symlinked directory, stale socket), `TestCheckPeer`, `TestSubscribeReplay` (unknown sessions within the window, cursor, old session named by the cursor, cursor beyond the end, missing journal, filters, short window, invalid parameters), `TestSubscribeLive` (other writers, double polls, new sessions, partial lines, views), `TestSubscribeRaceWithReplay`, `TestReadEvents`, `TestDaemon` (fake engine and an in-process agent: no agent, connected, `host.ping`, hint latency, sync with the window from `box.sessions`, `sessions.get`, `events.read`, warnings, housekeeping commands from `/`, the box stopping, `daemon.stop`), `TestHostileChannel` (garbage, oversized line, malformed JSON, another box, another protocol, an unknown field, a peer outside the container, a symlink); boxside: `TestAgentWithoutHost`, `TestHostPeer`, `TestAgentListenReplacesStaleEntries`, `TestCappedLog`, `TestSpoolHint`, `TestInitStartsAgentAfterReady`; CLI: `TestCommandsThroughDaemon`, `TestDaemonUsage`, `TestEventLine`; service: `TestDaemonText`, `TestInstallUninstall`. Integration (`test/integration/daemon_test.go`): `TestDaemon` (items 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7 plus `ps --watch` and `events --follow`), `TestDaemonHostileEndpoints` (5), `TestDaemonHousekeeping` (8), `TestServiceUnits`.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Socket name.** Plan 3.11 calls the box socket `/run/devc/helper.sock`; the work order's `/run/devc/agent.sock` is used (work order wins).
2. **API additions.** `daemon.stop` (for `devc daemon stop`); `sessions.list` returns an object (`sessions`, `more`, `warnings`) in pages, because one line holds at most 1 MiB, and takes `sync`; `events.subscribe` also takes `sessions` and `from_now`, and the daemon sends `caught_up` after the replay.
3. **`devc ps` through the daemon is as fresh as without it.** It asks with `sync`: the daemon ingests and reconciles the matching projects first (box state from the engine, live sessions over the channel when connected), so both paths give the same sessions (integration item 4 compares them). Invalid spool lines the daemon met on its own are handed to the next client that asks, which prints them as the phase 1 path does.
4. **`devc ls`** asks the daemon only for the session counts; the box rows (image, drift, start) are still computed by the CLI, because drift needs the box definition. `boxes.list` serves `devc daemon status` and later the hub.
5. **Housekeeping runs as child processes** (`devc bank --all --quiet`, `devc context commit --all`, the same binary, from `/`, with the daemon's directories made absolute): a crash or hang there cannot take the daemon down, and the CLI's locking and fail-closed rules apply unchanged. The timer's `ingest --all` step is not needed with continuous ingestion.
6. **Checks beyond the work order.** The agent takes a connection on `agent.sock` as the host's only when `SO_PEERCRED` reports PID 0 (outside the box's PID namespace), so a box process cannot stand in for the host towards in-box clients or knock the daemon off. The daemon additionally requires the peer to share the PID namespace of the container's main process when both namespaces are readable.
7. **Configuration reload** happens on a changed fingerprint before each box-watch pass (as ordered) and also before every `daemon.info`, `boxes.list` and `sessions.list`, so an answer never misses a project added in the last seconds.
8. **Test-only settings** are hidden flags of `devc daemon`: `--housekeeping-interval` and `--reconcile-interval`. `DEVC_NO_DAEMON=1` (not hidden in the guide) makes the CLI ignore a running daemon; the tests use it to compare both paths while the daemon runs.
9. **Hostile endpoint tests restart the daemon** after planting each endpoint: removing or replacing `agent.sock` does not close an established channel, so a fresh connection is what tests the checks. The misbehaving in-box helper is the fake harness's new `--hostile-helper garbage|oversized|malformed` mode, run as `dev` in the box.
10. **`devc events`** without a session needs `--follow` (it then follows every session from now); with a session it reads the journal directly when no daemon runs, so it works in phase 1 as well.
11. **Daemon mode for the phase 1 suites.** `DEVC_ITEST_DAEMON=1 ./dev itest` starts a daemon in every test environment (`itest.New`); `itest.NewNoDaemon` is for tests that run their own. In that mode `TestBankAll` skips its check that no journal exists before the first ingestion (a running daemon ingests by itself).
12. **Smaller additions.** `engine.Container.StartedAt`, `engine.Fake.Put`/`Drop` (concurrent use in tests), `execx.Fake.Reset`, `session.TailJournal`, `ReadEvents`, `FindJournal`, `JournalSessions`, `EndedBefore`, `Store.SpoolSnapshot`. Dependency: `github.com/hashicorp/yamux` v0.1.2 (approved list).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; with a configuration and a running box (`bin/devc up <ref>`, a box started before C08 needs a restart to get its agent): `bin/devc daemon --log-level debug` in one terminal; `bin/devc daemon status`, `bin/devc run <ref> -- /opt/devc/bin/devc box agent-status`, `bin/devc fake <ref> --detach`, `bin/devc ps --watch`, `bin/devc events <session> --follow`, `bin/devc ps` and `DEVC_NO_DAEMON=1 bin/devc ps`, `bin/devc daemon stop`. Service units against a scratch directory: `DEVC_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR=/tmp/u bin/devc service install [--timer-only]` (calls the real `systemctl --user`). Tests: `./dev test ./internal/proto/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/cli/ ./internal/service/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestDaemon|TestServiceUnits' -v`; the phase 1 suites with a daemon in every environment: `DEVC_ITEST_DAEMON=1 ./dev itest -v`.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes; `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean; the new packages also pass `go test -race` (run by hand in the toolchain image with cgo). `./dev itest -v` passes: 31 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before. `DEVC_ITEST_DAEMON=1 ./dev itest -v` (every environment with a daemon) passes the same 31; that run used the binary from before four small final changes (the daemon moving to `/`, absolute directories for housekeeping, the shorter retry while the agent socket is missing, the reload before answers), after which `TestSessions`, `TestBankAll`, `TestEscape` and `TestBoxLifecycle` were run again in daemon mode and pass. Measured here: a fake-harness event reaches a subscriber about 0.1 s after the hook wrote it. No test containers, images, daemons or directories were left behind.

### C09 Status and input

**What exists.**

- **Status wrapper** (`internal/ptywrap`, new; `devc box pty [--argv0 name] -- <exe> args…`, `internal/boxside/pty.go`). Allocates a pseudo-terminal (`/dev/ptmx` through `x/sys/unix`, the master kept non-blocking so read deadlines work), copies the outer terminal's settings and size to it, starts the harness as session leader with it as controlling terminal, puts the outer terminal into raw mode (restored on every return path, also after a panic), copies both directions, forwards SIGTERM/INT/HUP/QUIT/USR1/USR2, propagates SIGWINCH, keeps reading up to 2 s after the harness exited while something else holds its terminal, and exits with the harness's status or re-raises its signal (no core). The scanner finds `ESC ] 7501 ;` with `bytes.IndexByte` on the escape byte, holds back only a possible prefix (flushed after 30 ms without a next byte) or an unterminated report (≤ 4096 bytes; longer ones pass untouched), accepts BEL and `ESC \`, aborts on any other control character or escape. The query is answered (`…;?:states=…:kinds=…` with the query's terminator) before the rest of the chunk reaches the terminal. Reports are parsed per the specification (keys, value alphabet, last wins, base64 `msg`/`title` without control characters, limits, `id`), handed to a reporter goroutine (never blocks the output), and become `state` events with `source: "osc"`, `kind`, `message` (root record only, `clear` ignored, consecutive duplicates dropped). The wrapper sets `DEVC_STATUS=osc` and, at the first report, creates `/run/devc/status/<session>`; the hook receiver drops its `state` payloads only when both are present.
- **Launcher.** A description with `status.osc7501 = true` starts `/opt/devc/bin/devc box pty --argv0 <bin> -- <exe> args…` when standard input and output are terminals and `DEVC_NO_PTY` is not 1; otherwise the harness is executed directly as before.
- **Events and views** (`internal/proto`). `state` gained `source` (`hook`, `osc`) and `message`; hook-made states say `hook`. New host-only types `seen {turn}` and `answer {question, choice, text, via}`: `ParseSpoolLine` refuses them, the host writes them (`session.Store.AppendHostEvent`, after ingesting the spool, under the ingestion lock). `SessionView` gained `turn`, `done_turn`, `seen_turn`, `prompt_seq`, `state_seq`; `seen` applies after the end too; `NeedsYou` no longer counts a done turn that was marked seen. `CheckInputText`/`NormalizeInputText`, `ValidCommandID` (session-ID form or UUID), the named keys, receipts and every method's parameters.
- **Box side** (`internal/boxside/input.go`, `question.go`; `internal/tmux`). Agent methods `box.input.clients` (the session's window, its clients and their latest `client_activity`), `box.input.paste` (text to `load-buffer -b devc-in-<random> -` on stdin, `paste-buffer -p -r -d -b … -t %pane`, 150 ms, `send-keys` of the submit key) and `box.input.keys`; key names must match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9-]{0,31}$`. Question hooks: `devc box hook claude PreToolUse` (matcher `AskUserQuestion` in the managed settings, timeout `question_wait + 60`) and `devc box hook fake ask` record the question and `state blocked` (source hook, also under the wrapper); when no client showed the window in the last 60 s and `input.question_wait` > 0, they ask the daemon (`host.question`, sent before the question is recorded) and give up as soon as a client becomes active, the wait ends, or no daemon answers; an answer is printed in the harness's form (Claude: `permissionDecision: allow` with `updatedInput.answers`) followed by `state working`.
- **Daemon** (`internal/daemon/commands.go`, `receipts.go`, `adapter.go`). Methods `session.send` (`queue` default, `steer`), `session.answer`, `session.interrupt`, `session.keys`, `session.start` (also `args`), `session.stop`, `session.seen` (ID optional), `command.get {id, wait ≤ 600}`. Receipts in `local/receipts/<id>.json` (directory 0700, files 0600): created whole and exclusively by `link(2)` before anything happens; outcomes `accepted` → `delivering` (set before the first key) → `delivered`, `typed-not-submitted`, `unknown`, `refused`; the text is dropped once final; a repeated ID returns the stored receipt and does nothing. At start the daemon re-queues `accepted` commands in their order, ends `delivering` ones as `unknown` and removes receipts older than seven days (also at every housekeeping pass). Per session two FIFOs share one inject lock: "now" (steer, answer, interrupt, keys, stop) and "queued" (queue sends and a start's first prompt, each delivered when the session is idle, done or error with a state reported after the last prompt and no pending question; a session that never reported a state counts as at rest 60 s after it started). Input gate before every injection: wait while a client showing the window was active in the last 3 s, at most 60 s. Paste adapter: gate, paste + submit key, wait 5 s for a `prompt` event (hash of the text, or of it trimmed) after the seq seen before typing; otherwise one more submit key and 3 s; harness without hooks → `unknown`. Interrupt: `input.interrupt_keys`, confirmed by any state event within 5 s (else `unknown`). Answer: handed to a waiting hook (waiting up to 2 s for one while a question is pending), else `input.answer_keys` with `{n}` or the text pasted and submitted, confirmed by a state change; either way an `answer` event is recorded. `mod`, `app-server` and `extension` adapters report "not available" and the daemon uses paste, saying so in the receipt. `host.question` from a box: the session must be a live session of that box; one waiter per session, at most 30 minutes (box streams now idle out after 31 minutes).
- **Manifests.** `[input]` gained `interrupt_keys` (default `["Escape"]`), `answer_keys` (default `["{n}", "Enter"]`, must contain `{n}`), `question_wait` (seconds, default 600, 0–1800); key names are validated; the launch description carries `question_wait`. Built-in manifests spell the keys out; Claude's baseline has the `PreToolUse` hook.
- **CLI** (`internal/cli/inputcmds.go`). `devc send <session> [text | -] [--steer]`, `devc answer <session> <choice | text> [--question id] [--text]`, `devc interrupt <session>`, `devc keys <session> <key>…`, `devc seen <session> [--turn n]`, `devc <harness> [ref] --detach --prompt "<text>"` (prepares the box and worktree as before, then `session.start` through the daemon; without `--wait` it returns once the session exists). All take `--id`, `--wait`, `--json`, print `<id> <outcome>[ session <sid>][: detail]`, exit 1 on `refused` or `typed-not-submitted`, and say that they need the daemon when none answers. Sessions by ID or unique prefix.
- **Fake harness.** On a terminal: raw mode, own line editing, bracketed paste (a paste plus Enter is one prompt), Escape or C-c ends the current turn as `idle`, a line submitted during a turn has its prompt reported at once and runs after it; at start it sends `OSC 7501 ; ?` followed by `CSI c` and reports through OSC 7501 when the answer comes first. Flags `--ignore-first-submit`, `--never-submit`, `--busy <s>`, `--winsize`; modes `--cat` (prints `<cat ready>`, then echoes every byte; `<winsize RxC>` on resize); command `askhook <question> [| option…]`. New test manifest `fakeosc` (osc7501, input keys, `question_wait = 8`).
- **Tests.** Unit: `internal/ptywrap` (scanner on fixed cases in pieces of 1, 3, 2/5 bytes, oversized and exact-limit sequences, 300 rounds of random bytes with escapes and sequences in random pieces, report parsing, a benchmark; the wrapper with pipes: status and reports, signal, query answered, 4,000 arbitrary input bytes unchanged, a lone escape flushed, terminal settings restored after a kill), `internal/daemon` (`TestCommands` against an in-process agent whose tmux plays a harness: delivery, idempotence, no text in final receipts, queue waiting and order, ignore-first, never-submit, gate, interrupt, keys, refusals, answer through a waiting hook and on screen, seen, ended session; `TestCommandsSurviveRestart`; `TestStartAndStop`; `TestGateDecision`, `TestAtRest`, `TestReceiptStore` with concurrent creates, `TestPromptHashes`), `internal/boxside` (paste/keys/clients with argument checks, question hooks answered and every fallback, hook states under the wrapper, state writer, launcher wrapping), `internal/proto`, `internal/tmux`, `internal/cli` (usage), `test/fakeharness` (terminal mode on a pseudo-terminal: query, OSC reports, paste, interrupt, submit modes, cat). Integration (`test/integration/input_test.go`): `TestStatusWrapper` (items 1, 2), `TestInput` (items 3–12).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **The wrapper runs only on a terminal.** The work order says "when a launch description has `status.osc7501 = true`"; without a terminal (`claude -p … > file`, `podman exec` without `-t`) a pseudo-terminal would change the harness's behaviour and its output (carriage returns), so it is executed directly.
2. **Hook states stop only after the first report.** The wrapper's variable alone would leave a harness that never reports (an old version) without any state; the hook receiver also requires the wrapper's marker file, created at the first report.
3. **Two orders per session instead of one.** "One command at a time per session; later ones wait in order" holds within each FIFO, and one lock keeps injections apart; a queued send waiting for the session must not hold back an answer or interrupt the session may be waiting for, and `steer` means "now".
4. **"At rest" needs a state after the last prompt** (`state_seq > prompt_seq`), so a queued send cannot slip in between a delivered prompt and the harness's `working`; `error` counts as at rest; a session without any state report counts as at rest 60 s after its start.
5. **Extra receipt state `delivering`** marks a command whose input may have begun; it is what makes "typed at most once" hold across a crash. `accepted` is the stored-and-waiting state.
6. **Command IDs** are session-ID-like or UUIDs only (the C08 type allowed any word); they name files.
7. **`session.seen` takes an optional `id`** (the table of section 3 does not list it; section 6 says every command prints a receipt). Without an ID the receipt is returned but not stored.
8. **Answer events are host-written** (`via: hook | screen`) when devc delivered the answer; a box cannot write `answer` or `seen`.
9. **Questions with several parts** (Claude's AskUserQuestion with more than one question) are recorded but always shown as the dialog; the remote answer covers one question.
10. **The structured adapters** are not built (documentation not specific enough; see LEFTOVER); the daemon falls back to paste with a note in the receipt rather than refusing.
11. **A held prefix is flushed after 30 ms.** The scanner otherwise holds a trailing `ESC` (or `ESC ]7…`) until the next output; with a program that echoes a lone escape and then waits, that byte would never appear (found by the transparency test).
12. **Session start through the daemon needs a running box** and an existing launch description; the daemon does not run `up` or create worktrees (the CLI does both before `session.start`). It waits up to 10 s for its box watch to see a box the CLI has just started.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; a project listing `fakeosc` (from `test/fakeharness/manifests`, `@FAKEAGENT@` replaced) or `claude`; `bin/devc harness install fakeosc`; `bin/devc up <ref>`; `bin/devc daemon` in another terminal; `bin/devc fakeosc <ref> --detach`; `bin/devc events <session> --json` (states with `"source":"osc"`); `bin/devc send <session> "say hi" --wait`; `bin/devc send <session> "sleep 30" --steer`, then `bin/devc interrupt <session> --wait`; `bin/devc send <session> "askhook which? | a | b" --steer` and `bin/devc answer <session> 2 --wait`; `bin/devc seen <session>`; `bin/devc fakeosc <ref> --detach --prompt "say first" --wait`. In a box: `/opt/devc/bin/devc box pty -- /opt/devc/harness/fakeosc/current/bin/fakeagent --cat`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/ptywrap/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/boxside/ ./test/fakeharness/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestStatusWrapper|TestInput' -v`.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes; `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 33 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (`TestStatusWrapper` 15–32 s, `TestInput` about 90 s). `DEVC_ITEST_DAEMON=1 ./dev itest -run 'TestSessions|TestStatusWrapper|TestInput' -v` passes. 128 KiB of random bytes went through `podman exec -t`, the wrapper and the fake's cat mode and back unchanged in about 0.2 s. The last code change (`session.stop` no longer waits for the box channel) came after the full run and is covered by `TestStartAndStop` and the daemon-mode run. No test containers, images, daemons or directories were left behind.

### C10a Relay service, requests, on-demand secrets

**What exists.**

- **Relay service.** In the box, `git-remote-devc` (`boxside.RemoteHelperMain`) pings the host through the agent; when connected it offers `connect` (plus `fetch`/`push` for the fallback): `connect git-upload-pack|git-receive-pack` sends `host.git {service, session}` on a new agent stream, waits up to 3 minutes for the answer line, then relays bytes both ways (`CloseWrite` when git closes its side). Not connected, or the host gone between the two steps (`fallback`): the C05 stub. A refusal is printed and fails the git command. The agent's yamux `StreamCloseTimeout` is now 35 minutes, so a push stream half-closed after the pack survives receive-pack's checks and hooks. In the daemon (`internal/daemon/relay.go`), every box stream's first line is read first: `host.git` takes the stream over, anything else is JSON-RPC as before. The project is the channel's; the configuration must be free of problems and the relay must exist. upload-pack: optional fetch-through, then `git upload-pack --strict --timeout=600 <relay>` with `uploadpack.hideRefs=refs/devc`. receive-pack: the relay hooks are ensured, the box lock is taken (2 minutes, else "busy"), a report file is made in `local/tmp`, then `git receive-pack <relay>` with `core.hooksPath=<state>/local/git/relay-hooks`, `receive.hideRefs=refs/devc` and `refs/remotes`, `receive.denyDeletes`, `receive.autogc=false`, `receive.fsckObjects`, no push options, no shallow updates, and the environment `DEVC_RELAY_PROJECT`, `DEVC_RELAY_REPORT`, `DEVC_CONFIG_DIR`, `DEVC_STATE_DIR`, `DEVC_RUNTIME_DIR`. Both run through `gitx.Host` (isolated configuration, `GIT_*` removed, from `/`, `Host.Serve`: new session without a terminal, 30-minute limit), standard input fed through a pipe of the daemon's own so the run ends when git exits. Afterwards the report becomes a `note` event in the pushing session's journal (session from the request, validated, only if it has a journal in this project).
- **Push policy** (`gitx.CheckUpdate`, run by `devc relay-hook update <ref> <old> <new>`): branches created and updated (also non-fast-forward), deletion refused, `git.protected` refused (a malformed pattern protects everything), names as in banks (`names.CheckRef` plus `git check-ref-format --branch`); tags created only; everything else refused; malformed input refused; every refusal starts `devc: refused:` and reaches the pusher. Any configuration problem refuses all updates.
- **Recording pushes** (`gitx.RecordPush`, run by `devc relay-hook post-receive`): input parsed strictly; every new branch tip gets `refs/devc/bank/<b>/<t>` unless it equals the newest one; `refs/devc/boxtip/<b>` moves to the pushed tip only when the box's own `refs/heads/<b>` has it (read with `git for-each-ref` in the box, as dev), so `git push origin wip:feature` is no observation of the box's `feature` and the next bank does not take it back, while a pull after a normal push is kept; a new tag gets `refs/devc/tags/<t>/<time>` (so `devc push --tag` finds it). Branches matching `git.forward` are pushed to the upstream (background mode, 2 minutes per branch, at most 10 minutes) and the result goes to the pusher (`remote: devc: forwarded …`). With configuration problems nothing is forwarded.
- **Hooks** are symbolic links `update` and `post-receive` in `<state>/local/git/relay-hooks` (0700) to the daemon's own executable (`gitx.EnsureRelayHooks`, rewritten when the target differs, anything else removed); `cmd/devc` runs `devc relay-hook <name>` when invoked under those names with `DEVC_RELAY_PROJECT` set. No shell is involved.
- **Fetch-through** (`gitx.Service.FetchThrough`): before an upload-pack, for projects with an upstream and `git.fetch_through` (default true), at most once a minute per project (attempts count): box lock (5 s), upstream fetch with 20 s, then `syncHeads` as `devc pull` does (box tips untouched). Any failure is logged and the box gets what the relay has. Upstream commands in background mode (`gitx.Host.Background`): `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`, no `GIT_ASKPASS`/`SSH_ASKPASS`/`DISPLAY`, started in a new session (`execx.Cmd.Setsid`) so ssh cannot reach a terminal.
- **Box origin.** `InitBox` adds the fetch refspec `+refs/remotes/github/*:refs/remotes/github/*` and `remote.origin.tagOpt = --tags`.
- **Banking after turns** (`internal/daemon/turns.go`): the hub reports every live `state` event that moves a session from working to done or idle; the daemon runs `devc bank <ref> --quiet` (a child process with the daemon's directories, from `/`) at once, or when 30 s have passed since the last one for that box; turns in the meantime give one more bank. Only boxes with a relay that are running.
- **Requests** (`internal/requests`, `internal/proto/requests.go`, `internal/daemon/requests.go`). In the box `devc request seal|savepoint|note|package|net|grant|pr …` and `devc request status <id> [--wait]` (needs `DEVC_BOX=1` and `box.json`; validates locally first; a seal path that exists in the box is resolved through symlinks such as a worktree's `data` link; `DEVC_SESSION` is passed when valid). The daemon decodes strictly and validates (`requests.Validate`: kinds, fields that belong to the kind only, package names `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9+.-]{0,63}$` (1–32, no duplicates), managers apt/dnf/apk, `host:port` with a host name or IPv4 address or `lan`, durations 1m–168h (default 1h), SSH host aliases, secret names, savepoint names, branch names, note ≤ 2000 and title ≤ 256 characters single-line, body ≤ 8000, no control characters, paths without `..` or controls); seal paths are then resolved against the data root with C06's `data.Normalize` (projects without data refuse). Refusals are stored nowhere. At most 100 pending requests and 20 per minute per project. Requests are kept in `durable/realms/<realm>/requests/<project>.jsonl` (0600 in 0700; one line per change, last line of an ID wins, torn lines skipped) with ID (`r` + 20 time-ordered base32hex), time, project, session, kind, parameters, risk, status, result, update time, deciding origin, reason. Each change also becomes a `request` event (new host-only type: ID, kind, risk, status, summary) in the session's journal. `[requests] auto` (default seal, savepoint, note; only low-risk kinds allowed) approves at once with origin `auto`. `requests.list {all, realm, project}`, `requests.decide {id, approve, reason, origin}`: the local socket is origin `local`; a local client may declare the weaker origin `hub` (never the other way: `requests.EffectiveOrigin`); `requests.MayDecide` is the only place that says local decides anything, auto and hub low-risk only. Approved requests run as child processes: `devc seal -- <path> <ref>`, `devc save -- [name] <ref>`, `devc root <ref> -- env DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get update` then `… apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends <names>`, `dnf install -y`, `apk add --no-progress` (root log as for every `devc root`); notes are done when approved; net, grant and pr fail with "not available yet". Results keep the last 2000 characters of the output. Pending requests expire after seven days (at start, at every housekeeping pass and whenever a request is read); requests found approved at start fail without being repeated.
- **Host CLI.** `devc requests [--all] [--json]` (through the daemon, else read from the logs), `devc requests approve <id> [--wait]`, `devc requests deny <id> [--reason …]` (need the daemon; IDs or unique prefixes).
- **On-demand secrets.** `[secrets] delivery = "files" | "daemon"` in `config.toml` (default files; shown by `config show`; `Resolved.SecretsDelivery`). With daemon: `devc up` writes nothing into `/run/devc-secrets` (an earlier delivery is removed; the mount stays, so the box hash does not change), launch descriptions carry `delivery: "daemon"`, and the launcher asks `host.secret {name}` (10 s) for the credential and every granted secret with an environment name; the daemon answers only for `grants.secrets` and the secrets of the project's env-token/api-key harnesses, refuses in files mode, never logs values. Unreachable daemon: the harness starts without them and the launcher says how to fix it (asked once). `devc box secret <name>` prints a granted secret in such a box.
- **Sandbox description** mentions git push/fetch through the relay, banking after turns, `devc request` with every kind and which kinds are approved automatically, and that a finished run is sealed with `devc request seal`.
- **Documentation.** `docs/guide.md` (relay service, requests, secret delivery); `examples/config` shows `git.fetch_through`, `[requests] auto` and `[secrets] delivery`.
- **Tests.** Unit: `TestCheckUpdate` (the policy table), `TestParseHookInput`, `TestServeCall`, `TestEnsureRelayHooks`, `TestRecordPush` (with real git: bank refs, box tips, tags, no duplicate or reverted tip after a bank, `wip:feature`, a pull kept, forward); `requests.TestValidate` (every kind, about 60 hostile values), `TestRisk`, `TestMayDecide` (origins and declarations), `TestStore`; daemon `TestRateLimit`, `TestRequests` (auto, hub on low and high risk, local approval, root commands from `/`, deny with reason, failure, not available, hostile values stored nowhere, other project's request invisible, journal events, expiry), `TestRequestFlood`, `TestRequestCommands`, `TestRequestsInterrupted`, `TestSecrets`, `TestTurnBanking` (interval, coalescing, blocked is no end), `TestRelayStreams` (real git through agent and daemon: no relay, hidden refs, hooks, garbage streams); boxside `TestRemoteHelperConnect` (relay, fallback, refusal, stub, session), `TestDaemonSecrets`; `cmd/devc` hook dispatch; `proto` request events (host-only, validated). Integration (`test/integration/relay_test.go`): `TestRelayService` (items 1–8), `TestRequests` (9–12), `TestSecretsDaemon` (13). `TestGit` item 11 and the escape suite's git group accept relay-service pushes when a daemon runs (`DEVC_ITEST_DAEMON=1`).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Hooks are links to the devc binary, not scripts.** The work order says the hooks "call `devc relay-hook update|post-receive`"; a shell script would have to embed the binary's path. `cmd/devc` maps the names `update` and `post-receive` (with `DEVC_RELAY_PROJECT` set) to `devc relay-hook`, which also exists as a hidden command.
2. **Box tips move with a push only when the box's branch has the pushed tip** (one `git for-each-ref` in the box from post-receive). Recording the pushed tip always would let the next bank revert `git push origin wip:feature` to the box's older `feature`; never recording it would let a bank revert a pull that followed a normal push.
3. **The box lock is held for a whole receive-pack** (wait 2 minutes): a bank and a push never interleave, so neither sees the other's half-done update. A box that stalls its own push holds only its own lock.
4. **"SSH batch mode" without `-o BatchMode=yes`.** Setting `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` would override the user's own `core.sshCommand`; instead background upstream commands run without a controlling terminal and without askpass programs, so ssh fails instead of asking. Credential helpers and agents still work.
5. **Receive-pack refuses deletions itself too** (`receive.denyDeletes`, defence in depth); git then says "deletion prohibited" instead of devc's message. `refs/remotes/*` is hidden from receive-pack, so pushes there are refused as hidden refs.
6. **Tags pushed through the relay service** land in `refs/tags/<t>` and are recorded under `refs/devc/tags`; a later upstream fetch may overwrite `refs/tags/<t>` (as before), the record stays.
7. **`tagOpt = --tags`** for the box's origin: git refuses to clobber a box tag that differs from the relay's, and `git fetch` then fails for that tag (it says so).
8. **Origin declarations.** `requests.decide` takes `origin`, but only to weaken a local decision to `hub` (used by the tests, later by anything relaying for a hub); the connection decides the origin.
9. **Request execution reuses the CLI as child processes** (as C08 housekeeping does), so locks, fail-closed checks and the root log apply unchanged. `--` ends flag parsing before box-chosen values.
10. **Extra commands:** `devc box secret <name>` (programs outside a harness in daemon mode), `devc requests approve --wait`. Approved requests interrupted by a daemon stop fail at the next start rather than run twice.
11. **Seal paths in the box** are resolved through symlinks when they exist there (a worktree's `data` link), else sent as given (relative to the data root); the host validates either way.

**Upgrade notes.** Boxes started before this chunk run the old agent (1-minute stream close timeout): pushes whose hooks take longer than a minute can break until the box is restarted. Projects initialised before need `devc project init <ref>` once so the box's origin fetches `github/*` and tags. The daemon must be restarted to serve the relay, requests and secrets.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; a project with an upstream and a running box; `bin/devc daemon` in another terminal; in the box (`bin/devc sh <ref>`): `git push -u origin feature`, `git push origin --delete feature` (refused), `git tag t1 && git push origin t1`, `git fetch origin`; on the host `bin/devc log <ref> -- --all --oneline`. Requests: `devc request seal results/run1`, `devc request package apt jq`, then on the host `bin/devc requests`, `bin/devc requests approve <id> --wait`, `bin/devc requests deny <id> --reason x`; in the box `devc request status <id> --wait`. Secrets: `[secrets] delivery = "daemon"` in `config.toml`, `bin/devc up --recreate <ref>` (or down/up), start a harness. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/gitx/ ./internal/requests/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/boxside/ ./cmd/...`; `./dev itest -run 'TestRelayService|TestRequests|TestSecretsDaemon' -v`.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes; `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 36 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (`TestRelayService` about 50 s, `TestRequests` about 30 s, `TestSecretsDaemon` about 10 s). That run used the binary from before the last change (approved requests failing at daemon start, with its unit test); afterwards `DEVC_ITEST_DAEMON=1 ./dev itest -run 'TestGit$|TestEscape|TestSessions|TestBankAll|TestRelayService|TestRequests|TestSecretsDaemon' -v` passes with a fresh build. No test containers, images, daemons or directories were left behind.

### C10b SSH gate

**What exists.**

- **Package `internal/gate`** (new; host side). `gate.go`: constants (`SFTPMarker = devc-sftp-marker`, `HostKeyAlias = devc-gate`, `EnvSSHConfig = DEVC_SSH_CONFIG`, 4 KiB log cap), `SocketPath` (`<runtime>/gate/m-<16 hex of SHA-256(alias)>.sock`; fails when the path plus ssh's 17-byte temporary suffix exceeds 107 bytes), `HostFile` (`m-<hash>.host`, the alias, for `ssh ls`). `client.go`: the host's ssh commands, all with `-F $DEVC_SSH_CONFIG` when set and `%` escaped in `-S`: `LoginCmd` (`-M -S <sock> -o ControlPersist=<seconds> -o ServerAliveInterval=30 <host> true`), `Check`/`Exit` (`-O check|exit`, 10 s, own session), `ModeOf` (shell / sftp / exec from `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND`), `ExecArgs` (multiplexing client only: `-o ControlMaster=no -o ProxyCommand=false -o BatchMode=yes -o ClearAllForwardings=yes -o ForwardAgent=no -o ForwardX11=no -o Tunnel=no -o PermitLocalCommand=no -o RemoteCommand=none -o LogLevel=ERROR -e none`, then `-tt|-T <host>`, `-T -s <host> sftp`, or `-tt|-T <host> -- "<command>"`). `keys.go`: `Keys.EnsureHostKey` (`local/gate/host_ed25519`, once per machine under a lock), `NewBoxKey` (new ed25519 pair per box start, generated on the runtime tmpfs; `local/gate/keys/<box-id>.pub` = `restrict,pty ssh-ed25519 … devc-box-<id>`, 0600; returns the private key), `RemoveBoxKey`. `sshd.go`: `ExecTarget` (forced command `'<devc>' '--config-dir' … '--state-dir' … 'gate-exec' '--box' <id> '--host' <h> '--runtime-dir' … ['--ssh-config' …]`, every word single-quoted, words with `'"\!%` or control characters refused), `ServerConfig.Render` (paths double-quoted, quotes/backslash/`%`/`#` refused; `AuthenticationMethods publickey`, no password or keyboard-interactive, `UsePAM no`, `StrictModes no`, `DisableForwarding yes` plus every forwarding switch of the work order, `PermitOpen none`, `PermitListen none`, `PermitUserEnvironment no`, `PermitUserRC no`, no `AcceptEnv`, `PermitTTY yes`, `Subsystem sftp devc-sftp-marker`, `ForceCommand`, `PidFile none`), `Server.Prepare` (checks both keys, writes `<runtime>/gate/sshd-<box-id>-<random>.conf` 0600) and `Server.Command` (`sshd -i -e -f <conf>`, own session). `filter.go`: `CheckReadOnly` and `ReadOnlyNote`. `exec.go`: `Exec.Run`, the body of `devc gate-exec` (below). `log.go`: `AppendLog` (`durable/realms/<realm>/gate-log.jsonl`, 0600, `O_APPEND|O_NOFOLLOW`, one line per session under `flock`; fields time, box, project, host, mode, tty, command ≤ 4 KiB, exit, duration, refused). `box.go`: `ClientConfig`, `KnownHosts`, `HostLogin`, `BoxHooks`.
- **Configuration.** `grants.ssh` entries are `<host>` or `<host>:ro`; new `[grants.ssh_allow] <host> = [...]` (`config.Grants.SSHAllow`, copied into `Resolved`). Helpers `ValidHostAlias`, `ParseSSHGrant`, `Grants.SSHGrants`, `SSHGrantFor`, `SSHAllowed`. Validation: alias rule unchanged (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, first character alphanumeric), a host granted once (with or without `:ro`), an `ssh_allow` list only for a host granted `:ro`, each entry one word without blanks, quotes or metacharacters, no duplicates; the secret name `gate_key` is reserved. Example `analysis/project.toml` documents both.
- **Box lifecycle** (`gate.BoxHooks`, wired in `cli/boxcmds.go`). Contributor: a project with a grant gets `/etc/devc/ssh_config` mounted read-only at `/etc/ssh/ssh_config.d/50-devc.conf` (new `box.Mount.File`: the source must be a regular file and is never created as a directory; a file mount nested in another mount is refused). Preparer: host key, `/etc/devc/ssh_config` (one block per granted host, exactly the work order's lines, `User` = the host login name) and `/etc/devc/gate_known_hosts` (`devc-gate ssh-ed25519 …`), both removed for a project without grants. Secret source (new `harness.BoxHooks.Extra`, called by the secrets delivery): a new key pair at every start, the private key delivered as `gate_key`, in `files` and in `daemon` mode. Cleaner: the public key goes at `down`, `rm` and a rolled-back start.
- **Daemon** (`internal/daemon/gate.go`). A box stream whose first line is `host.gate {host}` (new method, `proto.GateParams`/`GateReply`) is served like `host.git`: alias validated, project = the channel's, configuration without problems, host in that project's `grants.ssh` (either profile), sshd found (`Options.SSHD`, else PATH and the sbin directories), both keys present (else "restart the box"); then the response line and `sshd -i -e -f <conf>` with the stream as standard input (through a pipe of the daemon's own) and output; sshd's log is kept (16 KiB) and logged at info (600 characters); the configuration file is removed afterwards. `Options.SSHConfig` carries `DEVC_SSH_CONFIG` (made absolute) into the forced command.
- **`devc gate-exec`** (hidden). Loads the configuration with the directories from its flags, finds the project by box ID, refuses (exit 1, one line on stderr, logged with `refused`) on configuration problems, a host no longer granted, and under `:ro` an interactive shell, sftp, or a command failing `CheckReadOnly`; refuses with exit 255 and "no SSH connection to <host> is established on the host; ask the user to run 'devc ssh login <host>'" when `-O check` fails; otherwise runs the multiplexing ssh with its own standard input, output and error, a terminal (`-tt`) when `SSH_TTY` is set, logs, and exits with the remote status (255 when ssh could not run). SIGHUP, SIGTERM and SIGINT cancel the run through the context, so the session is still logged.
- **`devc ssh login <host> [--ttl 12h]`** (attached to the terminal; already-alive master: says so; stale socket removed first; `--ttl` 1 min–30 days), **`devc ssh ls [--json]`** (alias and `alive`/`dead` for every recorded host), **`devc ssh close <host>`** (`-O exit`; idempotent). The gate directory is `<runtime>/gate` (0700, `paths.GateRuntimeDir`), never mounted; new `paths.GateDir/GateHostKey/GateKeysDir/GateBoxKey/GateLog` and the in-box constants `InBoxSSHConfig`, `InBoxSSHConfigD`, `InBoxGateKnownHosts`.
- **Box side.** `devc box gate-connect <host>` (`boxside.GateConnect`): opens the `host.gate` stream through the agent (stream opening factored out of the relay dialer as `openAgentStream`), relays standard input and output, and otherwise prints one line ("the devc daemon on the host is not connected to this box…", the host's refusal, or "the box agent does not answer") and exits 255. `devc box init` puts `Include /etc/devc/ssh_config` at the top of `~/.ssh/config` when `/etc/devc/ssh_config` exists (`EnsureSSHInclude`: creates `~/.ssh` 0700 and the file 0600, keeps an existing file's content and mode, does nothing when the line is there, leaves a symlinked config alone and logs why).
- **Sandbox description**: an SSH paragraph for projects with grants (hosts reachable with plain `ssh <host>`, `scp`/`sftp`/`rsync -e ssh`/git over SSH, read-only hosts with their commands, that it runs as the user's account, that `-L -R -D -A -X` are refused, and what to tell the user without a master or without the daemon).
- **Doctor**: `ssh-keygen` added to the `tools` check (warn).
- **Documentation**: `docs/guide.md`, "SSH to other machines (the SSH gate)".
- **Tests.** Unit: `config` (`TestSSHGrants`, `TestValidHostAlias`, eight new problem cases), `gate` (`TestSocketPath`, `TestLoginAndControlCommands`, `TestModeOf`, `TestExecArgs`, `TestCheckReadOnly` with every metacharacter, `TestServerConfig`, `TestExecTargetCommand`, `TestTruncate`, `TestAppendLog`, `TestExecFullGrant`, `TestExecRefusals`, `TestExecConfigurationProblems`, `TestBoxHooks`, `TestServerPrepare`), `daemon` (`TestGateStreams`: refusals, sshd invocation and configuration, the stream as sshd's input and output, a removed grant closing at once), `boxside` (`TestGateConnectWithoutHost`, `TestEnsureSSHInclude`, `TestInitSSHInclude`), `box` (`TestFileMount`), `harness` (`TestSandboxSSH`), `cli` (`TestSSHUsage`, `TestSSHCommands`), `doctor`. Integration: `test/integration/gate_test.go` `TestSSHGate` (items 1–11; the remote is a second unprivileged `sshd -D` on a free loopback port with its own host and user keys, reached through a temporary `DEVC_SSH_CONFIG` under two aliases; every process the test starts carries a marker in its environment and the test fails if one is left). `itest.Env.ExtraEnv` (new) passes `DEVC_SSH_CONFIG` to every devc process, daemons included.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **A shell without a terminal uses `-T`.** The work order says an empty `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND` gives `ssh … -tt <host>`. With direct SSH, `ssh host < script` runs the remote shell without a terminal; forcing one would echo the input and turn newlines into CRLF. So `-tt` only when the box's client asked for a terminal (`SSH_TTY` set), as for commands.
2. **Extra options on gate-exec's ssh.** Beyond `-S … -o ControlMaster=no`: `ProxyCommand=false` (a master that dies between the check and the use must not be replaced by a fresh connection, which could log in with the user's own keys or agent and outlive `devc ssh close`; verified that ssh then fails instead of connecting), `BatchMode`, `ClearAllForwardings` and no agent, X11 or tunnel (a mux client asks the master for the forwardings of the user's own configuration), `RemoteCommand=none`, `PermitLocalCommand=no`, `-e none` (the box's own client handles `~` escapes; a second escape level would act on `~~.`), `LogLevel=ERROR` (no "Shared connection closed" line). The sftp case also passes `-T`.
3. **The sftp marker arrives as `devc-sftp-marker ` with a trailing blank** (OpenSSH 9.6 appends the subsystem's arguments); blanks around it are ignored. Found by the integration test.
4. **The configuration mount exists only for boxes with a grant**, so projects without grants keep their box definition (no drift from this chunk). Adding a grant therefore changes the definition and needs `devc up --recreate`; removing one closes the gate at once (the daemon and gate-exec both check the current configuration).
5. **A new box key at every start** (the work order allows keeping one). The private key is never stored on the host outside the box's secrets directory (generated on the runtime tmpfs); a copy a box kept from an earlier run is worthless. The public key is removed at `down` too, not only at `rm`.
6. **gate-exec's flags.** sshd gives its sessions a fresh environment, so the daemon's directories travel in the forced command (`--config-dir`, `--state-dir`, `--runtime-dir`, `--ssh-config`) rather than in `DEVC_*` variables; `SetEnv` in sshd_config would need OpenSSH 8.7.
7. **Read-only filter details.** The first blank-separated word (space or tab, as a shell splits) is compared literally with the list; control characters other than tab are refused as well as the listed metacharacters; quotes, globs and `~` are allowed.
8. **`devc ssh ls` lists the hosts `devc ssh login` recorded** (`m-<hash>.host` beside each socket), since socket names are hashes.

**Upgrade notes.** The daemon must be restarted to serve `host.gate`; a box needs a restart (with `--recreate` when it had no grant before) to get its key, `/etc/devc/ssh_config` and the Include line.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; in `project.toml` `[grants] ssh = ["<alias>"]` (an alias of `~/.ssh/config`); `bin/devc up --recreate <ref>`; `bin/devc daemon` in another terminal; `bin/devc ssh login <alias>`; `bin/devc ssh ls`; in the box (`bin/devc sh <ref>`): `ssh <alias> hostname`, `ssh -t <alias>`, `scp f <alias>:/tmp/`, `git clone <alias>:<repo>`; `cat ~/.local/share/devc/durable/realms/<realm>/gate-log.jsonl`; `bin/devc ssh close <alias>`, then `ssh <alias> true` in the box prints the login hint and exits 255. Read-only: `ssh = ["<alias>:ro"]` and `[grants.ssh_allow] <alias> = ["ls"]`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/gate/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/cli/ ./internal/config/`; `./dev itest -run TestSSHGate -v`.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes; `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 38 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (`TestSSHGate` 43–61 s). The forwarding check's SOCKS output was changed to hex after that run; `TestSSHGate` was run again and passes (box's view: `L:[]`, `R:255`, `D:[0500]` (the box client's own SOCKS greeting, then nothing), `W:[]`, `agent:[]`; the gate's sshd logs "refused local port forward"). Password and 2FA logins were not exercised (key authentication only), SELinux was not either. No test containers, images, daemons, sshd processes, SSH masters or directories were left behind.

### C11a Hub core

**What exists.**

- **Package `internal/hub`** (new; the hub server). `hub.go`: `ResolveDir` (`DEVC_HUB_DIR`, default `~/.local/share/devc-hub`), `Layout` (`ca/ca.crt`, `ca/ca.key`, `server/server.{key,csr,crt}`, `hub.db`), `Init` (directory 0700, CA, database, and with a public name the server key, a CSR for a public CA and a server certificate from the devc CA). `ca.go`: CA (ECDSA P-256, ten years, path length 0), `IssueClient` (key and one-year client certificate, CN = name, OU = role, O = devc, client-auth only; devices also get a PKCS#12 bundle with key, certificate and CA, `pkcs12.Modern` or `Legacy`, passphrase of four groups of five characters from an unambiguous alphabet, printed once), `IssueServer`, `Fingerprint` (SHA-256 of the DER). `store.go`: SQLite (`modernc.org/sqlite`, WAL, busy timeout, immediate write transactions, writes serialised in-process, files 0600): `certs` (registry: fingerprint, role, name, serial, issued, expires, revoked; every change bumps `meta.registry_gen`), `machines` (name, fingerprint, host, version, instance, online, notifier, connected, last_seen, boxes), `sessions` (latest view per machine and session with the seq it was folded to: the cursor), `events` (tail per session: the most recent `TailEvents` = 500, at most `TailBytes` = 1 MiB, always the newest), `requests` (pending), `changes` (log of the last 20,000 changes that streams resume from). `validate.go`: everything a machine sends is decoded strictly and checked: events (`proto.ValidateData`, re-encoded), views (identity, enums, sizes, question), requests (`requests.Validate`, kind/risk/status/origin), boxes, daemon info. `inbox.go`: `SectionOf`, `BuildInbox`, `SessionSummary` (view + `stale`, `requests`, `section`). `server.go`: `New`/`Run`, the two listeners, the front (below), the registry watcher (every second; on a change every machine link and event stream whose certificate is no longer valid is closed), hourly pruning of sessions that ended before the 30-day window. `link.go`: the machine link. `api.go`, `stream.go`: the API.
- **Serving.** Direct TLS: TLS 1.2+, `RequireAndVerifyClientCert` against the devc CA, and `VerifyConnection` asks the registry, so an unknown or revoked certificate fails the handshake; HTTP/1.1. Behind a proxy: plain HTTP, refused at start unless the listen host is a loopback address; `X-Devc-Client-Cert` (URL-encoded PEM or base64 DER) honoured only from a loopback peer, verified against the CA again (client-auth usage). Then, per request, in one place (`Server.ServeHTTP`): security headers (nosniff, no-store, no-referrer, `default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`), `Host` must name the public name (port 443 optional), identity from the registry (known, not expired, not revoked; role and name from the registry), `/machine` for machine certificates only, `/api/` for device certificates only plus `checkUser` (the hook for C11b's session check), 1 MiB body limit (64 KiB of headers), and for anything but GET: `application/json`, an `Origin` (when sent) equal to the hub's origin and `Sec-Fetch-Site` (when sent) same-origin or none. No CORS headers are ever sent.
- **Machine link, hub side.** WebSocket on `/machine` (`coder/websocket`), the codec over `internal/wsconn` (new: one JSON object per text frame; binary frames, frames with a newline and frames above 1 MiB end the connection; read/write deadlines; closing does not wait for the peer). The machine must say `hub.hello` within 10 s (validated; recorded as online with notifier off before the answer, which names the machine as the registry does); `hub.role {notifier}` is recorded. A new connection of the same machine replaces the old one after it has marked itself down. The hub then asks `daemon.info` (boxes) and `events.subscribe` with its stored cursors (sessions live or active within 30 days) and a 30-day window; after `caught_up` and every 30 s it lists sessions (since 30 days, pages of 500) and boxes and the pending requests; request events and decisions refresh the requests. One worker per machine applies notifications in batches of up to 500 in one transaction: an event is applied only if its seq is the session's cursor + 1 (duplicates dropped); a gap, a `missed` with a project, or a session the list shows but the stream did not bring is read again with `sessions.get` (view and 500 events, events arriving meanwhile held back and applied afterwards); `missed` without a project removes the session. Calls to the machine never block the worker (they run in goroutines; their results come back through the queue). On disconnect the machine is marked offline (notifier off, `last_seen`), its rows stay. At start every machine is offline.
- **API** (JSON; `docs/hub-deployment.md` section 6): `GET /api/machines`, `GET /api/inbox`, `GET /api/sessions?machine=&realm=&project=&live=&limit=`, `GET /api/sessions/<machine>/<session>?before=&limit=` (from the tail; with `before`, events the hub does not hold come from the machine's `events.read`; `partial` when it cannot be asked), `POST /api/commands` (`{machine, type, id, params, wait}`, types send/answer/interrupt/keys/seen/start/stop mapped to the daemon's methods, params decoded strictly into the method's parameter type, ID required and a UUID or session-ID form, optional wait ≤ 60 s through `command.get`; the machine's receipt is returned), `GET /api/commands/<machine>/<id>?wait=`, `GET /api/requests`, `POST /api/requests/<machine>/<id>/decide` (`{approve, reason}`, no origin sent). Machine errors map to HTTP statuses (refused 403, not found 404, invalid 400, busy 429, not offered 501, offline 503, timeout 504). A command or decision for an offline machine gets 503 at once. Snapshots carry `stream_id`.
- **Stream** `GET /api/stream?session=<machine>/<session>` (≤ 32): server-sent events from the change log, each with its ID; `session` (summary with section), `session.removed`, `machine`, `request`, `request.removed` for everyone, `event` and `session.reset` for named sessions; `Last-Event-ID` (or `?last_id=`) resumes after that ID; no ID: `ready` at the present; an ID the log no longer reaches (or from another hub): `reset`; a comment every 15 s; ends when the device certificate is revoked.
- **Machine side** (`internal/daemon/hublink.go`). `hubMethods`, the allowlist in one place, and `hubHandlers` built from it: `daemon.info` (without config/state directory and PID), `boxes.list`, `sessions.list` (`sync` forced off), `sessions.get`, `events.read`, `events.subscribe`, the session commands of C09, `command.get`, `requests.list`, `requests.decide` with origin `hub` (`requests.EffectiveOrigin` refuses a declared `local`; `requests.MayDecide` refuses high risk). Everything else (`daemon.stop`, `hubs.status`, host methods) is unknown on a hub connection. One link per hub listed in `config.toml` (in order, normalised, duplicates ignored), following configuration reloads and, at every box-watch pass, the credentials' stamp (a new `devc join` reconnects); dial `wss://<hub>/machine` with the stored client certificate (and stored CA, else system roots), `HTTPS_PROXY` honoured, 30 s; `hub.hello {protocol, name, version, instance}`; the codec serves up to 8 hub calls at once; ping every 30 s; reconnection with backoff from 1 s doubling to 2 minutes (+0–20 % jitter), back to 1 s after a connection that lasted 30 s. The first connected hub in the list is the notifier; every connected hub is told its role (`hub.role`) whenever it changes. New local method `hubs.status` (per hub: state connecting/connected/disconnected/no-credentials, detail, since, notifier, the machine name the hub uses).
- **`internal/hubnode`** (new): `NormalizeURL`, `ID` (16 hex of SHA-256 of the URL), credentials under `durable/node/hubs/<id>/` (`hub.json` with the URL, `cert.pem`, `key.pem`, optional `ca.pem`; 0600 in 0700, written atomically), `Load`, `Remove`, `Stamp`, and the `config.toml` edits: `AddToConfig` appends a `[[hubs]]` block, `RemoveFromConfig` removes the block of that URL (with the blank line before it); both decode the result and refuse when anything but that entry would change (an inline `hubs = [...]`, a file that does not parse). `paths.NodeHubsDir`, `NodeHubDir`.
- **Codec** (`internal/proto`). `Options.Workers` (requests served by several workers; default one, in order as before), `Conn.Claim` (atomic once-per-connection guard, now used by `events.subscribe`), `DecodeResult` accepts any JSON value (arrays) but still refuses unknown fields, trailing data and `null`. `proto/hub.go`: `HubProtocol`, `hub.hello`/`hub.role`/`hubs.status` and their types.
- **CLI** (`internal/cli/hubcmds.go`). `devc hub init [--public-name]`, `devc hub ca issue machine|device|server <name> [--out dir] [--legacy-p12]` (files `<name>.crt` 0644, `.key` and `.p12` 0600, never overwritten, then recorded in the registry), `devc hub ca ls [--json]`, `devc hub ca revoke <name|fingerprint prefix ≥ 8> [--role]`, `devc hub serve --listen … [--tls-cert --tls-key | --behind-proxy] [--public-name] [--log-level]` (from `/`, umask 077, SIGTERM/SIGINT), all with `--hub-dir`; `devc hub status [--json]` (machine side, exit 1 without a daemon); `devc join <url> --cert --key [--ca]` (checks the pair, the expiry and that it is a machine certificate), `devc leave <url>`. Config validation: hub URLs without query or fragment, no duplicates.
- **Documentation.** `docs/hub-deployment.md` (both modes, Caddy and nginx configurations, a systemd unit, joining, what a hub can do, the API and the stream); guide section "Hubs"; `examples/config/config.toml` comment; the guide no longer lists `grants.ssh` and `[[hubs]]` as without effect.
- **Tests.** Unit: `hub` (`TestInitAndIssue`: files and modes, CA, server certificate for the public name, client certificates for both roles with usages, PKCS#12 modern and legacy, wrong passphrase; `TestRegistry`: records, expiry, revoke by name and role or fingerprint prefix, generation; `TestSectionOf` (20 cases), `TestBuildInbox`, `TestTailBounds` (count, bytes, an oversized event, paging); `TestProxyFront` (24 cases: loopback v4/v6, non-loopback, missing/garbage/doubled header, another CA, revoked, roles, Host, unknown paths, JSON-only, Origin, Sec-Fetch-Site, body limit, unknown parameters and types, headers); `TestListenModes`, `TestSplitPublicName`; `TestMachineLink` against a fake machine: replay, boxes, live events, duplicates, a gap read again, inbox, command forwarding, a method the machine does not offer, the stream (ready, live event, resume with Last-Event-ID, filter), offline (stale, command refused), reconnection from the stored cursor, a missed session removed, hostile events dropped, tail bounds and paging from the machine); `daemon` (`TestHubAllowlist`, `TestHubOrigin` against a fake TLS hub: hello, notifier role, methods outside the allowlist, info without directories, sync ignored, unknown parameters, high-risk refused, declared local refused, low risk decided as hub, one subscription, concurrency behind a waiting `command.get`, `hubs.status`; `TestHubRoles`: two hubs, the role moving when the first goes, a hub without credentials); `hubnode` (`TestNormalizeURL`, `TestCredentials`, `TestConfigEdits`); `wsconn` (`TestFrames`, `TestBinaryAndOversized`, `TestCallsBothWays`); `proto` (`TestWorkersAndClaim`, `TestDecodeResult`, `TestHubHello`); `cli` (`TestHubUsage`, `TestHubCommands`). Integration (`test/integration/hub_test.go`, helper `itest/hub.go`): `TestHub` (items 1–8, with hub A in direct TLS on a free loopback port, a box with `fakeosc`, the daemon joined; item 7 also through a hub of the test's own that calls what a real hub never calls; item 5 with a second hub B, then A restarted takes the role back, `devc leave` B), `TestHubBehindProxy` (item 9).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **"A server certificate request helper".** `devc hub init --public-name <name>` writes the server key, a CSR for a public CA (`server/server.csr`) and a server certificate from the devc CA (`server/server.crt`, the default of direct TLS); `devc hub ca issue server <name>` replaces them. Machines accept the devc certificate with `devc join --ca`.
2. **The proxy header** may be URL-encoded PEM (nginx `$ssl_client_escaped_cert`, as the work order says) or base64 DER: Caddy cannot put PEM (with newlines) in a header and offers `certificate_der_base64`.
3. **The registry decides twice in direct mode:** at the TLS handshake (`VerifyConnection`), so a revoked or unknown certificate gets no HTTP at all (item 6 "fails"), and on every request (keep-alive connections). Long-lived connections are closed when the registry changes.
4. **The hub list stays in `config.toml`** (rule 1); `devc join` appends a `[[hubs]]` block and `devc leave` removes it, both verified by decoding the result. Credentials without a listed URL are ignored; a listed URL without credentials is reported (`no-credentials`).
5. **What a hub may call, beyond the allowlist:** `daemon.info` without the configuration and state directories and the PID; `sessions.list` without `sync` (no ingestion or reconciliation on a hub's behalf); `session.start` is allowed with harness arguments (they run in the box). New local method `hubs.status` for `devc hub status` (not callable by a hub).
