6. **Codec additions:** `Workers` (the hub link serves 8 calls at once, so a `command.get` with a wait does not hold up the rest), `Claim`, and `DecodeResult` accepting arrays (`boxes.list` returns one).
7. **Inbox details.** Precedence is applied literally: a working session with an earlier done turn not yet seen is `finished`. Ended sessions stay while a turn is not marked seen (`turn > seen_turn`): `failed` when ended as lost, else `finished`; an ended session with a pending request is `needs-you`; an ended session without turns never shows (it cannot be marked seen).
8. **Stream design.** Inbox changes are per-session `session` messages carrying the summary and its section (plus machine and request messages), not whole inbox snapshots; clients apply them to the snapshot from `GET /api/inbox` (its `stream_id` is where to resume). The change log keeps 20,000 entries; older IDs get `reset`.
9. **Session detail.** Without `before` the events come from the hub's tail only (with `more`); the machine is asked only when paging backwards, so viewing a session does not query the machine every time.
10. **Requests on the hub are the pending ones** (`requests.list` without `all`), refreshed after caught-up, every 30 s, on request events and after a decision.
11. **The hub's window** is 30 days: sessions active within it are subscribed to and listed; sessions that ended before it are pruned hourly; at most 20,000 sessions per machine.
12. **Extras:** `--hub-dir` beside `DEVC_HUB_DIR`; `devc join` refuses device certificates; the daemon pings each hub every 30 s (the hub closes a link after 90 s without anything, heartbeats come every 15 s).
13. **Dependencies:** `github.com/coder/websocket` v1.8.15 and `modernc.org/sqlite` v1.62.0 (approved list; sqlite brings `modernc.org/libc`, `mathutil`, `memory`, `dustin/go-humanize`, `google/uuid`, `mattn/go-isatty`, `ncruces/go-strftime`, `remyoudompheng/bigfft`), `software.sslmate.com/src/go-pkcs12` v0.7.4 (new: PKCS#12 bundles for devices, as the work order asks; it needs `golang.org/x/crypto`, raised to v0.58.0 from the v0.11.0 it asks for). The build stays static (`CGO_ENABLED=0`); `bin/devc` is about 19 MB.
14. The C10a leftover "Hub decisions … the hub transport must use the same `requests.decide` path with its own origin" is done: `hubHandlers` uses `decideHandler(proto.OriginHub)`.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Hub: `export DEVC_HUB_DIR=/tmp/h; bin/devc hub init --public-name 127.0.0.1:18443; bin/devc hub ca issue machine m1 --out /tmp/c; bin/devc hub ca issue device phone --out /tmp/c; bin/devc hub serve --listen 127.0.0.1:18443`. Machine (other terminal, with a configuration): `bin/devc join https://127.0.0.1:18443 --cert /tmp/c/m1.crt --key /tmp/c/m1.key --ca /tmp/h/ca/ca.crt; bin/devc daemon; bin/devc hub status`. API: `C="--cacert /tmp/h/ca/ca.crt --cert /tmp/c/phone.crt --key /tmp/c/phone.key"; curl $C https://127.0.0.1:18443/api/inbox; curl -N $C 'https://127.0.0.1:18443/api/stream?session=m1/<sid>'; curl $C -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"machine":"m1","type":"send","id":"<uuid>","params":{"session":"<sid>","text":"say hi"},"wait":30}' https://127.0.0.1:18443/api/commands`. Revocation: `bin/devc hub ca revoke phone`. Behind a proxy: `bin/devc hub serve --behind-proxy --listen 127.0.0.1:8443 --public-name hub.example.org` and a configuration from `docs/hub-deployment.md`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/hub/ ./internal/hubnode/ ./internal/wsconn/ ./internal/proto/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestHub' -v`.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes; `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean; `internal/hub`, `wsconn`, `proto`, `hubnode` and the hub tests of `internal/daemon` also pass `go test -race` (run by hand in the toolchain image with cgo). `./dev itest -v` passes: 39 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (about 20 minutes; `TestHub` 28–46 s, `TestHubBehindProxy` under 1 s). Measured in `TestHub`: an event reaches a client of `/api/stream` 19–26 ms after it was written to the journal. The last changes (the stream clears its write deadline when it ends; comments) came after the full run; `./dev itest -run TestHub -v` and the unit tests were run again with a fresh build and pass. The proxy configurations of `docs/hub-deployment.md` were checked with nginx 1.28.0 and Caddy 2.10.0 containers in front of a hub behind-proxy, with curl and a machine daemon connecting through each (see LEFTOVER); the two images were removed afterwards. No test containers, images, daemons, hubs or directories were left behind; nothing was written to `~/.local/share/devc-hub`.

### C11b Hub access

**What exists.**

- **The front** (`internal/hub/front.go`, replacing C11a's `ServeHTTP` and `checkUser`). `routeTable()` lists every route with an access class (`accessMachine`; `accessDevice`: login and notification buttons; `accessUser`: device certificate plus a login session bound to it; `accessEnrol`: no certificate) and a cross-site rule (`xsiteRead`: Origin and Sec-Fetch-Site checked when sent, all GETs including the stream; `xsiteStrict`: `X-Devc-Request: 1`, an Origin equal to the hub's and a JSON body; `xsiteToken`: the header, Origin checked when sent; `xsiteOpen`: the enrolment download). `guard` enforces both before any handler. `ServeHTTP`: security headers (CSP `default-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, nosniff, no-store, no-referrer, COOP and CORP same-origin, X-Frame-Options DENY, HSTS in direct TLS), Host check, identity, then **`Server.gate`**, the one place a request without a certificate is handled: only exactly `/enroll/<well-formed token>` while a token is outstanding goes on (to a mux built from the enrolment routes alone); everything else gets 404 and `Connection: close`. Direct TLS now uses `VerifyClientCertIfGiven`, and `VerifyConnection` accepts a handshake without a certificate only while a token is outstanding (the gate does not rely on that). Behind a proxy, an empty certificate header counts as none. Rate limiting (`limiter`): token buckets per address (behind a proxy `X-Real-IP` from the loopback proxy) and per certificate, drained only by failures (bad certificates, gate refusals, missing sessions, failed logins, bad button and enrolment tokens): 30, then one per 2 s; 429 with `Retry-After`. Access log: `msg=request cert= addr= method= path= status=`, token paths shown as `/enroll/-` and `/api/act/-`, no query. Certificates' last use is recorded at most once a minute (`cert_seen`).
- **Login** (`auth.go`, `login.go`). argon2id (RFC 9106 second option: t=3, 64 MiB, p=4, PHC string; at most two hashes at a time), TOTP (RFC 6238, SHA-1, 6 digits, 30 s, one step of skew, a code works once: the last step is stored), 256-bit tokens stored as their SHA-256, constant-time comparisons. `POST /api/login` `{password, totp}` → cookie `__Host-devc-session` (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict, Path=/), bound to the certificate's fingerprint, 30 days of inactivity (last use written once a minute, the cookie renewed then); a cookie presented with another certificate ends that session. Five failures in a row per certificate lock its logins for 15 minutes (kept in the database); every attempt is logged with its outcome. `POST /api/logout`, `GET /api/me`. Event streams end at the next heartbeat when their session is gone.
- **Enrolment** (`enrol.go`). `CreateEnrolment` (single use, at most 15 minutes): for a device the key, certificate and PKCS#12 bundle are made at once and the bundle is kept with the token; `GET /enroll/<token>` downloads it and registers the certificate (HEAD does not use the token). For a machine, `POST /enroll/<token>` `{csr}`: the token is checked before the body is read, the CSR must be ECDSA P-256 with a valid signature, only its public key is used; the hub signs a machine certificate for the token's name (the server loads the CA key for this) and registers it in the same transaction that uses the token. `NewMachineCSR` for the machine side.
- **Notifications** (`notify.go`, `config.go`). `hub.toml` (strict; `public_url` must be https and name the public name, else the hub does not start; `[ntfy] url, topic, token`; a warning when the file is readable by others). The notifier follows the change log on its own goroutine: per batch the latest summary of each session; a key per notifying state (`needs-you/q/<question>`, `needs-you`, `failed`, `finished/<done turn>/<turn>`); a new key publishes when the machine is online and named this hub its notifier and no stream names the session (else it is recorded as not notified); leaving a key clears the message (`PUT /<topic>/<seq>/clear`) and removes its buttons; a question-less needs-you right after an answered question is not news. Messages are JSON-published to the ntfy root with `sequence_id`, title `<project> · <harness>`, the question or the last message cut to 300 characters (at most 3,500 bytes), priority 4 or 3, tags, click `<public_url>/#/s/<machine>/<session>`, http buttons for a question with one or two options plus Open. ntfy calls: 10 s timeout, three attempts (1 s, 2 s) on network errors, 408, 429 and 5xx. States the hub holds at start are not notified; published notifications are recorded (`notifications`) so a restarted hub can clear them. `POST /api/act/<token>`: device certificate, no session; the token (1 hour, single use, bound to machine, session, question and choice) is checked, an offline machine leaves it usable (503), using it uses up the message's other buttons; the answer is sent with a command ID stored with the token and the final receipt awaited (≤ 20 s); a short text answer.
- **Store** (`store_access.go`): tables `cert_seen`, `logins`, `login_failures`, `enrolments`, `actions`, `notifications`; the user in `meta`. `Store.Revoke` also deletes the login sessions of the revoked certificates (so `ca revoke` does too). Hourly pruning of expired sessions, enrolments and action tokens.
- **Doctor** (`doctor.go`): owner and modes of the hub directory, `ca/`, `ca.key`, `ca.crt`, `hub.db`, `server/`, `server.key`, `hub.toml` (nothing writable by group or others; secrets and directories not readable by them), password set, TOTP (advice), public URL https, ntfy `/v1/health`.
- **CLI** (`internal/cli/hubaccess.go`): `devc hub user set-password` (hidden prompt twice, or one line on stdin; at least 12 characters), `devc hub user totp enable|disable`, `devc hub sessions ls [--json] | revoke <id|device> | --all`, `devc hub devices ls|revoke <name>`, `devc hub machines ls|revoke <name>` (last seen, sessions, online), `devc hub enroll machine|device <name> [--ttl] [--legacy-p12]`, `devc hub doctor [--json]` (exit 1 on a failure); `devc join <url> --token <token|-> [--ca]` beside `--cert/--key`.
- **Documentation**: `docs/hub-deployment.md` rewritten (password and TOTP, enrolment, direct TLS and the proxy rules for the enrolment path with new Caddy and nginx configurations, ntfy on a phone without Google services, managing access, the API with login and buttons, curl); guide section "Hubs"; `specs-v2.md` section 13 says what exists (password and TOTP; passkeys planned).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Passkeys deferred** as the work order decides; the spec's wording is adjusted.
2. **The TLS layer is stricter than asked:** besides the HTTP gate, a handshake without a certificate fails while no token is outstanding (item 5's "closed connection"). The gate accepts only the exact enrolment path form, so path cleaning (`/enroll/x/../api/…`) cannot route anywhere.
3. **Buttons and enrolment requests** (`xsiteToken`) require `X-Devc-Request: 1` (the ntfy button sends it as a header) but check `Origin` only when sent: the ntfy app and `devc join` send none, and their single-use token is the credential. Every other state change requires the Origin.
4. **Reads check Origin too:** the work order asks it of the stream; it is applied to every GET route.
5. **The server reads the CA key** to sign a machine's certificate request at enrolment (C11a: the server read only the CA certificate). The alternative, a foreground `enroll` waiting for the request, would not "print a URL" and return.
6. **Device bundles are made by `devc hub enroll device`**, so the passphrase exists only in that terminal; the encrypted bundle waits in the database with the token (removed when used or expired). The certificate is registered at the download, not before.
7. **Token on the command line:** `devc join --token <token>` as the work order writes it (single use, 15 minutes); `--token -` reads it from stdin for those who mind. `--ttl` (≤ 15 minutes) on `enroll` lets tests see expiry.
8. **Lockout:** the fifth failure answers 401 and sets the lock; attempts while locked get 429. Failures are forgotten after 15 minutes without one.
9. **Cookie handling:** name with the `__Host-` prefix; the token's SHA-256 is the database key (lookups by hash leak nothing about the token); the cookie is renewed when the last use is written. `GET /api/me` added for C12.
10. **Notification details the work order leaves open:** keys per state (a new question re-notifies, a finished turn after another too); buttons cut to 40 characters; a suppressed state is not sent later; the notifier role is checked at publish time (a hub that becomes notifier later does not send what happened before).
11. **Schema stays 1:** only tables were added (an older binary still opens the database).
12. **Rate limits** count failures only; logged-in requests are never limited. Failed TLS handshakes in direct mode are not counted.
13. **Proxy configurations changed** (`verify_if_given` / `optional` plus a rule exposing only the enrolment path, `X-Real-IP`). Caddy leaves the certificate placeholder unreplaced without a client certificate, so its enrolment handler removes the header; the hub treats an empty header as none. nginx lists every `proxy_set_header` in each location (no partial inheritance).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Hub: `export DEVC_HUB_DIR=/tmp/h; bin/devc hub init --public-name 127.0.0.1:18443; bin/devc hub user set-password; bin/devc hub ca issue device phone --out /tmp/c` (or `bin/devc hub enroll device phone` and open the URL on the device once `serve` runs); `printf '[ntfy]\nurl = "https://ntfy.example.org"\ntopic = "devc-x"\n' > /tmp/h/hub.toml; bin/devc hub serve --listen 127.0.0.1:18443`. Machine: `bin/devc hub enroll machine m1` on the hub, then `bin/devc join https://127.0.0.1:18443 --token <token> --ca /tmp/h/ca/ca.crt; bin/devc daemon`. API: `C="--cacert /tmp/h/ca/ca.crt --cert /tmp/c/phone.crt --key /tmp/c/phone.key -c jar -b jar"; curl $C -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -H 'X-Devc-Request: 1' -H 'Origin: https://127.0.0.1:18443' -d '{"password":"…"}' https://127.0.0.1:18443/api/login; curl $C https://127.0.0.1:18443/api/inbox`. Access: `bin/devc hub sessions ls; bin/devc hub devices ls; bin/devc hub doctor`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/hub/ ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestHub' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit (`internal/hub/access_test.go` and updates): `TestPasswordHash`, `TestTOTP` (RFC 6238 vectors, skew of one step accepted and two refused, replay, malformed codes), `TestTokens`, `TestLimiter`, `TestLogin` (no session 401, cookie attributes, stored by hash, session bound to its certificate and ended when copied, 30 days of inactivity and renewal, logout, lockout after five failures with another certificate unaffected, TOTP required and single use, no password, the log holds no secret), `TestRevokeEndsSessions`, `TestRateLimit`, `TestEveryRouteNeedsACertificate` (walks `routeTable` in both modes, with and without an outstanding token, with a valid cookie and every header: no route but enrolment is reached; extra paths including `../` tricks), `TestEnrolment` (machine CSR, key never sent, registry, single use, role mismatch, broken CSR, device bundle decoded with its passphrase, HEAD does not use a token, expiry closes the endpoint, no token in the log), `TestDirectTLS` (handshake without a certificate refused without a token, 404 for the API and the enrolment over TLS with one, HSTS), `TestNotificationText`, `TestNotifier` (fake machine and fake ntfy: one publish with title, body, click and buttons after a retried 500, bearer token, the button's header, origin and role checks, exactly the bound answer, single use for both buttons, clear by sequence ID, none while followed, none without the notifier role, a finished turn), `TestActionTokens` (single use, expiry, bound command, cleared messages), `TestStreamEndsWithSession`, `TestDoctor`, `TestConfig`; `TestProxyFront` and `TestMachineLink` log in now. `cli`: `TestHubUsage` (new usage errors), `TestHubAccessCommands` (doctor exit codes, set-password from stdin, TOTP, enroll output, `devc join --token` against an in-process hub in direct TLS, the token twice, `machines`/`devices`/`sessions` ls and revoke). Integration: `TestHubAccess` (items 1–5, 8, 9 against `devc hub serve` in direct TLS: a daemon joined with a token connects; a device's bundle downloaded with the URL alone is used to log in), `TestHub` (C11a's items with login; items 6 and 7: a fakeosc question `ask which colour? | red | blue` produces one ntfy message with the expected title, body, click URL and buttons, the red button answers in the box (`answer: 1`), it and the other button fail afterwards, the message is cleared; nothing published while a stream follows the session; hub B, not the notifier, publishes nothing while A tells of the finished turn), `TestHubBehindProxy` (login, the gate, the enrolment download without the header).

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (31 packages); `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean; `internal/hub` also passes `go test -race` (run by hand in the toolchain image with cgo). `./dev itest -v` passes: 40 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (`TestHub` 34 s, `TestHubAccess` 6 s, `TestHubBehindProxy` 1 s). The proxy configurations of `docs/hub-deployment.md` were checked with nginx 1.28.0 and Caddy 2.10.0 containers (host network, loopback) by a script: 13 checks each pass (see LEFTOVER); the images were removed afterwards. No test containers, images, daemons, hubs or directories were left behind.

### C12 Web app

**What exists.**

- **The app** (`web/`, embedded by package `web` with `go:embed`; no build step). `index.html`, `manifest.webmanifest` (installable; SVG, 192/512 PNG and maskable icons rendered from `icons/*.svg` with headless Chrome), `sw.js` (caches the app shell only, network first for the app's files, cache first for the versioned vendor files; never touches `/api/`), and ES modules in `app/`: `lib.js` (Preact, hooks, htm), `api.js` (fetch with `X-Devc-Request`, commands with a client UUID reused on retry after network errors, 502 and 504), `store.js` (state, local-storage copy of machines, sessions and requests shown while offline, toasts, receipts, outbox of sent prompts, `markSeen`), `stream.js` (one `EventSource` resuming from the last change ID, backoff to 60 s, reopened when the open session changes, closed after 30 s hidden so a background tab does not hold back ntfy, reopened when visible; `reset` reads the snapshot again), `ui.js`, `inbox.js`, `session.js` (timeline with tool runs folded, question card, request cards, composer with Send/Steer/Stop), `terminal.js` (xterm.js loaded on demand), `changes.js`, `projects.js`, `newsession.js`, `machines.js`, `login.js` (password, TOTP when the hub says so, and explanations for a missing, revoked or refused certificate), `main.js` (hash routes: `#/`, `#/s/<machine>/<session>[/terminal|/changes]`, `#/p/<machine>/<realm>/<project>`, `#/projects`, `#/new`, `#/machines`, `#/login`), `cspshim.js`, `app.css` (system fonts, light and dark, 44 px targets, states as icon plus word, sidebar from 900 px). Vendored in `web/vendor/` with versions, URLs, checksums and licences in its README: Preact 10.29.8, htm 3.1.1, xterm.js 6.0.0.
- **Hub** (`internal/hub`): `static.go` serves the embedded files (types per extension, ETag; vendor files cached a year as immutable, the app's own revalidated); routes in `routeTable`: `/` (device certificate, `xsiteOpen`), `/app/`, `/vendor/`, `/icons/`, `/sw.js`, `/manifest.webmanifest` (device certificate, `xsiteRead`), and `GET /api/projects`, `GET /api/changes/{machine}/{session}`, `GET /api/terminal/{machine}/{session}` (login, `xsiteRead`). `web.go`: projects (all machines or one, asked concurrently; offline machines listed) and changes, both validated like everything from a machine (invalid entries dropped, texts made printable, sizes bounded). `terminal.go`: the WebSocket relay (hub-chosen stream ID registered before `terminal.open`, so no output races the sink; output as binary frames; `open`, `size`, `ping`, `end` messages; input only in control mode; ends with the machine's end, the link, the page, 30 minutes hidden, the login session, revocation; a sink queue of 512 that a slow browser overflows ends only its stream). `hub.toml` `[terminal] control` (default false); `GET /api/me` gains `terminal_control` (and `dev`); a 401 without login says whether TOTP is on. `serveLink` split from `handleMachine` so machines can come over a pipe.
- **Development mode** (`devmode.go`, `devmachine.go`, `devterm.go`; `devc hub serve --dev-no-auth`, hidden): needs `DEVC_DEV=1` and a loopback address; plain HTTP; Host check kept; no certificates or login (one built-in login session); database in a temporary directory removed at stop. Three invented machines speak the daemon's side of the machine link over `net.Pipe` to the real link code: sessions in every section (question with options, high- and low-risk requests, error, lost, finished unseen, working, idle), an offline machine last seen three hours ago, projects with worktrees, a diff, and a TUI-like terminal (24-bit colours, box drawing) whose prompt line echoes input in control mode; prompts, answers, interrupts, stops, starts and low-risk decisions take effect.
- **Daemon** (`internal/daemon`): `web.go`: `projects.list` (configured projects, box state, default branch, harnesses with `ready` = launch description present, worktrees from `devc box worktree list` in the box, remembered with their time and asked again after 20 s, live and needs-you counts per project and worktree) and `changes.get` (session → worktree → branch from the box's list or devc's convention; `gitx.Changes` on the relay). `terminal.go`: `terminal.open {stream, session, mode}` (session live, box running, window asked from the box's agent; window size read with `tmux display-message`; a pseudo-terminal of that size; `podman exec -i -t` of `tmux.Observe` with it as controlling terminal; output as `terminal.data` notifications of up to 32 KiB; size followed every 5 s with `terminal.size`; `terminal.end` with the reason), `terminal.input` (control mode, ≤ 4 KiB), `terminal.close`; at most 4 streams per connection and 16 in all; ending kills the grouped session in the box, then the exec. `[terminal] control` in `config.toml` (default false; in `examples/config/config.toml`). `session.start` takes `new_worktree`: the worktree is created with the box lock held as `devc wt new` does. All five methods are in `hubMethods`.
- **Other packages**: `proto/web.go` (methods, notifications, types, limits); `gitx/changes.go` (`Changes`, `ParseNumstat`: numstat `-z` with renames and binary files, diff cut at 384 KiB on a line boundary, both made printable; only the relay is read); `tmux.Observe`, `KillSession`, `WindowSize`, `ParseWindowSize`; `execx.Cmd.Ctty` and `engine.ExecOptions` `Stdout`/`Stderr`/`Ctty`; `ptywrap.OpenPTY`, `SetSize`; CLI flag `--dev-no-auth`.
- **Checks**: `web/check.sh` (bash with a small DevTools-protocol client in Python's standard library): starts the development mode and headless Chrome, opens nine screens at 390×844 (touch) and 1280×800 in light and dark, waits for each screen's content and a live event stream, saves screenshots and the DOM, answers a question, sends a prompt, expands a tool run, takes control of the terminal and types into it, and fails on any exception, console error or CSP violation (it first provokes a violation to prove it would see one). With `--url` and `--session` it checks a running hub instead; `TestWebApp` uses that.
- **Documentation**: guide section "The web app"; `docs/hub-deployment.md` (the hub's powers, the three endpoints, the terminal protocol, `[terminal] control`, the app's files).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Five daemon methods instead of three.** A terminal stream needs input and an explicit end: `terminal.input` and `terminal.close` sit beside `terminal.open` on the allowlist and act only on streams the same connection opened; output, size changes and the end are notifications. The stream ID is chosen by the hub (32 hex digits) so that the sink exists before any output arrives.
2. **`session.start` gained `new_worktree`** for the new-session screen's "a new name"; without it the daemon would start the harness in the project directory with a worktree name that does not exist. The daemon creates the worktree under the box lock (`gitx.WorktreeNew`, branch `<branch_prefix><name>`).
3. **Snapshot scope** compares the latest snapshot with the commit it was made on (its parent), not literally with the branch tip: when the branch moved after the snapshot, a diff against the tip would mix in the reverse of the new commits. Such a snapshot is marked `stale` with a note. The branch scope compares from the merge base with `github/<default>` (or the relay's default branch when there is no upstream).
4. **Control mode keeps `ignore-size`** (the phone never resizes the pane, also when typing) and removes the prefix key from the observer's grouped session, so keys reach the pane and cannot switch windows; the observer's session has no status line, so its size is the window's.
5. **xterm.js under the CSP** through two shims (`<style>` elements become constructable stylesheets; `setAttribute('style')` becomes a CSSOM assignment) instead of relaxing the policy. The check script proves that no violation is logged.
6. **The 30-minute rule** is enforced by the hub from the page's visibility messages (browsers throttle timers in hidden tabs). Independently, the app closes its event stream 30 s after the page is hidden, because a stream that names a session suppresses its notifications.
7. **Static files need a device certificate but no login** (the login screen is part of the app); `/` may be opened from another site's link (a web ntfy client), its parts not.
8. **Stop is interrupt** (the turn, as Escape); ending the session is in the session's ⋯ menu with a confirmation. When a question is pending the composer folds to Stop and "Write a message instead…" so the question card fits a phone.
9. **Marking seen**: opening a session marks its finished turns seen (`done_turn` for a live session, so a turn still running is not marked; `turn` for an ended one), once per turn, only while the page is visible.
10. **Preact 10.29.8** rather than 11.x (released weeks ago); its hooks module has one edited import (no import maps under the CSP). The manifest link has `crossorigin="use-credentials"` (otherwise the browser fetches it without the client certificate).
11. **The development mode** plays real machines over pipes through the hub's own link code instead of seeding the database, so the app sees what a real hub shows and commands can be tried.
12. **The browser check against a real hub** (not asked for) runs inside `TestWebApp` when a Chrome is found (`DEVC_ITEST_BROWSER=0` skips it, `DEVC_ITEST_WEB_OUT` keeps the screenshots): a loopback reverse proxy presents the device certificate and the login cookie.
13. **HTTP/1.1 stays** for direct TLS (C11a's open question): the app needs one event stream and at most one terminal socket.
14. **Project screen** lists sessions, worktrees and requests as the work order says; the spec's ports and data have no API yet.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. UI work: `DEVC_DEV=1 bin/devc hub serve --dev-no-auth --listen 127.0.0.1:8090`, open `http://127.0.0.1:8090/`. Checks: `web/check.sh [dir]` (screenshots and DOM in `dir`). With a real hub (see C11b), open `https://<public name>/` in a browser holding the device bundle; for the terminal's control mode put `[terminal]\ncontrol = true` in `hub.toml` and in the machine's `config.toml`. API: `curl $C https://127.0.0.1:18443/api/projects`, `curl $C 'https://127.0.0.1:18443/api/changes/m1/<sid>?scope=branch'`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/hub/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/gitx/ ./internal/tmux/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestWebApp' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit: `hub` (`TestStaticFiles`: every embedded file served with its type, the security headers, ETag/304 and the cache lifetime, `/` openable cross-site but not its parts, unknown files 404, machines and certificate-less requests refused; `TestAppReferences`: `index.html`, the app's imports and the service worker's shell name only served files and the shell lists them all; `TestNoInlineCode`: no inline script or style, no handler attributes, no HTML from strings, no eval in the app; `TestWebRoutes`; `TestWebEndpoints` against a fake machine: projects validated and filtered, changes forwarded and cleaned, a reply for another scope refused, the terminal relay (open, output, other streams' data dropped, size, input ending an observing stream, the machine's stream closed, control refused without `hub.toml`, input forwarded in control mode, 30 minutes hidden, the machine's end, a full sink); `TestDevMode`: refused without `DEVC_DEV=1` or off loopback, every inbox section filled, an offline machine, Host check, `/machine` refused, directory removed); `daemon` (`TestWebMethods`: projects.list with harness readiness and filter, the refusals of changes.get and the terminal methods, control off and on, `new_worktree` for main refused; the allowlist); `gitx` (`TestParseNumstat`, `TestChanges` on a real relay filled by banks: nothing banked, snapshot and branch scopes, a stale snapshot, every diff with `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`, host git only against the relay); `tmux` (`TestObserve`). Integration: `TestWebApp` (the app served; projects; a session started from the hub in a new worktree; the terminal observing the pane at its size, input refused, control refused by the hub and then by the machine, typing in control mode reaching the harness as a prompt; changes of banked uncommitted work and of the branch against `github/main`; web/check.sh in headless Chrome through a loopback proxy: 24 screens, the question answered by tapping an option, a prompt sent from the composer and its reply seen in the timeline and on the terminal, no console error or CSP violation; the terminal ending with the session; no tmux client left in the box).

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes; `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 41 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (`TestHub` 33 s, `TestHubAccess` 6 s, `TestHubBehindProxy` 1 s, `TestWebApp` 78 s with the browser step). `web/check.sh` passes: 36 screens (nine at 390×844 and 1280×800, light and dark) and the actions, without errors or policy violations; the screenshots were looked at and the layout fixed where it was cramped (filters, the docked composer, the sticky header). No hub, daemon, browser, container or directory was left behind.

### C12r1 Fixes after review C, part 1 (daemon and box boundary)

All twenty findings of `docs/dev/reviews/C1-review.md` are resolved as the work order's decisions table directs; `docs/dev/reviews/C1-response.md` has one paragraph per ID with its tests. What changed:

- **Session identity (C1-01).** `session.Store.Claim` (`internal/session/owner.go`, new) records the owner of each session ID in `durable/session-owners/<id>` (exclusive create; journals from before: the earliest first event wins). Ingestion refuses a spool file of another project's session: nothing reaches a journal, `spool-offsets.json` keeps `"refused": "<owner>"`, `IngestReport.Refused` reports it once (daemon log and warnings, `devc ps`). `AppendHost` never starts a journal of another project's session (it may add to a journal the project already has, so reconciliation can end an old duplicate). The daemon's hub indexes `sessionKey{ref, id}`; `hub.resolve(ref, id)` (bare ID only when unique). Commands carry `Command.Ref` from acceptance on; `execute` never looks an ID up alone; receipts stored without a project are refused at restart. CLI: session arguments accept `<realm>/<project>/<id>`; prefixes resolve to a project that is passed on; `ps --watch` and `events --follow` use keyed cursors; an exact ID in two projects is an error.
- **Capabilities (C1-02).** `box.Capabilities` (`internal/box/capabilities.go`, new): `local/boxes/<realm>/<project>/capabilities.json` (`paths.BoxCapabilitiesFile`) written at the end of every successful start (container ID, `GrantedSecrets` with harness tokens, `grants.ssh`, `grants.ssh_allow`), removed before every start and at down/rm/rollback. `host.secret`, `host.gate` and `devc gate-exec` allow the intersection with the current configuration (`AllowsSecret`, `SSHGrant`: read-only if either is, allow lists intersected). `daemon.GrantedSecrets` now wraps `box.GrantedSecrets`.
- **Transport guard (C1-03, C1-04).** `internal/daemon/muxguard.go`: frame inspector between the box socket and yamux; budgets `muxMaxOpens` 256 and `muxMaxControl` 32 per `muxWindow` 10 s, `muxMaxStreams` 128 open or half-closed; a violation closes the channel, which reports `refused` and reconnects with growing backoff.
- **Relay hooks (C1-05, C1-07, C1-09).** Hooks are `pre-receive` (refs ≤ `gitx.MaxPushRefs` 1000, configuration problems, `CheckPushSize` against `git.relay_quota`, box heads noted in `<report>.heads`), `update` (policy, then `gitx.RecordUpdate`: bank ref, box tip, tag record in one transaction with `core.fsync=reference`, refused when it fails; noted in `<report>.records`), `post-receive` (`gitx.ReportPush`: report and forwarding only). `cmd/devc` dispatches `pre-receive`. `gitx.RecordPush` remains (records then reports) for callers after the fact. `ServeCall` takes `maxInput` (`receive.maxInputSize`); the daemon stops feeding receive-pack after `max_push_size` + 64 MiB. New project keys `git.max_push_size` (default `2g`) and `git.relay_quota` (default `20g`), `config.ParseSize`, `ResolvedGit.MaxPushSize/RelayQuota` (bytes). Git and sshd streams go through `rawStream`: a write deadline (`streamStall` 2 min) and an abort of both directions when the operation or the worker ends, before the box lock is released.
- **Commands (C1-06, C1-13, C1-14, C1-19).** `commander.save` returns errors; `target.begin` runs after the input gate, immediately before the first key: session live, a queued send still at rest (else back to waiting), an answer's question still the pending one (else refused as stale), then `delivering` stored (else nothing typed; retried after `persistRetry` 10 s). Answers are bound to the pending question at acceptance; a hook waiting for another question refuses them, no fallback to the screen. `submit` returns a copy taken before dispatch.
- **Subscriptions (C1-08, C1-15, C1-16, C1-17).** Byte budgets per subscriber (32 MiB) and daemon-wide (256 MiB); polls of one project, and every tenth periodic poll, tail ended sessions too; a window longer than the default loads the cold sessions it reaches; a replay read error sends `missed`.
- **Requests (C1-10, C1-11, C1-18).** `requests.Store` compacts at `CompactAt` 4 MiB (open requests and the `KeepClosed` 200 newest closed), refuses new requests above `HardCap` 16 MiB, `Size`; `Find` and `requests.list` skip unreadable logs. One executor (2 workers, 32 places); an approval without a place is refused as busy and stays pending; pending plus approved count against `maxOutstanding` 100. Status polling expires only after reading the log again under the lock; outcomes replace only an `approved` state (`transitionLocked`).
- **Gate key (C1-12).** `gate.Keys.NewBoxKey` checks `secrets.CheckMemoryFS` before `ssh-keygen` unless `AllowPersistent` (`gate.BoxHooks.AllowPersistent`, from `DEVC_ALLOW_PERSISTENT_SECRETS=1`).
- **Documentation.** Guide: relay limits and record-before-accept, grants that need a restart, session ownership and `<realm>/<project>/<id>`; `examples/config/.../analysis/project.toml` shows the two git keys.

**Changed wire formats (for the hub and web app chunk).** All additions are optional, so the hub as it is keeps working while session IDs are unique:

1. `ref` (`"<realm>/<project>"`, optional) is new in `SendParams`, `AnswerParams`, `SessionCommandParams` (interrupt, stop), `KeysParams`, `SeenParams`, `SessionsGetParams`, `EventsReadParams`, `TerminalOpenParams` and `ChangesParams`. Without it the ID must be unique among the machine's sessions. An ambiguous ID gives `-32602` invalid params for `sessions.get` and `events.read`, a refused receipt for the commands, and not-found for `terminal.open` and `changes.get`; each message says "the session ID … exists in A and B: name the project (ref)". The hub should always send `ref` (it has it from every view and event). Once it does, the daemon could require it on hub connections.
2. `Receipt.ref` is new (the session's project; for `start`, set once the session exists).
3. Cursor keys and `SubscribeParams.sessions` entries may be `"<realm>/<project>/<id>"` (`proto.SessionKey`, `proto.ParseSessionKey`). A bare ID still works and applies to every session with that ID. The hub should key its cursor by project and ID.
4. `SessionsListParams.after` may be a key; pages are ordered by session ID, then project.
5. `MissedNote.ref` is now also set for a cursor key whose session has no journal.
6. `session.answer`: the receipt can now be refused later with "stale answer: …" (the question changed before delivery, or a hook waits for another one).
7. `requests.decide` can answer busy (`-32004`) when the executor is full; the request stays pending. `host.request` can answer busy for a full executor (automatic kinds) or a full log.
8. `host.secret` and `host.gate` refuse boxes without a capability record ("restart it") and grants added after the start.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **The "note" of a refused spool file** is kept in the offending project's ingestion bookkeeping (`spool-offsets.json`), reported once as a warning. The work order says a `note`, but the offending project has no journal for that session, and creating one would recreate the duplicate.
2. **Legacy duplicate journals stay visible.** If two projects already hold journals with one ID, both are indexed (by key). The owner record stops the later one from growing, and bare IDs become ambiguous.
3. **Bare IDs are still accepted from the hub**, not only from the CLI, so the current hub and the integration tests work until the next chunk sends `ref`. With the ownership record, new duplicates cannot arise.
4. **The record is written for every successful start**, also when `up` finishes the start of a running box that never reported ready (that box's definition matches the current one). `up` on a box that is already running does not rewrite it: the box was not started again.
5. **Budget values** (streams, pings, bytes, executor, log sizes) are this chunk's choices; they are constants at the top of their files.
6. **A pushed ref limit of 1000** (was a 10,000-line parse limit in post-receive). Each ref runs one `update` hook process, so the limit also bounds the push's cost.
7. **Box tips** are decided from the box's branches as `pre-receive` sees them (one `git for-each-ref` in the box per push, as before in `post-receive`).
8. **Approved requests left in the queue** when the daemon stops fail at the next start ("not repeated"), like interrupted ones.

**Upgrade notes.** A box started before C12r1 has no capability record: with `delivery = "daemon"` its harnesses get no secrets, and its SSH gate refuses, until it is restarted (`devc down`, `devc up`). Commands accepted by an older daemon and still queued are refused at restart. The daemon must be restarted to use the new hooks (they are relinked at the next push).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; `bin/devc daemon` in one terminal. Ownership: copy a spool file of one project's session into another box's `<runtime>/<box-id>/spool/` and run `bin/devc ps`; the warning names the owner. Commands by project: `bin/devc send demo/hello/<id> "text"`. Capabilities: `[secrets] delivery = "daemon"`, `bin/devc up <ref>`, then add a secret to `grants.secrets`. In the box, `devc box secret NEW` is refused until `bin/devc down <ref>; bin/devc up <ref>`. Relay limits: `[git] relay_quota = "1m"` and push from the box. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/session/ ./internal/requests/ ./internal/gitx/ ./internal/box/ ./internal/gate/ ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestDaemon|TestInput|TestRelayService|TestRequests|TestSecretsDaemon|TestSSHGate|TestHub|TestWebApp' -v`.

**Tests.** New unit tests: `session.TestSessionOwnership`; `proto.TestSessionKeys`; `daemon.TestSubscribeKeys`, `TestSubscribeEndedAndCold`, `TestReplayFailure`, `TestSubscriberByteBudget`, `TestDeliveryChecks`, `TestMuxGuard`, `TestHostileTransport`, `TestRequestExecutor`, `TestRequestLogs`; `requests.TestStoreCompaction`; `box.TestCapabilities`; `gate.TestExecStartedWith`; `cli.TestRelayHooksRecordFirst` (real `git receive-pack` with the test binary as the three hooks). Extended: `daemon.TestCommandsSurviveRestart` (no rebinding at restart, ambiguous bare IDs), `TestRelayStreams` (the box lock is taken, a reader that stops loses the stream and the lock is released), `TestSecrets` and `TestGateStreams` (capability records), `box.TestUpCreatesAndIsIdempotent` (record written and removed), `gate.TestServerPrepare` (no key on persistent storage), `gitx.TestServeCall`, `TestEnsureRelayHooks`.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (31 packages); `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 41 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (21.6 minutes). `DEVC_ITEST_DAEMON=1 ./dev itest -run 'TestSessions|TestInput|TestStatusWrapper|TestRelayService|TestRequests|TestSecretsDaemon|TestSSHGate|TestGit$|TestBankAll|TestEscape|TestHub$' -v` passes (11 tests) with a fresh build. The full run used the binary from before three small last changes (cold sessions are checked only for windows longer than the default; the CLI looks up the project of a full session ID in the journals; host events may be added to an existing journal of a non-owner, so reconciliation can end it); the daemon-mode run and the unit suite cover them. The race detector was not run (CGO is off in `./dev`; see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, hubs, sshd processes or directories were left behind.

### C12r2 Fixes after review C, part 2 (hub and web app)

All seventeen findings of `docs/dev/reviews/C2-review.md` are resolved as the work order's decisions table directs; `docs/dev/reviews/C2-response.md` has one paragraph per ID with its tests. The hub and the web app now follow C12r1's wire changes. What changed:

- **Machine link bounds (C2-01, C2-02; `internal/hub/link.go`).** `listSessions`: at most `MaxMachineSessions` sessions asked for per refresh, pages that advance strictly by (ID, project) with `after` a `ref/id` key, no page larger than asked, no empty page with `more`, `refreshBytes` (64 MiB) of answers, `refreshDeadline` (2 min) for the whole refresh; a broken bound closes the link (`errBound`). `missed` is acted on only for a held session of the same project. Reads again (`sessions.get` with `ref`) go through `resyncQ` (≤ `resyncQueueMax` 256) and `resyncWorkers` (4); `used()` counts held sessions plus outstanding first reads against `MaxMachineSessions`; `finishResync` rechecks capacity and the project; held-back events ≤ `maxHeldTotal` (20,000); a gap that cannot be queued sets `resyncLost`, and the next refresh reads every session behind the machine's list. The subscribe cursor is keyed `ref/id`.
- **Pruning (C2-15).** `Server.pruneSessions`: a connected machine's sessions through its worker (`itemPrune`), an offline machine's with `Store.PruneMachine` under `pruneMu` (which a new link's `load` waits for); both emit `session.removed`. `Store.Prune` is gone.
- **Front (C2-03, C2-04, C2-05).** Login: `loginSlot` (one attempt per certificate, else 429), `hashSlot` (two hashing, `maxHashWaiting` 8 waiting, else 429), the lock checked again before the password. `bodyDeadline`: a read deadline of `bodyTimeout` (10 s) on every request with a body, lifted when the body has been read to its end (`decodeBody` reads to the end and refuses anything but white space after the object); refused when it cannot be set. `anonSem`: at most `maxAnonymous` (64) requests of access classes device and enrolment at once (429). `Identity.Expires` (registry end, or the certificate's if earlier); `addLive` sets a timer that ends machine links, streams and terminals at expiry.
- **Buttons (C2-06).** `POST /api/act` with the token in `X-Devc-Action` (`ActionHeader`); the access log no longer needs to hide a button token. `docs/hub-deployment.md`: no access log (Caddy `log_skip`; nginx `access_log off` and `error_log /dev/null`) for the enrolment path, and why.
- **Reads (C2-08, C2-09).** `Server.readOlder` follows `events.read` (`More`) up to right below the cursor, keeping the newest events within `olderPageBytes` (1 MiB), with `olderReads` (64) and `olderReadBytes` (16 MiB) as bounds. `Store.changesAfter` reports a gap when a page's first ID is not the position + 1; streams send `reset`, the notifier resynchronises.
- **Notifications (C2-13, C2-14; `notify.go`).** Table `notify_states` (machine, session, key → state `pending`/`sent`/`cleared`, seq fixed per record, tries, next attempt, created); rows of the C11b `notifications` table are migrated at open. Pending records are published when the machine names this hub its notifier (re-evaluated on every machine change and every `notifyRetryTick`, 15 s) and the state is still current; failures back off from `notifyRetryBase` (30 s) doubling to 15 min; cleared records are retried until ntfy confirms (given up after 7 days); a state that comes back while its old message is being cleared republishes that message. The change-log position is kept in `meta.notify_pos`; on the first start the states the hub holds are recorded as sent without a message (as before), after a longer absence the projection is compared with the records.
- **API (C2-11 and C12r1).** `rpcStatus` returns whether the outcome is uncertain; `writeRPCError` adds `"uncertain": true` (offline mid-call 503, timeout 504, a machine that could not be asked or sent an invalid receipt 502). A failed wait for the final receipt returns the receipt so far (200). The hub names `ref` on every call about a session: `sessions.get`, `events.read`, `terminal.open`, `changes.get`, the button's answer, and commands (a client's `ref` is validated, a missing one filled from the hub's view, `Server.sessionRef`). Busy from `requests.decide` is 429.
- **Terminal (C2-17).** The hub counts a stream as hidden until the page says it is visible.
- **Web app.** `timeline.js` (new): a window of at most `MAX_EVENTS` (1,000) events per open session with entries built event by event; rows are a Preact class component that redraws only when its entry changes; at most 300 rows drawn until **Show earlier**. `store.js`: `stale()`/`online()` from the current machine map (C2-12); `loadDetail` holds back stream events during a full read and merges them by seq, ignores superseded answers (C2-10); pending commands (`state.pending`, kept in local storage) for unknown outcomes and non-final receipts, resolved from receipts when the machine is online, `retryPending` (same ID), `dismissPending`; stale answers and busy decisions get their own messages. `api.js`: `ApiError.uncertain`; retries only uncertain failures. `stream.js`: a reset stops the stream, reads the snapshot (newest read wins) and reopens from its position. `terminal.js`: visibility on open. `newsession.js`: a start with an unknown outcome is repeated with its ID. `sw.js` lists `timeline.js` (cache `devc-shell-2`). `web/check.sh` has a third action run: a prompt whose four attempts fail on the network (DevTools `Fetch`) is shown as unknown, then as not arrived, and **Send again** delivers it.
- **Daemon test (C2-16).** `TestHubOrigin` queues a send to a working session so `command.get` waits, and checks it is pending while `boxes.list` is answered.
- **Documentation.** `docs/hub-deployment.md` (button header, proxy logging, notification records and hand-over, login and request limits, expiry, uncertain errors, `ref`, backward paging, mid-stream reset, terminal visibility); guide (the timeline window, unknown outcomes and **Send again**).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **The hub still keys its own store by machine and session ID.** It sends `ref` everywhere and keys its cursor by project and ID, and it never mixes events of another project into a session it holds (that was so before). A legacy duplicate ID on one machine (only from before C12r1's ownership record) shows as the first project the hub learned; re-keying the database, the API paths and the app's routes was not needed for new sessions.
2. **Records beyond `MaxMachineSessions` in a refresh are not asked for** rather than treated as a violation: the hub never asks for more than it keeps, so a legitimate machine with more sessions is truncated (logged once) instead of disconnected; a machine that sends more than asked is disconnected.
3. **The request cap covers the enrolment endpoint too** (no certificate at all), not only requests with a certificate and no session.
4. **Non-final receipts are tracked too.** The app keeps every command whose receipt is not final (not only uncertain ones) and asks for its receipt every 10 s while the machine is online, so the user learns the final outcome; seen marks are not tracked.
5. **Sessions a client follows** are recorded as sent without a message (C11b's rule that a suppressed state is not sent later), and so is a question-less needs-you right after an answered question.
6. **Hub and machines are upgraded together.** A daemon from before C12r1 refuses the keyed cursor (and pages `sessions.list` wrongly with a key), so its link fails; nothing else changes for current daemons. The daemon still accepts calls without `ref` from hubs (C12r1's open choice); it could now require it.

**Upgrade notes.** The hub migrates its `notifications` table into `notify_states` at the first open (the schema stays 1; an older binary still opens the database and ignores the new table). After the upgrade the notifier starts from the present (no `notify_pos` yet). Machines must run C12r1 or later.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Hub as in C11b. Buttons: `curl $C -X POST -H 'X-Devc-Request: 1' -H 'X-Devc-Action: <token>' https://hub.example.org/api/act`. Unknown outcomes in the app: `web/check.sh` (third action run) or stop a machine's daemon while a prompt is being sent. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/hub/ -run 'TestRefreshBounds|TestResyncBounds|TestBackwardPaging|TestStreamRetention|TestPruneThroughWorker|TestCertificateExpiry|TestLoginLimits|TestSlowBodies|TestRPCErrors|TestNotifierRetries|TestNotifier$'`; `./dev test ./internal/daemon/ -run TestHubOrigin`; `web/check.sh`; `./dev itest -run 'TestHub|TestWebApp' -v`.

**Tests.** New unit tests: `TestRefreshBounds`, `TestResyncBounds`, `TestBackwardPaging`, `TestStreamRetention`, `TestPruneThroughWorker`, `TestCertificateExpiry` (`link_bounds_test.go`), `TestLoginLimits`, `TestSlowBodies`, `TestRPCErrors` (`front_limits_test.go`), `TestNotifierRetries` (`notify_test.go`). Changed: `TestMachineLink` (cursor keys, `ref` on `sessions.get` and commands; the fake machine pages `sessions.list` as the daemon does), `TestNotifier` (button header, pending at hand-over, an answered question dropped), `TestNotificationText`, `TestEveryRouteNeedsACertificate`, `TestWebEndpoints` (`ref` on `changes.get`, a terminal never said visible), `TestProxyFront` and the access tests (a recorder that accepts a body deadline), `daemon.TestHubOrigin`; integration `TestHub` presses the button with ntfy's URL, method and headers.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (31 packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 41 tests, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (21.5 minutes; `TestHub` 33 s, `TestHubBehindProxy` 1 s, `TestHubAccess` 6 s, `TestWebApp` 76 s with the browser step). That run used the binary from before two small changes to `web/app/store.js` (a seen mark whose outcome is unknown is dropped silently; a successful detail read clears an earlier 404); `web/check.sh` and the web tests of `internal/hub` were run again with a fresh build and pass. `web/check.sh` passes: 36 screens and 3 action runs, no errors or policy violations; the screenshots of the session screen, the inbox and the unconfirmed prompt were looked at. The nginx configuration of `docs/hub-deployment.md` was run in an nginx 1.28.0 container (an enrolment request left no log line, another request was logged), Caddy's validated with Caddy 2.10.0; both images were removed. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, hubs, browsers or directories were left behind.

### C13 Network: allowlist, exceptions, local URLs

The work order's design replaces the plan's gateway container: no gateway, no Podman network of devc's own. `specs-v2.md` section 11 (and its component list and phase 3 line) and `docs/dev/plan.md` section 3.9 (package map, labels, chunk table) now describe it.

**What exists.**

- **Level `allowlist`** (`netpolicy.BoxArgs`): `--network none` plus `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `http_proxy`, `https_proxy` = `http://127.0.0.1:3128`, `NO_PROXY`/`no_proxy` = `127.0.0.1,localhost`, `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` (in the create arguments, so a level change is drift). No rules, no canary (no interface to filter). Box init starts the agent's proxy listener when `box.json` says allowlist (`AgentOptions.Proxy`).
- **Box agent relays** (`internal/boxside/netrelay.go`): every connection to `127.0.0.1:3128` becomes a `host.proxy` stream, unparsed: one request line, then raw bytes with half-closes. `box.listen {ports}` (host to box) makes the agent listen on exactly those loopback ports for host exceptions, each connection a `host.dial {port}` stream; failures (port in use) are answered per port. Without a host, a connection is closed at once. At most `DefaultMaxRelays` (64) relays at once and 512 relay streams per 10 s (half the daemon's open budget), excess closed locally.
- **Proxy** (`internal/netpolicy/proxy.go`, served by the daemon in `internal/daemon/netproxy.go`): `CONNECT host:port` and plain HTTP with absolute `http://` URLs, keep-alive and pipelining (each request decided again; an upstream connection reused only for the same host and port), `Expect: 100-continue` answered by the proxy, hop-by-hop headers (also those named by `Connection`, and `Proxy-Authorization`) dropped both ways, requests and responses re-framed by net/http, header limit 64 KiB (431), header timeout 30 s, keep-alive 2 min, idle 10 min (1 min for the remaining direction once one has ended). Decision: `NormalizeHost` (IP literals in any form refused, also inet_aton-style numeric last labels), the allowlist (`Allow`) or an exception's name (`Exceptions`), ports 80/443 unless the entry names one; the daemon resolves (IPv4 only), drops private-range, non-IPv4 and host addresses (`CheckAddr`; host addresses cached 10 s), dials one of the rest with a `net.Dialer.Control` that runs `CheckAddr` on the socket's address again. Refusals are `403`/`400`/`502` with a one-line reason and the hint `devc request net <name>:<port>`. Every decision goes to `durable/realms/<realm>/netlog/<project>.jsonl` (`netpolicy.NetLog`: printable text, 0600, rotated to `.1` at 4 MiB).
- **Policy source.** The configured allowlist is a start capability: `box.Capabilities` gained `network` and `allowlist` (canonical entries); `Capabilities.ProxyAllowlist(r)` requires level allowlist in the record and in the configuration and returns the entries in both. Exceptions come from their own record and apply at once. Both are read for every request.
- **Configuration.** `[network.allowlist] domains = […]`, `presets = […]` in realm and project, merged (`Resolved.NetAllowlist`, realm first, no duplicates), validated with `netpolicy.ParseEntry` (names, `*.suffix` with at least two labels, optional `:port`) and `netpolicy.Presets` (`presets.go`: anthropic, openai, github, pypi, npm, cran, debian; explicit names only). `[router] listen` in `config.toml` (default `127.0.0.1:7480`; loopback only, or `"off"`).
- **Exceptions** (`netpolicy/exceptions.go`, `box/netexceptions.go`, `cli/netallow.go`). Record `local/boxes/<realm>/<project>/net-exceptions.json` (0600, strict, validated, at most 256, expired ones dropped on write): ID `x` + 6 hex, kind `addr` (host as given, port, IPv4 addresses resolved at grant time), `lan`, `host` (port), created, until (absent = forever), origin `cli` or `request <id>`. `devc net allow <target> [ref] [--for 2h|forever]` (1 s to 30 days), under the box lock; the level is the running box's (from its capability record) or the configured one: `lan` only at internet; `addr` at internet (names resolved now), at allowlist names only, refused at none and open; `host:<port>` at every level, refused without a daemon. Applying: at internet `Manager.SyncExceptions` verifies the binding and rules (`verifyRunning`) and runs `netpolicy.ExceptionScript` through `Installer.ApplyExceptions` (flush `allow4` and `lan4` and add every element in force with its remaining seconds, one nft transaction; rules without `lan4` refuse lan exceptions); host exceptions through the daemon's `net.sync`. An exception that cannot be applied is removed again (fail closed). `devc net ls [ref] [--json]`, `devc net revoke <id>`, `devc net log [ref] [--denied] [-n N] [--json]`. `box.NetExceptionHook` (a start hook, so after the readiness checks and the canary) puts the valid exceptions back at every start; `devc rm` drops the record; down and a recreation keep it.
- **Rules.** `netpolicy.Rules` gained the interval set `lan4` (`flags interval, timeout`) and now rejects all the host's own IPv4 addresses (not only the public ones) before `ip daddr @lan4 accept`, which comes before the private ranges. `Verify` is unchanged, so boxes started with the old rules still verify.
- **Daemon.** First lines of box streams wait in a pool of `maxBoxStreams + maxRelayStreams` (96) places; then a request (or git/gate) needs one of 32 places and a `host.proxy`/`host.dial` stream one of 64 relay places (else `503` for the proxy, a closed stream for a dial). `host.dial` connects to `127.0.0.1:<port>` on the host only while a host exception is in force; relays are tracked and ended when their exception goes. Per connected channel a loop sends `box.listen` at connect and whenever the record's host ports change (checked every 5 s, so expiry closes the listeners). Local API `net.sync {ref}` (not available to hubs) applies at once and returns connection state, ports and warnings. Approved `net` requests run `devc net allow --request <id> --for <d> -- <target> <ref>`.
- **Channel budgets** (`muxguard.go`, their one place): `muxMaxOpens` 256 → 1024 per 10 s, `muxMaxStreams` 128 → 192 (at most 48 MiB of receive windows); the reasoning is in the comment there. yamux `AcceptBacklog` is 96.
- **Router** (`internal/daemon/router.go`): listens on `router.listen` (hidden flag `--router-listen` overrides) when the daemon starts; a failure is a warning. `ParseLocalHost` + `match`: `<port>.<project>.<realm>.localhost[:port]` (realm names have no dots, so the realm is the last label). Routed only for ports in `box.ports` or with a live forward record; else 404 with `box.ports` and `devc forward <port> <ref> --detach`; 502 when the box does not run or nothing answers. `httputil.ReverseProxy` keeps the Host, sets `X-Forwarded-*`, passes WebSocket upgrades; its transport dials through `netpolicy.Forwarder.Dial` (new: a socket pair, one end the `nsenter … devc box dial` process's standard input and output) with the box's namespaces pinned per container generation. At most 128 routed requests at once.
- **Forwards.** Foreground forwards now save a registry record too (`Record.ProcStart`, checked against the process's start time), so the router serves them and `forward ls` lists them; both kinds print the local URL.
- **Other.** `devc ls` has a NETWORK column (level, `+N` exceptions, `FOREVER`) and warns on stderr about each exception without expiry; `--json` adds `network` and `exceptions`. Sandbox description of allowlist names the proxy, the failure mode without the daemon and `devc request net`; internet mentions LAN and host requests. Hidden daemon flags for tests: `--test-resolve <name>=<ipv4>`, `--test-exempt <ipv4>:<port>` (the proxy then logs a warning at start), `--router-listen`. `fakeagent --serve <addr>` (web page and WebSocket echo). Example configuration shows `[network.allowlist]` and `[router]`; test environments set the router to `"off"` (`itest.Env.SetRouter`).
- **Documentation.** Guide: levels, how allowlist works, presets, exceptions, local URLs, requests, what is and is not protected.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Exceptions at level allowlist take names only**, and the proxy still never connects to a private address: a LAN host cannot be reached at that level (use `internet` with a LAN exception, or `host:<port>`). The work order says only that a name is added to the allowlist.
2. **`lan` means 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16 and 100.64/10, never the host itself**: link-local is left out (cloud metadata), and the host's own addresses are rejected before the LAN set, which also keeps the canary meaningful. Reaching the host's LAN address needs an explicit `<address>:<port>` exception (`allow4` comes before that rule).
3. **The configured allowlist needs a restart to grow** (capability of the start, like grants since C12r1); exceptions are the runtime path. Removing an entry applies at once.
4. **No session in the proxy log**: the proxy sees connections, not processes; the field exists and stays empty.
5. **IPv4 only** for the proxy's upstream connections, matching C07's rule list.
6. **The router routes forwards of both kinds**; foreground forwards got a registry record for that. Router address changes need a daemon restart.
7. **Test settings are hidden daemon flags**, not configuration: only the user who starts the daemon sets them; a box cannot.
8. **Agent self-limits** (64 relays, 512 relay streams per 10 s) keep ordinary use below the channel guard, which closes the whole channel; the daemon's own limits stay the hard bound.
9. **First-line pool.** Streams now wait for their first line in a pool of their own (96) instead of holding one of the 32 request places; found when a burst of 65 proxy connections had some closed before they were read.

**Upgrade notes.** Boxes started before C13 keep their old rules (no `lan4`): address and host exceptions work after the daemon restart, `lan` exceptions ask for a restart; their agent does not know `box.listen`, so host exceptions need a restart of the box. A capability record without `network` falls back to the configured level. The daemon must be restarted for the proxy, exceptions and router.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; a project with `[network] level = "allowlist"` and `[network.allowlist] presets = ["pypi"]`; `bin/devc daemon` in another terminal; `bin/devc up <ref>`; in the box `pip download requests`, `bash -c 'exec 3<>/dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/3128; printf "GET http://example.org/ HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: example.org\r\n\r\n" >&3; cat <&3'` (403); `bin/devc net log <ref> --denied`. Exceptions on a box of level internet: `bin/devc net allow <lan-ip>:<port> <ref> --for 30s`, `bin/devc net ls`, `bin/devc net revoke <id>`, `bin/devc net allow host:5432 <ref>`; the sets with `podman unshare nsenter --net=$(podman inspect -f '{{.NetworkSettings.SandboxKey}}' <box>) nft list table inet devc`. Local URLs: `box.ports = [3000]`, a server on `127.0.0.1:3000` in the box, `curl http://3000.<project>.<realm>.localhost:7480/`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/netpolicy/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/box/ ./internal/cli/ ./internal/config/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestNetworkAllowlist|TestNetworkExceptions|TestLocalURLs|TestEscape|TestNetwork|TestForward' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit: `netpolicy` `TestNormalizeHost`, `TestParseEntry`, `TestEntryMatches`, `TestExpandAndIntersect`, `TestNetLog`, `TestParseTarget`, `TestParseFor`, `TestExceptionStore`, `TestExceptionScript` (also applying through a fake engine), `TestProxyPlain`, `TestProxyRefusals` (unlisted, IP literal, private, link-local, host address, loopback, port rule, redirects to private and unlisted names, policy error, unlisted names never resolved), `TestProxyConnect`, `TestProxyDialChecksAddress`, `TestForwarderDial`, `TestRules` and `TestBoxArgs` updated; `boxside` `TestAgentRelays`, `TestRelayLimiter`; `daemon` `TestNetProxy` (through a real channel: no record, allowed, unlisted, added after the start, exception, log, relay places separate from requests, 503 beyond the limit, level changed), `TestNetHostDial`, `TestParseLocalHost`, `TestRouterRefusals`, request tests for the `net` kind; `box` `TestInternetLevel/exceptions` (hook after the canary, sync, readiness undisturbed, down/up, rm), `/allowlist` (capability record, intersection), `TestSpecRejects`; `config` broken cases and the merged allowlist; `cli` `TestNetCommands`, `TestNetUsage`; `harness` `TestNetworkSentence`. Integration (`test/integration/netc13_test.go`): `TestNetworkAllowlist` (items 1, 2, 7: interfaces, variables, plain HTTP, CONNECT, wildcard, git through the proxy, refusals and log, no direct connection to public, host or loopback addresses and no DNS (`allowlistEscapes` in `escape_test.go`), a name exception, a host exception at this level, daemon stopped and started without restarting the box), `TestNetworkExceptions` (items 3, 4, 5: LAN exception with kernel expiry and revoke, `up` still "already running", `lan` does not open the host, host exception and revoke, both after down and up), `TestLocalURLs` (item 6: page, WebSocket echo, 404 for an unlisted port, a detached forward routed and printed, a foreign Host).

**Changed tests of earlier chunks.** `TestMountsAndRealms` and `TestNetworkLevels` expected level allowlist to be refused ("not available yet"); they now check that it creates a box with network mode `none` (and that an unknown level still creates nothing). `daemon.TestRequests`/`TestRequestCommands` expect the `net` kind to run `devc net allow`.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (31 packages and `test/fakeharness`); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 45 tests: 42 passed, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before, and the two obsolete expectations above failed; after updating them, `./dev itest -run 'TestMountsAndRealms|TestNetworkLevels|TestNetworkAllowlist|TestNetworkExceptions|TestLocalURLs' -v` passes with a fresh build (that build also has the load check in `TestNetworkAllowlist`: 60 parallel and 300 sequential proxy connections, all answered, no channel closed; the cap on interim responses; and a clearer message for host exceptions without a connected channel). `TestEscape` passed in the full run, including the new rules. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, forwards or directories were left behind.

### C14 Data sync and manifest

**What exists.**

- **Remotes** (`[data.remotes.<name>]`, schema from C01): `host` may now be empty (a directory on this machine, path absolute); `config.CheckRemote` (used by validation and again by the rsync builder) refuses aliases that are not host aliases, paths starting with `-` or `~`, wildcard characters (`* ? [ \`), control characters and `..`. New global `machine_name` (`Global.Machine()`: the setting, else the host name with other characters replaced by `-`; rule `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,62}$`). Example configuration: a local remote `nas` beside `cluster1`, `machine_name`.
- **rsync command line** (`internal/data/rsync.go`, one function, `RsyncArgs`): always `--recursive --links --times --no-owner --no-group --no-devices --no-specials --partial --relative --protect-args`, operands `<base>/./<path>` (each starts with `/` or `<alias>:`); pull adds `--perms`; push adds `--no-perms --executability --chmod=D0777 --backup --backup-dir=.devc-backup/<stamp>`; dry runs `--dry-run --out-format=%i %l %n`; `--info=progress2` on a terminal; `--rsh=<words>` only when a shell is set (`gate.Client.RsyncShell`: `ssh [-F $DEVC_SSH_CONFIG] [-S <master socket> -o ControlMaster=no]`, words quoted for rsync's own splitter). Never `--delete`, never `--copy-links`. `ParseItems`/`Totals` read dry runs (names made printable).
- **Pull** (`Service.Pull`): plan (remote, paths or the remote's `pull` list, each `boxside.CheckSyncTarget`: inside a zone or a zone, no wildcards or control characters, not `.devc-backup`), a check worker (`pull-in` with `check`: an explicit target that is or lies inside a sealed entry is refused before any transfer), the shell (master when `ssh -O check` answers on the gate socket, else plain ssh), the staging directory `local/staging/<realm>/<project>/<stamp>-<remote>/` (one left by an interrupted pull from the same remote is reused, which is what makes `--partial` useful), a dry run (always: it gives the total for `--max-size`), the preview and the question unless `--yes`, the transfer, the `pull-in` worker with the staging directory read-only at `/staging`, the manifest records, the removal of the staging directory. A failed transfer or move keeps the staging directory and says so.
- **pull-in worker** (`internal/boxside/datasync.go`): layout setup, a dry pass for layout conflicts (a file where the layout has a directory), then a merge: new entries are copied (reflink when possible) owned by the box user with the remote's mode bits minus set-ID bits; an existing regular file or symlink with the same bytes or target is left alone (`same`), otherwise the existing entry (file or whole directory) is moved to one trash entry `<stamp>-pull-<remote>` (reason `pull`) first; sealed entries below a target are never touched and reported when they differ (`sameTree`); special files are skipped. It reports digests of what the pull covered.
- **Push** (`Service.Push`): the `push-stage` worker hard-links the targets into `<project>/.devc-staging/push-<stamp>[-N]/` (`.devc-staging` vault-owned `0711`, created only by devc, a box-made one refused; directories below vault-owned `0755`; regular files `linkat` without following; symlinks new vault-owned links with the same target; special files skipped; stale staging directories older than 7 days removed); digests (and with `--content` content hashes) of the staged tree; the dry run, preview and question; rsync from the staging directory; manifest records; `push-unstage` (always, also on failure) removes the staging directory and `.devc-staging` once empty. Capabilities: `CHOWN FOWNER DAC_OVERRIDE` for staging (FOWNER for hard links of other owners' files), `DAC_OVERRIDE FOWNER` for unstaging.
- **Digests** (`boxside.Digests`): list digest = `sha256:` + SHA-256 over `<type>\0<path>\0<size>\0` for every regular file (`f`) and symlink (`l`, size = length of the target) in byte order of the paths relative to the entry (`.` for an entry that is a file); content hash = the same with the file's SHA-256 (or the link target) instead of the size. Directories are not items. Walked through `safefs.Dir`, never following links; entries that vanish during the walk are left out.
- **Manifest** (`internal/data/manifest.go`): `durable/realms/<realm>/context/data/<project>/manifest.jsonl` (`paths.ManifestFile`), records `{v, time, machine, action, path, files, size, digest, content?, remote?, savepoint?}`; appended through `safefs` beneath the context directory (no symlink followed, 0600) under the context worker's lock (`paths.ContextLockFile`, now shared with `ctxrepo`); read with a 64 MiB cap, strictly, invalid lines skipped. Written by seal (`Seal(…, content)`; when it sealed, or with `--content` also for an entry sealed already), save (path `.`, the savepoint ID; reflink: digest of the copy, restic: of the tree at save time minus input zones and sealed entries, from the setup worker), pull and push. A failure to append is a warning. The CLI commits the realm's context repository after every pull and push (`devc data pull|push …` as message); seal and save records go with the next context commit.
- **Commands.** `devc data pull <remote> [path…] [-p ref] [--dry-run] [--yes] [--max-size 2g]`, `devc data push <remote> [path…] [-p ref] [--dry-run] [--yes] [--content]`, `devc data status [ref] [--json]` (per top-level entry of each zone: files, size, state `new|recorded|changed|absent`, last record, machines and remotes with `*` for records that differ now; the last savepoint record), `devc data verify <path> [ref] [--content] [--json]` (exit 1 when a recorded digest or content hash differs), `devc seal … --content`. pull and push refuse while the project's configuration has problems and take the box lock only for the worker phases.
- **Documentation.** Guide: "Moving data between machines" (remotes, pull, push, the hard-link caveat, the manifest, status and verify, clusters); the protections list; the state layout. Plan 3.8 describes the sync and manifest as built.
- **Tests.** Unit: `boxside` `TestDigests` (by-hand vectors, order independence), `TestSyncTargets`, `TestDataPullIn` (new, replaced, identical, nested, set-ID, outside links, sealed entries, refusals, trash, digests, layout conflict), `TestDataPushStage` (modes, hard links, links, FIFO, stale and parallel stages, unstage, box-made staging directory, staged digests equal direct ones), `TestDataDigestOps` (zones, content, seal, setup and reflink-save digests); `data` `TestRsyncArgs` (every case, no `--delete`, operands, refusals, quoting), `TestParseItems`, `TestManifest` (round trip, refused and skipped records, symlinked file and directory), `TestServicePull` (preview, question, locks, operands never in the project, records, decline, `--max-size`, dry run, interrupted and continued pull, sealed target before any command, bad remotes), `TestServiceSSHRemote` (master, plain ssh, `DEVC_SSH_CONFIG`), `TestServicePush` (source is the staging directory with hard links while rsync runs, backup directory, capabilities, records with content, dry run, failure), `TestStatusAndVerify`, `TestDecodeDigests`; `config` remote and `machine_name` cases, `TestMachineAndRemote`; `gate` `TestRsyncShell`; `safefs` `Link`; `cli` usage errors and `TestSyncOutput` (preview, status table, verification). Integration (`test/integration/datasync_test.go`, `TestDataSync`, about 55 s): items 1–6 against a local-directory remote with an rsync wrapper first in `PATH` that logs every argument list, item 7 as subtest `ssh` (the C10b test sshd; after `devc ssh login` the authorized keys are emptied, so only the master can carry the pull), plus refusal without a terminal, a box-made `.devc-staging`, `verify --content`, the status table, and the manifest in `devc context log`.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **The push staging directory is `<project>/.devc-staging/push-<stamp>/`, not `.devc/staging/…`.** With `keep-id`, the host user is in-box UID 1000, the box user, as far as the kernel is concerned: anything the host's rsync may read, the box may read. `.devc` is vault-owned `0700`, so rsync could not pass through it; opening it to others would let the box enter it, which the escape suite forbids. A vault-owned sibling `0711` with vault-owned `0755` directories below keeps the property that matters: the box can read its own staged data but cannot add, remove, rename or swap anything in the tree rsync walks.
2. **Pushes send no directory permissions** (`--no-perms --chmod=D0777`, `--executability`): the staging directories must be readable by the host and so carry no meaningful mode; sending them would widen permissions on the remote (0700 would arrive as 0755). Files keep their bits masked by the remote's umask; new directories get the remote's defaults.
3. **`--max-size` limits the total of a pull** (from the dry run; default 2g, `0` for none) rather than passing rsync's per-file `--max-size`, which skips larger files silently.
4. **A dry run always precedes the transfer**, also with `--yes` (for `--max-size`); `--yes` only skips showing it and the question. Without a terminal and without `--yes`, the commands refuse after the preview, like `trash empty`.
5. **Interrupted pulls continue**: the staging directory is kept and reused by the next pull from the same remote (the work order removes it after a pull; without reuse `--partial` would have no effect).
6. **Targets** must lie in a zone (work, input or sealed) or be one. "Ownership follows the zone": every pulled file belongs to the box user (input zones have been box-owned since C06); new entries in a sealed zone arrive unsealed. Explicit targets that are or lie inside a sealed entry are refused before the transfer; sealed entries below a target are left alone.
7. **Identical means same bytes** (and type, symlink target); modes and times do not count.
8. **The box lock is held only for the worker phases**, not during a transfer that may take hours.
9. **Manifest entries**: for a target that is a zone, one record per top-level entry it covered; otherwise one for the target path. Pull records describe this machine's copy after the move, push records the staged tree. `data status` also lists recorded top-level entries that are absent here.
10. **Commits**: after pull and push the CLI commits the context repository (a failure is a warning); seal and save records are committed with the next context commit (down, timer, `devc context commit`).
11. **`--protect-args`** is always passed, so the remote shell never parses a path; rsync then expands wildcards itself, which is why paths and remote paths may not contain them. The remote needs rsync 3.0 or later.
12. **`devc seal --content`** stores the content hash also for an entry that is sealed already (the work order asks for `--content` on seal); a plain repeated seal records nothing, as before.
13. The daemon and the hub still name the machine by its host name; only the manifest uses `machine_name`.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; in a project with `[data]`, add `[data.remotes.nas] path = "/tmp/nas"` with `pull`/`push` lists (or an SSH alias as `host`); `bin/devc up <ref>`; create files in the box; `bin/devc data push nas -p <ref>` (preview, question), `ls -la /tmp/nas/.devc-backup`; change files on the remote; `bin/devc data pull nas -p <ref> --dry-run`, then without; `bin/devc data trash ls <ref>`; `bin/devc data status <ref>`; `bin/devc seal results/run1 <ref> --content`; `bin/devc data verify results/run1 <ref> --content`; `cat ~/.local/share/devc/durable/realms/<realm>/context/data/<project>/manifest.jsonl`; `bin/devc context log <realm>`. Over SSH with a master: `bin/devc ssh login <alias>` first. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/data/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/config/ ./internal/gate/ ./internal/safefs/ ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run TestDataSync -v`.
