**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 46 tests: 44 passed, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before, and `TestDataSync` failed at its start because it added a second `machine_name` key to the test `config.toml` (the example configuration had gained the key after the test was first run). After the test was fixed to replace the existing line, `./dev itest -run TestDataSync -v` passes (53 s, including the `ssh` subtest). The race detector was not run. No test containers, images, sshd processes, SSH masters, staging directories or runtime directories were left behind.

### C15 GitHub

**What exists.**

- **`internal/github`** (new). `Endpoints` from `DEVC_GITHUB_API` (default `https://api.github.com`) and `DEVC_GITHUB_URL` (new, default `https://github.com`: the web and git host). `ParseRepo(upstream, web)`: https/http (same host and port as the web base), ssh, git and `[user@]host:owner/repo`, with or without `.git`; anything else is `ErrNotGitHub` ("not a GitHub project"). `RemoteURL`, `CredentialScope`. `Client` (bearer token, `X-GitHub-Api-Version`, redirects only to https or the API's own test host, Go drops `Authorization` on cross-host redirects; bodies capped, logs keep their end; the client's credential is scrubbed from every body and error). `PlanRead` validates a `proto.GitHubParams` (operations `pr.list`, `pr.view`, `issue.list`, `issue.view`, `run.list`, `run.view`, `api`; only the fields of the operation; numbers, `state`, `limit` 1–100, branch via `names.CheckRef`; the repository must be the project's or in `grants.github_read`, compared case-insensitively). `ParseAPIPath`: `repos/<owner>/<repo>[/…][?query]`, ASCII without spaces, `%`, `\`, `#`, no empty, `.` or `..` segments. `Client.Read` performs a plan (pull request or issue with comments and run with jobs are returned as `{"pull_request"|"issue"|"run": …, "comments"|"jobs": …}`, each document as received; `run view --log` gathers the jobs' logs, `--failed` only failed jobs, keeping the last 5 MiB with a `[devc: …]` header). `HostToken`: realm secret `github-token`, else `gh auth token --hostname <host>` (from `/`, 15 s, no terminal). `AppJWT` (RS256 with `crypto/rsa`, `iat` −60 s, `exp` +9 min, `iss` the App ID), `ParsePrivateKey` (PKCS #1 and #8). `Minter`: installation looked up with `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/installation` unless configured (cached), `POST /app/installations/<id>/access_tokens` with `repositories: [<repo>]` and the permissions, cached per API/App/installation/repository/permissions until five minutes before expiry, one mint at a time per key. Permission rules (`CheckPermission`: known repository permissions, levels read/write, admin only for `repository_projects`, `workflows` always refused), `DefaultPermissions` (contents and metadata read), `IntersectPermissions`. `CreatePull`, `Comment`.
- **Configuration.** `grants.github`: `relay` (default), `none` (same), `app`, `token:<secret>`; `grants.github_read = ["owner/repo", …]`; `[grants.github_token] permissions = {…}` (only with `app`, at least one entry); realm `[github.app]` with `app_id`, `key_secret`, optional `installation_id` (`app` without it is a problem of the project). `Resolved.GitHubApp`; `Grants.GitHubTokenMode`, `GitHubTokenSecret`, `GitHubPermissions`. Example configuration shows all keys.
- **Capabilities.** The start record holds `github`, `github_permissions` and `github_read`; `Capabilities.GitHubToken(r)` (same mode then and now; App permissions intersected at the lower level) and `GitHubReadRepos(r)`.
- **Daemon.** `host.github` is a raw stream like `host.git`: the daemon validates, checks the budget (120 reads a minute per project), reads the host credential, performs the plan (150 s), answers one `GitHubReply` line (`status`, `text`, `length`, `truncated`, `note`) and then exactly `length` bytes. Extra repositories need the start record of this container; the project's own does not. `host.github.token` (JSON-RPC): token modes only, start record required, 120 a minute; `token:` returns the stored secret, `app` a minted token with `expires`; replies carry `username` (`x-access-token`), `protocol` and `host` of the web base; logs say "GitHub token delivered" with project, mode and repository only. Both reload a changed configuration first. Requests: new kind `comment` (`number`, `body`, high risk); `pr` gained `base` and `draft`; approved `pr` runs `devc pr create --yes --branch=… --title=… --body=… [--base=…] [--draft] -- <ref>` (fails without a branch), `comment` runs `devc pr comment --body=… -- <ref> <n>`. Option `GitHub` (from `devc daemon`'s environment).
- **Box side.** `devc gh pr list|view`, `issue list|view`, `run list|view`, `api` (in-box only; `--json` raw, otherwise a short readable form made printable; `--repo` for `grants.github_read`; non-2xx answers print GitHub's message and exit 1; the parameters are checked locally first). `devc box git-credential get|store|erase` (hidden): asks `host.github.token`, answers only for the protocol and host the token is for, prints `password_expiry_utc` for minted tokens, answers `quit=1` with a message on a refusal; store and erase do nothing. Launch descriptions carry `github_token` in the token modes; the launcher asks the daemon at every start and exports `GH_TOKEN` (or says why it starts without one). `devc request pr` defaults `--branch` to the current directory's branch and takes `--base`, `--draft`; new `devc request comment <n> --body <text>`.
- **Host CLI.** `devc pr create [ref] --branch <b> [--title] [--body | --body-file f|-] [--base] [--draft] [--yes]`: refuses on configuration problems, gets the host credential, banks and pushes the branch with `gitx.Service.Push` (preview and question unless `--yes`), then opens the pull request (title: the branch's newest commit subject; base: `default_branch`, else the relay's HEAD, else `main`) and prints its URL. `devc pr comment [ref] <n> --body <b> | --body-file f`. Start hook `githubHook` and `devc project init`: in the token modes the box repository gets `remote.github.url` (the upstream's URL on the web host) and `credential.<scope>.helper` = "" followed by `/opt/devc/bin/devc box git-credential` (`gitx.Service.ConfigureGitHub`); in relay mode exactly those entries are removed again (the user's own remote or helpers stay; refs stay).
- **Sandbox description.** For projects with an upstream or a token mode: the `devc gh` commands, whether the real `gh` has a credential (and that a minted token expires within an hour), `git push github` in token modes, how to ask for a pull request or a comment. The requests line lists `comment`.
- **Documentation.** Guide: section "GitHub" (host credential, reading, `devc pr`, requests, token modes with the hour limit, `[github.app]`, permissions, the trade-off and rulesets, App setup steps for the user); requests list; protections. Plan package map lists `internal/github`. The web app describes `comment` requests.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **`DEVC_GITHUB_URL`** is new (the work order names only `DEVC_GITHUB_API`): the web and git base decides which upstream URLs are GitHub's, what the box's remote `github` points at and which URL the credential helper is registered for. Tests point it at the fake git server; default `https://github.com`.
2. **The facade answers on a raw stream** (response line, then the body) because channel lines are capped at 1 MiB and answers may be 5 MiB.
3. **Combined views** (`--comments`, `run view`) wrap GitHub's documents in one JSON object; each part is capped at 2.5 MiB. `run view --log` uses the per-job logs (plain text), not the run's zip archive. `--diff` with `--comments`, and `--failed` without `--log`, are usage errors.
4. **API paths accept no percent-encoding at all** (simplest way to rule out encoded separators); queries with encoded values cannot be expressed.
5. **Budgets and limits** (this chunk's choice): 120 reads and 120 token requests a minute per project, 150 s per read.
6. **`grants.github_read` follows C12r1's rule** (start record and configuration); the project's own repository is served without a record. Token modes need the record and the same mode then and now; `app` permissions are the intersection.
7. **The helper is registered in the box repository's configuration** (`.repo/config`), preceded by an empty value that resets helpers from the box's global configuration; the remote `github` uses the default refspec, so `git fetch github` updates the same `github/*` refs the relay hands in (both mean "the upstream's branches"). Relay mode removes only devc's own entries.
8. **The token always comes from the daemon**, whatever `secrets.delivery` says; a stored `token:` secret is never written to `/run/devc-secrets` (unless also listed in `grants.secrets`).
9. **`grants.github = "none"`** (accepted since C01) is kept as a synonym of `relay`.
10. **`devc pr create` reads the host credential before it pushes**, so a missing credential publishes nothing; without `--title` and with an empty commit subject the branch name is the title.
11. **The `pr` request gained `base` and `draft`**; the in-box command fills the branch, the host refuses a request without one.
12. **Configuration is reloaded** (when changed) before each `host.github` and `host.github.token`, so removing a grant applies to the next request rather than within the 3 s watch interval.
13. The installation is looked up per repository (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/installation`), which covers user and organisation owners alike.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; `bin/devc secret set <realm> github-token` (or `gh auth login`); a project with `upstream = "git@github.com:<owner>/<repo>.git"`; `bin/devc daemon`; in the box `devc gh pr list`, `devc gh run view <id> --log --failed`, `devc gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/commits?per_page=3`; `devc request pr --title …` then `bin/devc requests approve <id> --wait`; on the host `bin/devc pr create <ref> --branch <b>`, `bin/devc pr comment <ref> <n> --body …`. Token modes: `[github.app]` in `realm.toml` and `bin/devc secret set <realm> github-app-key < key.pem`, `grants.github = "app"`, `bin/devc down <ref>; bin/devc up <ref>`; in the box `git push github <branch>`, `echo $GH_TOKEN | cut -c1-4`. Against a fake: `DEVC_GITHUB_API` and `DEVC_GITHUB_URL`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/github/ ./internal/config/ ./internal/box/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/gitx/ ./internal/cli/ ./internal/harness/ ./internal/requests/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestGitHub' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit: `github.TestParseRepo` (HTTPS, SSH, scp form, with and without `.git`, other hosts and ports, test server), `TestEndpoints`, `TestParseAPIPath` (traversal, other repositories, encoded separators and dots, backslashes, fragments, full URLs), `TestPlanRead`, `TestPermissions` (workflows, levels, unknown, intersection, defaults), `TestAppJWT` (verified with the public key; another key fails; PKCS #8; bad PEM), `TestRead` (combined views, diff, logs truncated from the front with a note, failed jobs, redirect, 404 passed on, GET only, credential scrubbed), `TestMinter` (signed JWT, installation lookup once, repository and permission restriction, cache until five minutes before expiry, workflows refused), `TestHostToken` (secret first, `gh` fallback from `/`, neither); `config.TestGitHubGrants` and twelve new broken-configuration cases; `box.TestGitHubCapabilities`; `requests.TestValidate` (comment, base, draft); `daemon.TestGitHubFacade` (a 2 MiB body through the stream, 404, refusals before any request: other repository, traversal, unknown operation, `method`/`headers`/`url` fields, `github_read` without and with the start record, not a GitHub project, budget; credential never logged), `TestGitHubToken` (relay, mode changed after the start, minted with the configured and then the intersected permissions, cached, `token:` mode, another container's record, nothing logged), `TestGitHubRequests` (hub approval refused, local approval runs `devc pr`), `TestRequestCommands`; `boxside.TestGitCredential`, `TestLaunchGitHubToken`; `gitx.TestConfigureGitHub`; `cli.TestGitHubUsage`, `TestPRComment` (fake API: body, 404, not a GitHub project, configuration problems send nothing, no credential), `TestGHFormats`; `harness.TestGitHubSentence`. Integration (`test/integration/github_test.go`, fake API and `git http-backend` on 127.0.0.1, a stub `gh` first in PATH that records any call): `TestGitHub` (items 1, 2, 5: reads of every kind through the box, refusals before any request, `devc pr create` pushes to the bare upstream and posts the expected body, a `pr` request stays pending and runs after `devc requests approve`, a `comment` request, relay mode has no token, helper or remote, and the host credential is in no file or process environment of the box while a session runs, checked against a control), `TestGitHubTokens` (items 3, 4: the box reaches the fake through a host exception; signed JWT with the App ID, one repository and the configured permissions; `git push github` authenticates with the minted token through the helper; `GH_TOKEN` is the cached token; after the refresh point a new token is minted and used; `token:` mode is refused until the box is brought up again and then delivers the stored token; back to relay removes the remote and helper).

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 48 tests, 47 passed and `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (25 minutes; `TestGitHub` 40 s, `TestGitHubTokens` 54 s). That run used the binary from before one last change (the web app describes `comment` requests, `web/app/session.js`); the hub tests were run again afterwards and pass. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, fake servers or directories were left behind.

### C16 Shares, dependency sources, more harnesses

**What exists.**

- **Safe extractor** (`internal/extract`, new). `Tar` (plain, gzip or bzip2, recognised by magic bytes), `Zip`, `File` (either, by content). The target must be a new, empty directory opened without following a link; every directory and file is created beneath its descriptor component by component without following symlinks (`safefs.Dir`), files `O_EXCL` (a repeated name may replace only a regular file), modes 0644/0755 (no set-ID, sticky or owners), directories 0755. Names: no absolute names, `..`, NUL, components over 255 bytes, names over 4096, depth over 256 (left out and reported); `Strip` removes leading components. Symlinks are created last and only when relative and lexically inside; then each is resolved by the kernel with `openat2(RESOLVE_BENEATH)` and removed when it escapes or loops (all checked before any is removed). Hard links, devices, FIFOs and unknown types are left out. `Limits` (entries, bytes per file, bytes in all, bytes read from the decompressed stream) stop with `ErrLimit`; `ShareLimits` 20,000 / 128 MiB / 512 MiB / 1 GiB, `SourceLimits` 200,000 / 512 MiB / 2 GiB / 4 GiB. `Report` counts files, directories, links and bytes and lists up to 100 left-out entries with reasons.
- **Shares** (`internal/shares`, new; `devc share publish|ls|log|rm`). `Prepare`: name (`names.KindShare`; `dep-source` reserved), `--path` (clean, relative, no `..`, no leading `-`, `.` for the root), the relay of `--from` (refused without one), `--ref` a branch (`names.CheckRef`) or 4–64 hex characters, default the relay's HEAD; commit via `rev-parse --verify --quiet <ref>^{commit}`, tree via `<commit>:<path>` and `cat-file -t`; `ls-tree -r -t -l -z` counts entries and bytes against the limits before anything is extracted; comparison with the current version's tree (`diff --stat --summary` and `diff`, Display mode, sanitised, 1 MiB) when that tree is in this relay, else the file list. `Publish` (per-share flock in `local/locks/`): `git archive --format=tar <tree>` streamed through `Host.Serve` into the extractor in `durable/shares/.staging/<name>-*/tree`, renamed to `durable/shares/<name>/<UTC stamp>`, a log line appended to `durable/share-log/<name>.jsonl` (0600; version, time, source project, ref, commit, path, tree, files, bytes, left out, skill), then `current` switched. `Remove` deletes `current`, every version and the log, keeping the empty share directory. CLI: the plan is printed (source, entries, size, file list or stat and diff), confirmation unless `--yes` (refused without a terminal), the result with left-out entries and which projects list the share; `ls [--json]` (version, source, commit, files, skill, versions, users), `log <name> [--json]` (current marked), `rm <name> [--yes]`. All refuse inside a box.
- **Shares in boxes** (`shares.BoxHooks`, contributor and preparer). `/opt/shares` is a read-only mount of `local/boxes/<r>/<p>/shares/`, rendered at every start: `dep-source -> /opt/devc/skills/dep-source`, and per listed share `<name> -> .v/<name>/current` with the mount point `.v/<name>`, where `durable/shares/<name>` is mounted read-only (created empty when not published yet, with a warning at `up`). Configuration: `shares = […]` in `realm.toml` and `project.toml`, resolved as the realm's followed by the project's (`Resolved.Shares`); names validated, no duplicates, `dep-source` refused. `box init` links every entry of `/opt/shares` whose root holds `SKILL.md` into `~/.agents/skills/<name>` and `~/.claude/skills/<name>` and removes its links to shares no longer there (`boxside.LinkSkills`; entries the user made are kept and reported). The sandbox description names `/opt/shares` (and the listed shares) and `devc src`.
- **Built-in skill** `assets/skills/dep-source/SKILL.md` (embedded as `assets.Skills`), written by `Toolbox.Sync` to `toolbox/skills/` (stale files removed).
- **`devc src`** (`internal/depsrc`, new; in-box command `devc src <spec> [--json]`). Specs `r:`, `pypi:` (PEP 503 normalised), `npm:` (scoped names), `github:<owner>/<repo>`, each with `@<version|ref>`; names, versions and refs validated before they become paths. Discovery without a version: R from `DESCRIPTION` in `R_LIBS`, `R_LIBS_USER`, `R_LIBS_SITE`, `Rscript`'s `.libPaths()`, `~/R/*-library/*` and the usual directories (a GitHub remote with a commit, `RemoteType: github` or the older `Github*` fields, becomes `github:<user>/<repo>@<sha>` with `RemoteSubdir`); Python from `*.dist-info/METADATA` or `*.egg-info/PKG-INFO` in `VIRTUAL_ENV`, `CONDA_PREFIX` and `python3`'s `sys.path`; npm from `node_modules/<name>/package.json` upwards from the working directory and `NODE_PATH`. Not installed: the current release (CRAN `PACKAGES`, PyPI `/pypi/<name>/json`, npm `latest`, GitHub `HEAD`). Downloads: CRAN `src/contrib/<pkg>_<v>.tar.gz` then `Archive/<pkg>/…` (MD5 from `PACKAGES` for the current release), PyPI's sdist with its sha256 (a project with only wheels is refused, naming its GitHub repository when listed), the npm tarball with its sha512 integrity (else sha1), GitHub: ref to commit through `/commits/<ref>` (`Accept: application/vnd.github.sha`), then `/tarball/<sha>`. https only, plain http only to the configured base's own host (tests); redirects checked the same way; documents ≤ 64 MiB, archives ≤ 2 GiB; `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` only to api.github.com. Unpacked with `extract.SourceLimits` into `<parent>/.tmp-*/tree`, a single top directory becomes the version, renamed into `/src/<eco>/<name>/<version>/` (a concurrent winner is used). Errors at level `allowlist` name the preset (`cran`, `pypi`, `npm`, `github`) and `devc request net <host>:443`; at level `none` they say there is no network. Base URLs: `DEVC_SRC_CRAN`, `DEVC_SRC_PYPI`, `DEVC_SRC_NPM`, `DEVC_SRC_GITHUB_API`. Cache: `cache/realms/<realm>/src` mounted at `/src` in every box of the realm (`box.BuildSpec`). `internal/depsrc/depsrctest` is a stand-in for the four registries used by the unit and integration tests.
- **Toolbox component `node`** (`internal/toolbox/node.go`): Node.js 24.22.3 (LTS), `.tar.gz` for amd64/arm64 pinned by SHA-256 from the release's `SHASUMS256.txt`, cached by checksum like restic, unpacked with the safe extractor (strip 1, own limits) into `toolbox/node/<version>/`, `current` switched; old versions kept. `devc toolbox install node`, `toolbox ls` shows it. `harness install` and `update` install missing components a manifest needs first.
- **Manifest schema** (`config.Harness`): `install.needs` (validated against `config.ToolboxComponents`, now `node`): the component is mounted read-only at its in-box path for the installer and the check container with its bin directory first in `PATH`, put first in the login container's `PATH`, and named in the launch description as `path` (the concrete version directory, `/opt/devc/node/<v>/bin`), which the launcher prepends to `PATH`; a missing component is a warning at `up` and the harness gets no description. `home.also` (further state directories below `/home/dev`, no nesting with `home.dir` or each other or other harnesses' homes): with strategy `shared` each is mounted from `durable/realms/<r>/harness/<id>/also/<dir>` in boxes and in the login container; the description carries `home.also`. `input.args` (added to the command line only when `input.adapter` is not `paste`). `baseline.files` may be `format = "text"` (rendered, not parsed).
- **Built-in manifests** `pi`, `cursor` (`bin = "cursor-agent"`) and `opencode` (`assets/harness/`), templates `pi-input-extension.ts.tmpl` and `opencode-managed.json.tmpl`. pi: script `https://pi.dev/install.sh` with `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR={dir}/home/.pi/agent`, `needs = ["node"]`, shared home `.pi/agent`, `shared-home` login (`pi auth login`, `pi auth check`), instructions `/etc/pi/devc-instructions.md` passed with `--append-system-prompt`, the extension `/etc/pi/devc-input.ts` passed with `-e` only for `input.adapter = "extension"`. Cursor: `https://cursor.com/install`, shared `~/.cursor` plus `home.also = [".config/cursor"]`, `shared-home` login with `auth.env = "CURSOR_API_KEY"`/`auth.secret = "cursor-api-key"` for the realm override `auth = "api-key"`, `NO_OPEN_BROWSER=1`, no baseline. OpenCode: `https://opencode.ai/v2/install`, private home `.config/opencode`, `auth.method = "none"`, managed `/etc/opencode/opencode.json` naming `/etc/opencode/devc-instructions.md`.
- **pi input adapter** (`extension`, C09's interface). Box agent (`boxside/extension.go`): an in-box client whose first request on `@devc-agent` is `box.input.register {session, harness, version}` is served by the agent itself (never relayed), accepted only when the peer process's initial environment names that session (`/proc/<pid>/environ`), newest registration wins; host methods `box.input.submit {session, text, mode: queue|steer}` (forwarded as `input.submit`, 15 s, the extension's error made printable) and `box.input.extension`. Daemon (`adapter.go`): `extension` sends through it (no input gate: nothing is typed), `steer` for steered and `queue` for queued sends, receipt "through the extension (…)"; without a connected extension it types like paste with a note; interrupts and answers are typed. The extension (TypeScript, rendered into the baseline) connects, registers, retries every 5 s, never keeps pi alive (`unref`), submits with `pi.sendUserMessage` (with `deliverAs` while busy).
- **Fake harness**: accepts and ignores `--append-system-prompt <v>` and `-e <v>` (pi's flags).
- **Documentation**: guide sections "Shares: skills and reference material for boxes" and "Dependency sources: `devc src`", pi/Cursor/OpenCode and the new manifest keys under logins and harness state, the state layout, two protection bullets; example configuration shows `shares`; plan package map.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **A new version is visible at once** (the work order leaves the choice): boxes mount `durable/shares/<name>` whole and `/opt/shares/<name>` is a link to `.v/<name>/current` inside the box, so no remount is needed; `publish` says which projects' boxes see it now, and that a share that just became a skill is linked at the boxes' next start. The price: boxes also see earlier versions below `/opt/shares/.v/<name>/` (documented). A changed `shares` list changes the box definition (drift, `up --recreate`).
2. **Realm shares are added to the project's**, not replaced by them (like the network allowlist); a project cannot drop a realm default.
3. **The share log lives in `durable/share-log/<name>.jsonl`**, beside rather than in `durable/shares/<name>/`: the share directory is mounted into boxes of other realms, which should not see the source project and commits. `share rm` keeps the empty share directory so running boxes keep a valid mount and see a later publish under the same name.
4. **The diff comes from the relay** only when the current version's tree is in the publishing project's relay; otherwise (first version, or published from another project) the new version's file list is shown.
5. **Hostile entries are left out and reported**, not fatal (work order test 2: "is published without anything landing outside"); limits are fatal.
6. **`dep-source` comes from the toolbox** (`/opt/devc/skills/dep-source`, rewritten at every sync) rather than as a published share; it cannot be published over or listed.
7. **`devc src` downloads in Go in the box** (not with pip, npm or R, which could run build steps); the version directory is the registry's canonical version (GitHub: the commit, so a branch ref needs one API request even when the tarball is cached). Without a version and not installed it fetches the current release rather than failing.
8. **`harness install` installs needed components itself** (the work order only says needs puts node on PATH); `devc toolbox install node` also exists.
9. **Opting into pi's extension needs a user copy of the pi manifest** with `input.adapter = "extension"`, as for the other structured adapters (C09); `input.args` keeps the built-in manifest from loading the extension otherwise.
10. **Cursor has no baseline** (no instruction file or managed settings outside the project and its writable home); OpenCode's managed configuration sets only `instructions` (the only key confirmed).

**Upgrade notes.** Every box definition gained the `/src` and `/opt/shares` mounts: boxes created before C16 report drift and need `devc up --recreate` (home, project and state are kept). The toolbox gains `skills/` at the next sync.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Shares: a project with a relay (`bin/devc project add demo/tools --from <url>`), commit and `bin/devc bank demo/tools`, `bin/devc share publish review --from demo/tools --path skills/review`, `shares = ["review"]` in another project (any realm), `bin/devc up --recreate <ref>`, then in the box `ls -l /opt/shares ~/.agents/skills ~/.claude/skills`; publish again and `cat /opt/shares/review/SKILL.md`; `bin/devc share ls`, `share log review`, `share rm review`. Sources (box with network): `bin/devc run <ref> -- devc src pypi:requests`, `devc src github:cran/git2r@0.36.2 --json`. pi: `bin/devc toolbox install node`, `bin/devc harness install pi`, `bin/devc login <realm> pi`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/extract/ ./internal/shares/ ./internal/depsrc/... ./internal/toolbox/ ./internal/harness/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/config/ ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestShares|TestDepSources|TestNodeAndPi' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit: `extract.TestTarNormal`, `TestTarHostile` (absolute and `..` names, a link out then a file through it, an absolute link, a link inside then a file through it, chains that resolve outside, loops, hard links, devices, FIFOs, over-long names, nothing beside the target), `TestTarLimits` (file, total, entries, a bomb of skipped entries), `TestNames`, `TestTargetMustBeEmpty` (a planted link, a symlinked target), `TestGzipAndFile`, `TestZip`; `shares.TestPublish` (real git: uncommitted work not published, unchanged, diff of the second version, commit as ref, log, list, rm), `TestPublishHostile`, `TestPrepareRefusals`, `TestBoxHooks`; `depsrc.TestParseSpec`, `TestDiscover` (DESCRIPTION with remotes, old Github fields, GitHub Enterprise, a lying DESCRIPTION, dist-info and egg-info, node_modules upwards, Rscript and python3 answers), `TestFetch` (all four ecosystems against the stand-in, cache hits, the archive fallback, R from GitHub with subdirectory, wheels only, refused redirect, wrong checksum not cached, hostile archive, allowlist hint); `toolbox.TestInstallNode`, `TestSyncSkills`; `harness.TestNewHarnesses` (mounts, baselines, node missing and present, extension arguments), `TestCursorAPIKey`; `boxside.TestLinkSkills`, `TestExtensionRegistration`, `TestLaunchPath`; `daemon.TestExtensionAdapter` (typed without an extension, steer and queue through it); `config` ten new broken cases, `TestResolvedShares`; `cli.TestShareAndSrcUsage`. Changed: `box` golden files (the `/src` mount), `config.TestExampleLoadsCleanly` (five built-in manifests), the "harness without manifest" case (no longer pi). Integration (`test/integration/c16_test.go`): `TestShares` (items 1, 2, 4), `TestDepSources` (item 3, the box reaching the stand-in through a host exception), `TestNodeAndPi` (item 5, with the real Node.js download); `TestHarnessNetwork` (optional) now also covers pi, Cursor and OpenCode. By hand: the rendered pi extension was imported by Node.js 24.22.3 (type stripping, as pi's loader does) with a stand-in `pi` object and a stand-in agent on the host's `@devc-agent`: it registered with the session, submitted a message while idle without `deliverAs` and a steered one with `deliverAs: "steer"` while busy, and answered an unknown method with "method not found".

**Changed tests of earlier chunks.** `TestBoxLifecycle` lists every mount of a box and now expects `/src` and `/opt/shares`; the escape suite's read-only checks include `/opt/shares` and `/opt/devc/skills/dep-source` (removing, planting a link, writing, unmounting).

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 51 tests: 49 passed, `TestHarnessNetwork` was skipped as before, and `TestBoxLifecycle` failed only because its list of expected mounts lacked the two new ones (about 25 minutes; `TestShares` 28 s, `TestDepSources` 20 s, `TestNodeAndPi` 11 s). After the expectation was updated and the escape suite extended, `./dev itest -run 'TestBoxLifecycle|TestEscape$' -v` passed with a fresh build. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, fake servers or directories were left behind.

### C17 Fleet: archive, export, secrets across machines

**What exists.**

- **`internal/archive`** (new, leaf package shared by machine and hub). Archive paths and `ParsePath`: `files/<rel>[~<gen>]` (generation 0 has no suffix), `bundles/relay/<project>/<seq 8 digits>-<id 16 hex>.bundle`, `bundles/context/…`, `bundles/<repo>/refs.jsonl`; `ValidRel` accepts only the kinds of durable state the archive carries (`journal/<project>/<session>.jsonl`, `requests|savepoints/<project>.jsonl`, `netlog/<project>.jsonl[.1]`, `gate-log.jsonl[.1]`, `harness/<id>/…`), components from `[A-Za-z0-9._+=,@%-]` (no `.`/`..`, `~` reserved for generations), at most 24 components and 1 KiB. `Excluded(patterns, rel)`: the one place credential files are kept out (a match, or a path below a matching directory). Wire types (`PutReply`, `ErrorReply` with `length`/`complete`/`quota`, `Summary`, `Index`/`Entry`, `HashReply`, `SecretsReply`, `RefLine`). `Sealed`: argon2id (t=3, 64 MiB, p=4, 16-byte salt) and XChaCha20-Poly1305 with the realm in the additional data; `Validate` bounds the KDF parameters a bundle may ask for; `DecodeSealed` is strict.
- **Configuration.** Manifest key `home.secret_files` (paths below `/home/dev`, `*`/`?` patterns, no brackets; must lie in the harness's shared state), `Harness.SecretStatePatterns()` maps them to the state directory (`home/…`, `also/<dir>/…`, `<shared sub>/…`). Built-in manifests: Codex `.codex/auth.json`, pi `.pi/agent/auth.json`, Cursor `.config/cursor/auth.json` and `.cursor/auth.json` (unverified); test manifest `fakeshared` `.fakeshared/auth.json`. Realm `[sync] max_file_size` (default `256m`), global `[sync] upload_rate` (default `4m` per second per hub); example configuration shows both.
- **Hub** (`internal/hub/archive.go`). Routes in the route table, all `accessMachine`: `PUT /machine/archive/{realm}/{path...}` (`xsiteToken`), `GET /machine/archive-index`, `GET /machine/archive-index/{machine}/{realm}`, `GET /machine/archive-file/{machine}/{realm}/{path...}` (Range via `ServeContent`, `?sha256=<n>` prefix hash), `PUT|GET /machine/secrets/{realm}`. Storage `DEVC_HUB_DIR/archive/<registry name>/<realm>/<archive path>`; the tree is chosen by the certificate's registry name only. Appends only at the stored length (409 with the length otherwise), `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` creation, fsync before the answer; an empty PUT probes the length. Bundles: sequence `n` needs a complete `n-1`, written to `.part`, made final with `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` only while no other attempt of `n` is complete. Budgets (`hub.toml [archive]`, `ArchiveConfig.Limits`): machine quota 50g, realm quota 20g, 200,000 files per machine, 8 MiB per request (the front lifts the 1 MiB body limit and gives 2 minutes only for archive PUTs), 8 uploads at once (2 per machine), 8 downloads, 2 GiB disk reserve (`statfs`); 507 with `quota: true` and a message naming the quota and `devc hub archive prune`, 429 with `Retry-After`. Usage is counted once per machine and kept current; `prune` touches `archive/.pruned` so a running hub counts again. Secrets bundles below `secrets/<machine>/<realm>/<version>.json`, the newest ten per machine and realm; GET returns the newest across machines (or of `?machine=`). `ArchiveSummaries`, `ArchiveIndex`, `PruneArchive` (files last written before the date; a repository's bundles and ref log only when its newest is older). `InitFrom` (`devc hub init --ca-from <dir>`) and `ImportCert` (`devc hub ca import <cert>`) for a second hub with the same CA.
- **Workers.** `devc box archive-worker` (helper container, `boxside/archiveworker.go`): the realm's `harness/` read-only at `/target`, request on stdin; walks descriptor by descriptor, never follows anything; op `list` prints `ArchiveListing` (files, up to 100 skipped with the count, excluded credential files), op `tar` packs the same files and ends with `.devc-archive-report.json`. Skipped: links, FIFOs, sockets, devices, names outside the character set, files above the cap, more than 200,000 files. `DecodeArchiveListing` validates every path on the host. Context worker gained `bundle` and `restore` (`boxside/contextarchive.go`), exchanging files through `/io`: bundle of branches and tags minus excluded commits (`git bundle create --stdin`), restore fetches `/io/<seq>.bundle` with fsck into `refs/devc/restore/*`, creates missing refs from `refs.json`, and when it created `main` writes main's missing files through safefs. `ctxrepo.New` (shared constructor), `Repo.Bundle`, `Repo.Restore`. gitx: `Relay.ArchiveRefs` (all but `refs/devc/incoming/` and `refs/devc/restore/`), `HasObjects`, `Bundle`, `FetchBundle`, `RestoreRefs` (create-only transaction), `DropRestoreRefs`.
- **Machine side** (`internal/fleet`, new). `Client` (own HTTP transport with the machine certificate; never the machine link). `Uploader.Pass`: per realm with `[sync] hub`, host-written files listed directly; harness files from the shared `HarnessScanner` (archive worker, cached `DefaultScanEvery` 5 min), each read through `safefs` beneath the realm's durable directory, names and exclusion checked again; relays and the context repository via `syncRepo`. Per hub bookkeeping `local/archive/<hub id>/state.json` (+ `bundles/`, an flock): per file generation, bytes sent, inode, mtime, SHA-256 of the first and the last 4 KiB sent. A file that shrank, or whose anchors differ, gets a new generation (at most one per file per hour). 409: a confirmed generation resumes at the hub's length; an unconfirmed one (lost bookkeeping) compares the hub's prefix hash with the local prefix and resumes or starts a new generation. Repositories: a bundle of the objects not reachable from the tips the ref log records, uploaded as pending (kept across restarts), made final, then a ref log line `{bundles, set, del}`; ref changes without new objects are ref log lines only; lost bookkeeping is rebuilt from the hub's index and ref log (`ReplayRefs`). The context worker runs only when the context repository's ref files changed. Rate limit per hub (token bucket, chunks of about a second's worth, 64 KiB to 4 MiB; 413 halves the chunk); 507 pauses that hub's uploads for 10 minutes. `Restorer.Restore` from a `HubSource` or `DirSource`: refuses a realm with durable state (`ExistingState`) unless merge; files: the newest generation, created through `safefs.Dir` without following links, `O_EXCL`, removed again when incomplete; harness files only for known manifests and never credential files; relays: fresh or existing relay, bundles fetched in order, refs created only where missing (conflicts reported), HEAD to `main`; context through the context worker. `Exporter.Export` (tar: `devc-export.json`, host files directly, harness state from the worker's tar stream checked entry by entry, one full bundle and a one-line ref log per repository, the context repository committed first), `ReadExport` (safe extractor, `ImportLimits`). `SealSecrets`/`OpenSecrets` (plaintext `SecretSet`, names and sizes checked).
- **Daemon** (`internal/daemon/archive.go`). One uploader per listed hub beside its link (`hubLink.archiveLoop`, own goroutine), interval `fleet.DefaultInterval` (1 min), state no-credentials when the hub was not joined; local method `archive.status` (`{"wake": true}` starts a pass; not callable by hubs); `hubs.status` carries each hub's archive status. Options `ArchiveInterval`, `ArchiveScanEvery`, `ArchiveWorkers`; hidden flags `--archive-interval`, `--archive-scan`.
- **CLI** (`internal/cli/fleetcmds.go`). `devc sync status [--json] [--now]`; `devc state pull <realm> --from <url> [--machine] [--list] [--merge]`, `devc state export <realm> [--out file|-]` (default `devc-state-<realm>-<time>.tar`, never overwritten), `devc state import <file> [--realm]`; `devc secret push <realm> --to <url>`, `devc secret pull <realm> --from <url> [--machine] [--overwrite]` (passphrase from a hidden prompt, twice for push, or one stdin line; at least 10 characters for push); `devc hub archive ls|du|prune`; `devc hub init --ca-from`, `devc hub ca import`; `devc hub status` has an ARCHIVE column.
- **Documentation.** Guide: "The archive on the hubs", "Secrets on another machine", "Backups" (three options), `home.secret_files` and why, a protection bullet; the obsolete "`[sync]` has no effect" sentence removed. `docs/hub-deployment.md`: what to back up, section 10 (archive endpoints, the ratchet, budgets, `hub archive`, exposure: every enrolled machine can read all archived realm state), section 11 (a second hub: same CA, own database, `ca import`, own ntfy, notifier hand-over). Plan package map.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Generations are named by the machine** (`files/<rel>~<gen>`), so the hub only appends and never needs to know about rewritten files; `~` is outside the archive's character set for components. New generations are rate-limited to one per file per hour, so a database rewritten in a shared home cannot flood the hub (the quota bounds the rest).
2. **Refs travel in an append-only ref log beside the bundles.** `git bundle` leaves out refs whose commits are excluded (a new branch at an archived commit, a deletion), so bundles alone cannot restore refs; each repository has `refs.jsonl` with the changes after each bundle. Bundle attempts carry a random ID (`<seq>-<id>`): an abandoned partial upload is never overwritten, a new attempt simply takes over, and the hub accepts one complete bundle per sequence number.
3. **Export uses the archive's layout** (files plus bundles and ref logs, not raw repository directories), so import and `state pull` share one restore path and imported relays get devc's configuration instead of whatever a tar file holds.
4. **"A machine may read any machine's archive of realms it is configured for"**: the hub cannot know a machine's configuration, so it lets every enrolled machine read every archive; the machine refuses realms it is not configured for. Documented as the work order asks.
5. **Secrets bundles are kept per machine** (ten per machine and realm), so one machine cannot push the others' versions out; `pull` takes the newest across machines unless `--machine`. The work order says the hub keeps the last ten versions.
6. **Restore into existing state**: `--merge` adds missing files and refs; a ref with another value is reported and kept; the context repository's working tree is written only when the restore created `main`. Restored journals appear as this machine's sessions (and are archived again under its name).
7. **Shares (`durable/shares/`) are not archived or exported**: they are not part of a realm's durable state as section 1 defines it, and can be published again from their relays. The C16 leftover pointing at C17 stays open (see LEFTOVER).
8. **Additions:** `devc sync status --now`, `devc hub init --ca-from`, `devc hub ca import` (the "same CA files" of section 5 need a way to register certificates on the second hub), the ARCHIVE column of `devc hub status`, `--realm` for import, `--overwrite` for secret pull.
9. **`init` and `join` polish** (plan 4, not in the work order) was not done.
10. **Dependency:** `golang.org/x/crypto` (already required since C11a for argon2 and PKCS#12) now also provides `chacha20poly1305`; no new module.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Two hubs as in C11a/C11b (`bin/devc hub init --public-name 127.0.0.1:18443`, `… hub serve --listen 127.0.0.1:18443`; the second with another directory and port, or `--ca-from`), the machine joined to both, `bin/devc daemon`; `bin/devc sync status --now`, `bin/devc hub status`; on the hub `bin/devc hub archive ls|du`, `ls $DEVC_HUB_DIR/archive/<machine>/<realm>/files`. Second machine (other `DEVC_STATE_DIR`/`DEVC_RUNTIME_DIR`, joined with its own certificate): `bin/devc state pull <realm> --from https://127.0.0.1:18443 --list`, `… --machine <first>`, `bin/devc context log <realm>`. `bin/devc state export <realm> --out /tmp/r.tar`, then on a fresh state directory `bin/devc state import /tmp/r.tar`. `bin/devc secret push <realm> --to <url>` and `bin/devc secret pull <realm> --from <url>`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/archive/ ./internal/fleet/ ./internal/hub/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/cli/ ./internal/config/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestFleet$' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit: `archive.TestParsePath` (every kind, traversal, encodings, generations, categories), `TestExcluded`, `TestSealed` (round trip, wrong passphrase, another realm, tampering, parameter bounds, strict decoding); `boxside.TestArchiveWorker` (links to a host file and a directory, FIFO, oversized file, bad name, credential file, harness without manifest; list and tar; hostile listings refused); `hub.TestArchiveRatchet` (a hostile machine: offsets at 0, inside and beyond, another machine's tree, traversal in many encodings, categories, realms, parameters, methods, roles, the request header, bundles out of sequence, appended to when complete, a second attempt, reads, ranges, prefix hashes), `TestArchiveBudgets` (realm and machine quota, file limit, disk reserve, request size, concurrent uploads, a prune freeing the quota), `TestArchivePruneChains`, `TestSecretsBundles` (ten kept, newest, malformed bundles, no plaintext on disk), `TestSecondHubCA`, `TestEveryRouteNeedsACertificate` (the archive routes, machine paths and the machine class together, state changes with the header); `fleet` (against a real hub behind a loopback "proxy", workers run in-process): `TestOffsetProtocol` (byte for byte, credential file, link and oversized file left out, appends, shrink, rewrite rate limit, lost bookkeeping resumed by prefix hash and a differing file as a new generation, an interrupted pass resumed after lost bookkeeping, a realm without sync), `TestBundleSequence` (bundle per new objects, ref-only lines, deletions, context repository, restore on a second machine, refusal and merge), `TestRestoreFilesAndHostileArchive` (newest generation, credential file, existing file kept, a planted link not followed, secrets and unknown harnesses left out), `TestExportImport`, `TestSecretsRoundTrip`, `TestRateLimiter`, `TestContainerWorkers`; `config.TestSecretStatePatterns` and five broken cases; `daemon.TestHubAllowlist` (archive.status not for hubs), `TestHubRoles` (archive status per hub); `cli.TestFleetUsage`, `TestHubArchiveCommands`. Integration (`test/integration/fleet_test.go`, `TestFleet`, about 75 s): two hubs in direct TLS with their own CAs, machine A with a daemon and a box, machines B and C restoring; items 1–7 in the order 1, 3, 2, 4, 6, 5, 7 (item 1 includes the daemon killed while hub A holds part of a 900 KiB transcript at 128 KiB/s).

**Test results.** `./dev test ./...` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 52 tests, 51 passed and `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (about 27 minutes; `TestFleet` 53 s, `TestHub` 34 s). After that run only comments changed (package documentation of `hub` and `daemon`). The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, hubs or directories were left behind; nothing was written to `~/.local/share/devc-hub`.

### C18 Service logins

The work order's design replaces the plan's "service containers" and spec section 17's "a separate container holds the password": the daemon holds the credential and proxies; only a cookie login runs in a throwaway container. `specs-v2.md` section 17 and `docs/dev/plan.md` (package map, chunk table) now say so.

**What exists.**

- **Definitions** (`internal/config/services.go`). `realms/<realm>/services/<name>/service.toml`: `upstream` (https; http only with `allow_insecure = true`; scheme and host with an optional port, nothing else), `port` (1025–65535, not 3128, unique in the realm), `[auth]` `kind = "header"` (`header`, `value_secret`) or `"cookie"` (`login` argv, `secrets`, `login_path`, `refresh` 1m–2160h default 12h, `image`, `auto`); keys of the other kind are problems; `[[allow]]` (1–64 rules, methods from GET HEAD POST PUT PATCH DELETE OPTIONS, `path` already in normal form, `config.CheckRulePath`); `[limits]` `max_body` (default 10m, ≤ 4g), `timeout` (default 60s, 1s–1h). Service names follow the general rule, at most 58 characters (the jar is the secret `_jar.<name>`). `grants.services` names services of the project's realm; a project may not also receive a service's credential secrets (grants.secrets or a harness credential), may not grant `_jar.*` secrets, and its `box.ports` may not hold a service port. A granted service whose definition has problems blocks the project (`ProblemsFor` covers the service directory). `Config.Service`, `Services`, `ServiceProblems`, `GrantedTo`; `Resolved.Services` (JSON views only). `Service.Fingerprint()`: upstream, allow_insecure, port, kind, header, value_secret, rules. The daemon's configuration fingerprint includes the service files.
- **Capability record** (`internal/box/capabilities.go`). `Capabilities.Services` (name, port, fingerprint) written at every start; `Capabilities.Service(r, name)` is the definition in force (granted at the start and now, same fingerprint and port) or why not; `ServicePorts`.
- **Box agent** (`internal/boxside/netrelay.go`). New host-to-box method `box.services {services: [{name, port}]}` (validated: ≤ 64, names valid, ports above 1024, each once): the agent listens on exactly those loopback ports and relays every connection unparsed on a `host.service {name}` stream with C13's relay code (limits shared with the proxy); services left out or moved are closed; failures answered per port. Without a host a connection is closed at once.
- **Daemon** (`internal/daemon/services.go`). Per connected channel `serviceLoop` sends `box.services` at connect and when the services in force change (checked every 5 s, so a removed grant closes the port); agents without the method are logged once. `host.service` streams take one of the 64 relay places (else 503). Per stream: the service must be in force (`serviceInForce`: configuration without problems, grant now, capability record of this container, unchanged definition), else 403 with the reason; the head is read (`svclogin.ReadHead`, 64 KiB, 30 s) and checked (`svclogin.CheckHead`), then the rules (`svclogin.Allowed`; a miss gets 403 with the `[[allow]]` block that would be needed); only then the bytes go to one `http.Server` of the daemon (keep-alives off, `ConnContext` carries the decision) whose handler is `svclogin.Proxy`. Credentials: header kind from the realm secret (missing: 503 "devc secret set …"; must be a single line); cookie kind from the jar for the request's path. Login state per service (`local/services/<realm>/<name>.json`): a jar that is missing, older than `refresh`, without a live cookie for the upstream's host, or a mark newer than the jar (401 or a redirect to `login_path`) means "needs a login": 503 with what to tell the user, the mark written once, a host `note` in every live session of the requesting box once per need and box. With `auto`, `autoLogin` runs `devc service login --quiet -- <realm> <name>` as a child process (from `/`, the daemon's directories): one at a time per service (concurrent requests wait for it), at most once a minute; a failure is kept in the state file and the daemon log, never in the box's message. Every request is logged to `durable/realms/<realm>/service-log/<name>.jsonl` (time, box, method, path without query, status, a devc reason for refusals; rotated at 4 MiB). Upstream transport: the host's trust store (`SSL_CERT_FILE`/`SSL_CERT_DIR`), no proxy, HTTP/2, no added compression, no redirects followed (`Options.ServiceTransport` for tests).
- **`internal/svclogin`** (new). `ReadHead`/`CheckHead`: CRLF lines only, request line `METHOD SP target SP HTTP/1.x` with single spaces, origin-form only (absolute-form, CONNECT, `*` refused), token header names, no obs-fold, no control characters, at most 100 fields, Content-Length digits only and at most once, Transfer-Encoding exactly `chunked` and at most once, never both, none with HTTP/1.0, exactly one Host equal to `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, no Upgrade/HTTP2-Settings/`Connection: upgrade` (403), Expect only `100-continue` (417). `NormalizeTarget`: RFC 3986 path characters only, valid percent-encodings, unreserved ones decoded, others upper-cased, encoded `/` `\` `%` and controls refused, `.`/`..` segments (also before `;`) and empty segments (except the last) refused, query characters checked, `#` refused. `PathMatches` at segment boundaries. `Proxy` (`httputil.ReverseProxy` with `Rewrite`): target = the normalised path (`URL.Opaque`), `Host` = upstream; removes `Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Cookie2`, the credential header, `Proxy-*`, `Forwarded`, `X-Forwarded-*`, `X-Real-IP`, `Via`, `Upgrade`, `HTTP2-Settings` (and hop-by-hop); `Origin`/`Referer` of the local address become the upstream's, others are dropped; adds the credential. Responses: 401 or a redirect to `login_path` of a cookie service become 503 plus `LoginNeeded`; `Set-Cookie`, `Set-Cookie2`, `Alt-Svc`, `Authentication-Info`, `Proxy-*` dropped; `Location`/`Content-Location` on the upstream rewritten to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`; every header scrubbed (`ScrubValues`: the value, its fields, Basic's decoded parts; URL-encoded forms; names case-insensitive; `Content-Length` dropped instead of changed); trailers dropped; 1xx never forwarded (the reverse proxy's client trace is cut off, `noTrace`); bodies limited to `max_body` both ways (413/502, a streamed response is aborted); `timeout` for the whole exchange (504). Errors are scrubbed. `ParseLoginOutput` (strict JSON array, fields name/value/domain/path/expiry only, RFC 6265 names and values, a domain matching the upstream's host, ≤ 64 cookies, ≤ 4 KiB values, no duplicates, at least one live, ≤ 256 KiB output), `Jar` (stored form with `login_at`, `Header` with path-match and expiry, `Problem`, `Expires`, `ParseJar` validates again), `State`, `Log`/`ReadLog`, `Login.Run` (the container below).
- **Login** (`svclogin.Login`). Serialised per service by `local/services/<realm>/<name>.lock`. Scratch directory below `local/tmp` (0700): the regular, non-hidden files of the service's directory except `service.toml` (≤ 32, ≤ 1 MiB each, executable bit kept) and `secrets/<NAME>` (0600) for `auth.secrets` (missing: an error naming `devc secret set`). Container: `auth.image` or the installer image (built when missing), network on, `--cap-drop=ALL`, the aux security options (no-new-privileges, the toolbox seccomp profile), the only mount the scratch directory at `/login` (working directory), tmpfs, `HOME=/tmp`, `DEVC_SERVICE`, `DEVC_SERVICE_UPSTREAM`, `DEVC_LOGIN_PATH`, `DEVC_SECRETS_DIR`, no standard input, 5 minutes, labels `devc.role=aux`, `devc.aux=service-login`. Standard output capped and parsed; standard error kept (64 KiB), made printable, secret values (and the new cookie values) replaced. The jar becomes `_jar.<service>`; the state is reset.
- **CLI** (`internal/cli/svccmds.go`). `devc service ls [realm] [--json]` (kind, port, upstream, login state, granted projects), `devc service login <realm> <name> [--quiet]` (refuses header services before touching the toolbox), `devc service status <realm> <name> [--json]` (exit 1 unless the login or secret is in order), `devc service log <realm> <name> [-n N] [--json]`. `devc service status` without arguments still reports the systemd units.
- **Sandbox description** lists each granted service with its address and rules, how refusals and needed logins look, and that the daemon must run.
- **Documentation.** Guide section "Service logins" (definition, checks, headers, cookie logins, limits including body reflection and a reminder of the service's terms) and two lines in "What is protected"; `examples/services/` (README with notes on browser-driven logins, `intranet/service.toml`, `intranet/login.sh`: a curl form post that converts curl's cookie file to devc's JSON); `examples/config/…/analysis/project.toml` mentions `grants.services`.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **No service containers** (plan, spec 17): the work order's daemon-held credential; the login alone runs in a container.
2. **Malformed or ambiguous requests get 400** (431, 505, 501, 417 where they fit); 403 is for rule misses (with the rule that would be needed), services not in force, and upgrades. The work order says "anything else gets 403 with a body saying which rule would be needed", but no rule can allow `..` or an encoded slash.
3. **One request per connection.** The daemon checks the raw head and hands exactly those bytes to net/http, which then answers with `Connection: close`; nothing after the first request is read, so nothing can be smuggled behind it. Clients that pipeline get one answer per connection.
4. **Normalised paths go upstream.** Rules compare the normalised path, and the upstream receives that same form (`URL.Opaque`), so what was checked is what is sent. Prefixes match at segment boundaries; `HEAD` is allowed only where listed.
5. **Ports come from the daemon** (`box.services`, like C13's `box.listen`), not from `box.json`: the box definition and its hash do not change, and a removed grant closes the port within 5 s. The service is a start capability with a fingerprint (finding C1-02): any change of upstream, port, credential header or secret, or rules needs a restart; the login command, `refresh`, `auto`, `login_path` and limits apply at once.
6. **Services work at every network level**, `none` included: they are an explicit grant like the SSH gate.
7. **`Host`** may be `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` (case-insensitive name), the port always.
8. **The "note for the user"** is a host `note` event in every live session of the box that met the need, once per need and box, plus the warning in the daemon log and the state in `devc service ls`/`status`. A box without live sessions gets no note.
9. **Automatic logins**: at most once a minute per service; after a 401 or a login redirect with `auto` the request gets 503 (its body cannot be replayed) and the login starts in the background. The failure's text, which comes from the login command, stays on the host.
10. **Scrubbing threshold**: only values of 8 characters or more are replaced (shorter cookie values such as a language would garble ordinary headers).
11. **The login sees the other files of the service's directory** (copied into `/login`), so a script and a CA file need no extra keys. The login has no standard input: a second factor cannot be typed.
12. **No private-address checks for upstreams**: the upstream is the user's configuration and internal services are the point. The test CA reaches the daemon through `SSL_CERT_FILE`; no hidden flag.
13. **`devc service status`** is overloaded by argument count (C08's unit status with none, a service's state with two).

**Upgrade notes.** The daemon must be restarted. A box started before C18 has no services in its capability record and an agent without `box.services`: restart it (`devc down`, `devc up`) after granting a service.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; copy `examples/services/intranet` to `<config>/realms/demo/services/`, adapt `upstream` and the rules, `bin/devc secret set demo INTRANET_USER`, `… INTRANET_PASSWORD`, `bin/devc service login demo intranet`; or a header service (`kind = "header"`, `bin/devc secret set demo WIKI_TOKEN`). `[grants] services = ["intranet"]` in a project, `bin/devc up <ref>`, `bin/devc daemon`; in the box `bash -c 'exec 3<>/dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/8602; printf "GET /wiki/ HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: 127.0.0.1:8602\r\nConnection: close\r\n\r\n" >&3; cat <&3'`; `bin/devc service ls`, `bin/devc service status demo intranet`, `bin/devc service log demo intranet`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/svclogin/ ./internal/config/ ./internal/box/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/cli/ ./internal/harness/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestServiceHeader|TestServiceCookie' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit: `svclogin.TestNormalizeTarget` (traversal, encoded dots and slashes, double encoding, empty segments, `..;`, characters, fragments), `TestPathMatchesAndAllowed`, `TestCheckHead` (about 40 hostile heads: absolute form, CONNECT, versions, Host forms, duplicated and conflicting lengths, Transfer-Encoding variants, obs-fold, controls, upgrades, Expect, field count), `TestReadHead` (bare LF, oversized line and head, incomplete), `TestProxyHeaders` (box credential/cookie/proxy/forwarding headers removed, credential added, Host, Origin/Referer, scrubbing, Set-Cookie, 1xx and trailers not forwarded, redirects, normalised path, log), `TestProxyLimits` (declared and chunked bodies, large and streamed responses, timeout), `TestProxyCredentialErrors`, `TestProxyCookieLogin` (401 and login redirect), `TestScrubValues`, `TestProxyExpectContinue`, `TestParseLoginOutput` (about 20 hostile outputs), `TestJar`, `TestStateAndLog`, `TestLogin` (fake engine: only the scratch mount, files and secrets, run options, redacted stderr, jar and state, failures); `config.TestServices`, `TestBrokenServices` (about 50 cases), `TestCheckRulePath`, `TestExampleService`; `box.TestServiceCapabilities`; `boxside.TestAgentServices`; `daemon.TestServiceStreams` (real channel and agent, TLS upstream: no record, ports opened by sync, missing secret, credential added and not returned, refusals, crafted stream for another service, log, cookie service without login, note once per need, jar, 401, automatic login once for 8 concurrent requests, removed grant closes the port, changed definition refused), `TestServiceHostileStreams`; `cli.TestServiceCommands`; `harness.TestServicesInDescription`. Integration (`test/integration/service_test.go`): `TestServiceHeader` (items 1, 2, 4, 5: credential at the upstream, absent from responses and from the box's files and environments, rules, traversal, encoded slashes, absolute form, other Host, smuggling shapes, upgrade, Set-Cookie, redirect, log, status, a box without the grant and a box of another realm, daemon stopped), `TestServiceCookie` (item 3, with the shipped `login.sh` in the installer image against the test upstream on the host's LAN address: no login, form login stores a jar, cookie carried and not returned, the upstream ending the session, an expired jar, notes, `auto = true` with 6 concurrent requests and one login).

**Test results.** `./dev test ./...` passes (38 packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` passes: 54 tests, 53 passed and `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (about 28 minutes; `TestServiceHeader` 28 s, `TestServiceCookie` 39 s). After that run only comments changed (the `daemon` package comment). The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, processes or directories were left behind.

### C19 GUI sidecar and browser view

**What exists.**

- **Image** `localhost/devc-aux/desktop` (`assets/desktop/Containerfile`, `assets/desktop/devc-desktop`): Debian bookworm-slim with `tigervnc-standalone-server`, `websockify`, `socat`, `matchbox-window-manager`, and noVNC's web files taken from Debian's `novnc` package (`apt-get download` + `dpkg-deb`; `index.html` links to `vnc.html`). About 470 MB. `toolbox.EnsureAuxImage` (new, generalises the installer's build: several context files, hash over all of them, a single-file image keeps the old hash so the installer cache stays valid; `rebuild` builds with `NoCache` and `Pull`) builds it on first use into `<cache>/aux/desktop`.
- **`devc-desktop`** (bash, in the image): `run` waits up to 120 s for the secrets, then starts `Xvnc :1 -auth … -rfbauth … -SecurityTypes VncAuth -interface 127.0.0.1 -rfbport 5901 -nolisten tcp -nolisten local -nopn -geometry 1600x900`, `socat TCP-LISTEN:6001,bind=127.0.0.1,fork UNIX-CONNECT:/tmp/.X11-unix/X1`, `websockify --web /usr/share/novnc 127.0.0.1:6080 127.0.0.1:5901` and matchbox, and exits when any of them ends (`wait -n`); `secrets` unpacks a tar archive from standard input into `/tmp/devc/secrets` (once); `ready` waits until the X socket exists and Xvnc, socat and websockify each own their listening socket (the socket inode of the `/proc/net/tcp` line among the process's descriptors), failing at once when a process has ended (with the tail of its log).
- **`internal/display`** (new leaf package): Xauthority encode/parse/lookup (`CookieFile`: one entry of family wild, display 1), the X11 connection setup (`Handshake`, `RefusedError`, bounded), VNC's password file and challenge response (DES with bit-reversed key bytes; vectors from `vncpasswd -f`).
- **`internal/gui`** (new): `SidecarArgs` (`--name devc-<realm>-<project>_gui`, labels of plan 3.1 with role `aux` plus `devc.aux=desktop`, `devc.gui.box=<box id>`, `devc.gui.box-start=<pid>@<start ns>`; `--network container:<box id>`, `--userns=container:<box id>`, `--user 1000:1000`, `--cap-drop=ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, the toolbox seccomp profile, the box's `label=` options, `--read-only --read-only-tmpfs=false`, one tmpfs at `/tmp`, `--init`, `--pull=never`, `--http-proxy=false`). `NewSecrets` (16-byte cookie, 8-character password from `[A-Za-z0-9]` by rejection sampling), `Archive`. `Manager.Up` under the box lock (`box.Manager.WithRunningBox`, which starts the box like `EnsureRunning`): keeps a running sidecar of the box's current start and reprints its address (forwarding again if its forward ended) unless `Restart`; otherwise removes any old one, ensures the image, creates and starts the sidecar, hands over the secrets (stdin of `podman exec -i --user 1000:1000 … devc-desktop secrets`), waits for `ready`, checks the box with `box.Manager.CheckStart` (same container, PID and start time, ready, and at level internet the binding and rules), writes the cookie to `<runtime>/<box id>/display.xauth` (0600; `safefs.OpenDir`, `O_EXCL` temporary name, `renameat`) and `/etc/devc/display.env`, starts a detached forward of port 6080 and saves `<runtime>/gui/<box id>.json` (0600: password, forward, host port). A failure stops the sidecar (kept for `podman logs`) and removes the box's files. `Down` (box lock), `RemoveDependent` (box lock held by the caller), `Status` (absent, running, stopped, stale).
- **`box`**: `Dependent` interface and `Manager.Dependents`, removed (all tried, any failure stops the operation) in `down` (after the stop hooks, before the engine stop; also for a stopped or missing box), `rm` (before the engine removal, also for a stopped box), a recreation, the restart of a box running without its rules, and `publish` (every start). `WithRunningBox`, `CheckStart`; `EnsureRunning` now shares `ensureRunning`.
- **Box side**: `boxside.DisplayEnv`, `ReadDisplayEnv` (strict: exactly `DISPLAY` and `XAUTHORITY`, checked values, 1 KiB), `WithDisplay`; `PrepareLaunch` sets both when `/etc/devc/display.env` exists (before the manifest's environment); `devc box shell` (the login shell of `devc sh`) too. Hidden `devc box xcheck [--no-auth]`: opens the display from `DISPLAY`/`XAUTHORITY` or `display.env` and prints `accepted: …` or `refused: …` (exit 1).
- **CLI**: `devc gui up [ref] [--restart] [--host-port N] [--bind ADDR] [--rebuild]`, `gui down`, `gui status [--json]`, `gui build [--rebuild]`; `devc browser [ref] [--port 9222] [--host-port N] [--detach]` (the forward of `devc forward`, then the DevTools endpoint, how to add it in `chrome://inspect`, and a hint with the Chromium flags when `/json/version` does not answer). The forward code was split (`spawnForward`, `stopForward`, `urlHost`) so the sidecar's viewer is an ordinary detached forward. The box environment registers the sidecar manager as the box manager's dependent.
- **Sandbox description**: one line on graphical programs: the Chromium flags (with `--no-sandbox` and why), `devc browser`, and `display.env` while a desktop runs.
- **Documentation**: guide section "Watching a browser or a desktop" (both ways, what the sidecar is and runs, the cookie readable in the box so anything there can drive and read the display, levels, lifecycle, phone access needing the hub's care), a bullet in "What is protected and what is not"; spec phase 4 line; plan package map; README map.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **The sidecar joins the box's user namespace** (`--userns=container:<box>`), not a separate identical mapping: with its own `keep-id` mapping, runc cannot mount sysfs in a network namespace that the box's user namespace owns (levels none and allowlist: "error mounting sysfs … operation not permitted"). Joining gives exactly the box's mapping and works at every level; mount, PID, IPC and UTS namespaces stay the sidecar's own.
2. **X reaches 127.0.0.1:6001 through socat.** Xvnc can listen for X over TCP only on every address or none, so it runs with `-nolisten tcp -nolisten local` (no TCP, no abstract socket, which would be shared through the network namespace) on a socket file in the sidecar's tmpfs, and socat offers that on loopback only. Everything the sidecar serves listens on 127.0.0.1 of the shared namespace; the integration test checks the box's other addresses and the abstract socket table.
3. **Window manager matchbox** (windows full-size, which suits watching one browser); openbox would have added about 100 MB (imlib2 with Ghostscript and video codecs). **noVNC's files come from Debian's package without its dependencies** (Node.js and the OpenStack console proxy).
4. **Secrets are drawn by devc** and reach the sidecar as a tar archive on the standard input of an exec, before Xvnc starts; the VNC password file is computed in Go. The password has eight characters because VNC uses no more. The printed address carries it after `#` (noVNC reads its settings from the fragment, which the browser does not send).
5. **The cookie is in `/run/devc/display.xauth`** (the runtime directory is a tmpfs, so the cookie never touches disk) and is written without following anything the box planted; `display.env` lives in the read-only `/etc/devc`. The Xauthority entry has family "wild", so `:1`, `127.0.0.1:1` and `localhost:1` all find it.
6. **`gui up` is idempotent** (not in the work order): a host record (`<runtime>/gui/<box id>.json`, 0600, a directory no box sees) keeps the password and the forward, so a second `gui up` prints the same address; `--restart` draws new secrets. Added `gui build`, `--rebuild`, `--host-port`, `--bind`, `browser --detach/--host-port`, and the hidden `box xcheck` (the integration test's X client, also useful by hand).
7. **Readiness proves ownership**: `ready` checks that the sidecar's processes hold the listening sockets, so a box process that took port 6001, 5901 or 6080 first makes `gui up` fail instead of handing out an address the box serves. A failed start stops the sidecar but keeps it for its log; `gui down`, `down`, `rm` or the next `gui up` remove it.
8. **Fail closed after joining**: `CheckStart` runs after the sidecar is ready; a box that was restarted, lost its readiness marker or (level internet) its binding or rules gets its sidecar stopped and `gui up` fails. Nothing on this host disturbed either; the check is there so a future engine change cannot go unnoticed.
9. **The sidecar goes at every start of the box**, not only at down and rm: it must not outlive the start whose network namespace it joined (a box stopped outside devc leaves its sidecar in the old, unconnected namespace; `gui status` says "stale" until then). In `down`, the stop hooks (banking) run first, so a refused bank keeps box and desktop.
10. **A container with the sidecar's name that devc did not create** stops `gui up` and `gui down`; `down` and `rm` leave it alone with a warning.
11. **Hardening beyond the work order**: read-only root filesystem without Podman's extra tmpfs mounts, `--init`, `--pull=never`, `--http-proxy=false`.
12. **Sandbox description** got the work order's Chromium sentence and a second clause about `display.env`.
13. **`devc run` does not get `DISPLAY`**: the work order names `devc box launch` and login shells; `devc run` executes its argument vector directly (see LEFTOVER).
14. **Tests**: `itest.RemoveTestContainers` removes auxiliary containers first (the engine refuses to remove a box with a dependent); the desktop image is kept between runs like the installer image (`KeptImages`).

**Upgrade notes.** Nothing changes for existing boxes; the image is built on the first `devc gui up` (or `devc gui build`), which needs the network. Sessions started before `gui up` do not have `DISPLAY`.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`; `bin/devc gui up <ref>`, open the printed address; in the box (`bin/devc sh <ref>`) `xdpyinfo`, a browser, or `/opt/devc/bin/devc box xcheck`; `bin/devc gui status <ref>`, `bin/devc gui down <ref>`; `bin/devc forward 5901 <ref>` and a VNC client with the password. Browser: Chromium in the box with `--remote-debugging-port=9222 --remote-debugging-address=127.0.0.1 --no-sandbox`, then `bin/devc browser <ref>` and `chrome://inspect`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/gui/ ./internal/display/ ./internal/box/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/cli/ ./internal/toolbox/ ./internal/harness/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestGUI' -v`.

**Tests.** Unit: `display.TestVNCPasswordFile` (vectors of `vncpasswd -f`), `TestVNCResponse`, `TestXAuthority` (encoding, lookup by family, truncation, size cap), `TestHandshake` (success with vendor and screen size, refusal, authenticate, truncated, unknown status); `gui.TestSidecarArgs` (exact arguments, one tmpfs mount and nothing else, nine bad inputs), `TestSecrets` (lengths, alphabet, distinct draws, deterministic source with rejection, failing sources, archive contents and modes), `TestViewerURL`, `TestUpDownStatus` (box started by `gui up`, image built, join arguments, secrets on stdin only, cookie 0600 equal to the display's, display.env, forward, record, status, reuse, new forward, restart, down removes the sidecar before the box stops and every file), `TestRemoveAndStartRemoveSidecar` (rm order, stale after the box stopped, removed at the next start, stale after a restart, replaced by `gui up`, `gui down` twice), `TestUpFailures` (not ready, box disturbed after the join, box restarted meanwhile, forward fails, foreign container, a planted symlink at the cookie's place); `box.TestDependents` (when the dependent is removed in create, up, down, start, recreate, rm of running, stopped and missing boxes; a failure keeps the box), `TestWithRunningBoxAndCheckStart`; `boxside.TestReadDisplayEnv`, `TestWithDisplay` (also through `PrepareLaunch`), `TestXCheck` (against a fake X server on a loopback port: with the cookie from display.env, without, `XAUTHORITY` from the environment, no display, bad `DISPLAY`); `toolbox.TestEnsureAuxImage`; `cli.TestGUIUsage`, `TestGUIHelp`; `harness.TestGUIInDescription`. Integration (`test/integration/gui_test.go`): `TestGUI` (items 1 to 5 at level internet: viewer page and its WebSocket through the forward, VNC offers only VNC authentication and accepts only the password, `box xcheck` accepted with the cookie and refused without, cookie mode and owner, display.env, nothing listening on the box's other addresses and no abstract X socket, sidecar without capabilities, with no-new-privileges and seccomp, only tmpfs mounts, network mode of the box, UID 1000, none of `/w`, `/home/dev`, `/opt/devc`, `/etc/devc`, `/run/devc` visible, read-only root; the box still "already running" with its rules and sentinel after the join; a second `gui up` prints the same address; `down` removes the sidecar, status before and after, the viewer stops answering; `devc browser` against a fake DevTools endpoint, and the hint when nothing answers), `TestGUILevels` (open, none, allowlist: viewer and X work, the sidecar sees exactly the box's interfaces, only `lo` at none and allowlist, the box undisturbed; `rm` removes the sidecar; a box stopped outside devc leaves a stale sidecar that the next start removes with display.env; `gui down`).

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (all packages, `internal/gui` and `internal/display` new); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 56 tests: 55 passed and `TestHarnessNetwork` was skipped as before (about 30 minutes; `TestGUI` 37 s, `TestGUILevels` 56 s with the image cached; the first build of the desktop image took about a minute here). After that run, `remove` was changed so that only a sidecar that cannot be removed stops `down`, `rm` or a start (a box-planted directory at the cookie's place is now a warning; unit test `planted directory`), and `./dev itest -run TestGUI -v` passed again with that build. No test containers, forwards, processes or directories were left behind; `localhost/devc-aux/desktop:latest` stays as a cache, like the installer image.

### C19r1 Fixes after the final review, part 1

All twenty findings of `docs/dev/reviews/D1-review.md` and the unconfirmed D1-21 are resolved as the work order's decisions table directs; `docs/dev/reviews/D1-response.md` has one paragraph per ID with its tests. What changed:

- **Router (D1-01; `internal/daemon/router.go`).** `crossOrigin`: a WebSocket upgrade or a method other than GET/HEAD needs no `Origin` or exactly `http://<requested host>`; `Sec-Fetch-Site` other than `same-origin`, `same-site` or `none` is refused except a top-level navigation by GET. 403 with a one-line reason.
- **Proxy (D1-02, D1-06; `internal/netpolicy/proxy.go`).** Upstream response heads: 64 KiB per head (`io.LimitedReader` under the buffered reader, reset per interim answer) and a fixed deadline for the head (`Proxy.ResponseTimeout`, default 5 min; `idleConn.setFixed`). `Proxy.guard` watches every tunnel and every upstream connection serving a plain request: checked at once, every `RecheckEvery` (5 s), when an exception in force ends (`ProxyPolicy.Ends`, from `netpolicy.NameEntriesEnd`), and on `Tunnels.Recheck`; closed (and logged) when the destination is no longer allowed or the policy cannot be read. The daemon gives each box worker a `netpolicy.Tunnels`; `syncNet` (the 5-second loop and `net.sync`) calls `Recheck`; `devc net allow/revoke` now call `net.sync` for name exceptions at level allowlist too. `ProxyPolicy.Allows`.
- **Host aliases (D1-21; `internal/netpolicy`).** `BackendHostAddrs` (slirp4netns's gateway from its CIDR, pasta's `169.254.1.2`, named networks' gateways) joins the hosts file's alias in `Facts.HostAlias`; `Install` rejects them with the host's addresses before `@lan4`, and the canary tries them.
- **Relays (D1-05, D1-07; `internal/gitx`, `internal/daemon/relay.go`).** Relay configuration and every receive-pack and box fetch: `transfer/receive/fetch.unpackLimit = 1`. Before receive-pack: 1 GiB free on the state file system (`pushMinFree`; statfs, fail closed) and a relay within `git.relay_quota`; `watchPush` measures `gitx.QuarantineUsage` (`objects/tmp_objdir-incoming-*`) every 200 ms and ends the push beyond the budget or below 512 MiB free, then `RemoveQuarantines`. `serveGit` reloads the configuration first (the hooks read it from disk; the daemon's limits must agree). Archive refs and restores only in devc's namespaces (`gitx.ArchiveRef`); every host git command gets `--no-replace-objects` and `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` (the context worker the variable).
- **Receipts (D1-11).** `receiptStore.publish` fsyncs the directory after the link and after the rename; a failure fails the step (a new record is removed again).
- **Hub archive (D1-03, D1-04, D1-08, D1-14, D1-15, D1-18, D1-20; `internal/hub/archive.go`).** `archiveStore.admit`: every endpoint (upload, secrets push; index, summary, download, hash, secrets read) takes a global and a per-machine place first (new `[archive] machine_downloads` 2, `max_realms` 100). Summaries from the cached per-machine usage (which now includes secrets bundles); `ArchiveIndex(ctx, …, after, limit)` pages of `archive.IndexPage` (2000) in walk order, skipping subtrees before the cursor. Downloads: `stallWriter` with a 30 s write deadline per write (`archiveStall`), refused when it cannot be set. Secrets bundles count against quotas, files and realms. File generations have a record `<file>~meta` (class, mode, committed length; written whole and renamed); `PUT …&final=1` on a file marks the generation complete (an empty body creates an empty file); `class=` and `mode=` are taken when a generation is created; an older generation than one stored is refused (the newest generation per file is learnt from one listing per directory and kept current, reset by a prune); on completion of a mutable generation the hub drops mutable generations older than the newest `archive.KeepMutable` (3) complete ones (`dropOldGenerations`, usage adjusted). Prune removes records with their files.
- **Machine side (D1-14–D1-20; `internal/fleet`, `internal/archive`, `internal/config`).** `Harness.TranscriptStatePatterns`, `archive.MatchGlob` (`**`), `archive.ClassOf`; host-written logs are append-only. `FileState` (state version 2): class, full-prefix SHA-256, completion length, snapshot of a mutable generation, source stat.
