Append-only files: full prefix rehash when inode/mtime changed or more is to be sent; completion mark when an upload caught up with an unchanged file. Mutable files: snapshot (`local/archive/<hub>/snap/`, copied again if the file changed while copied), a new generation for any change at most once per `[sync] mutable_interval` (new global key, default `6h`, 1m–720h), `Waiting` count in the status. New bookkeeping starts after the newest generation the hub's index shows. Ref log lines in bounded chunks (`appendRefs`), cut lines ended at resync (`terminateRefLog`), unterminated last lines skipped (`ReplayRefs`). `Client.Put(…, PutOptions)`, `Client.Index` reads pages. Restore: `chooseGenerations` (newest complete at its length; reports incomplete ones; legacy archives as before), `restoreMode`, `Source.SourceRealm` (another realm refused). Exports and the archive worker's tar carry permission bits. The harness listing is bounded end to end (`boxside.MaxArchiveListing`, worker-side `listingLimit`, `engine.RunOptions.MaxOutput`, a cut capture is an error); a failed scan sets `ArchiveRealmStatus.Incomplete` and the hub state `incomplete`.
- **CLI.** `devc state import --realm` other than the export's realm is refused; `devc sync status` prints incomplete passes and waiting files; `devc hub status` shows "incomplete".
- **Web app (D1-09, D1-10, D1-12, D1-13).** `timeline.js`: runs of at most `TOOL_RUN_MAX` (200) calls with per-call seqs, trimmed with the window; `prepend` beyond `MAX_EVENTS` moves the window back (`live: false`). `api.js`: an unreadable successful answer or a command answer that is not a receipt is an uncertain `ApiError`. `store.js`: `sessionVer` per session from stream IDs, detail `stream_id` and the snapshot position (`putSession`); a non-live window ignores live events except for settling the outbox. `session.js`: **Load earlier** whenever older events exist, paged-in entries shown at once, **Back to the latest**. `stream.js` passes event IDs. `sw.js` cache `devc-shell-3`. Dev mode has a desktop session with about 2,600 events (`home/blog`, worktree `history`).
- **Documentation.** Guide (router origins, LAN exceptions and host aliases, tunnels ending, push storage, archive classes and versions, restore under the realm's own name); `docs/hub-deployment.md` section 10 (generations, classes, retention, admission, new keys); `examples/config/config.toml` shows `mutable_interval`.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Append-only regeneration stays rate-limited** to one new generation per hour per file (as before): every append-only generation is kept, so a box rewriting a transcript continuously must not fill the quota. The decision table names an interval only for mutable files.
2. **Mutable generations come from a snapshot** copied when the generation begins (the review allowed a snapshot or a check that the source did not change); a file that keeps changing during its upload would otherwise never complete.
3. **The hub refuses a generation older than one it holds**, and a machine without bookkeeping starts after the newest generation in the index: with the hub dropping old mutable generations, counting up from 0 could otherwise re-create a dropped number below a newer one.
4. **Session summary versions are stream positions**, not `last_seq`: summaries also change without events (machine online, request counts), and the stream position orders every change.
5. **Router: `same-site` passes Fetch Metadata** as the decision says (only cross-site is refused); the Origin rule still refuses WebSockets and non-GET requests between ports and projects.
6. **Push free-space reserve**: 1 GiB to start, 512 MiB while running (constants), and the push watchdog also ends a push that would take the file system below that, not only one beyond the relay budget.
7. **`devc net revoke` of a name exception at level allowlist calls the daemon** (`net.sync`, best effort) so open tunnels end at once; without a daemon there is no proxy and nothing to end.
8. **Restore without any complete generation**: an append-only file's newest generation is restored as far as it goes (any prefix is a state it had) and reported; a mutable one is skipped and reported. Archives from before completion marks restore as before.

**Upgrade notes.** Boxes started before C19r1 keep their old rules (the host aliases are rejected after the next start). The daemon must be restarted for the router, proxy and push checks. Existing relays get the unpack settings at their next `Configure` and on every receive-pack and box fetch already. The uploader's bookkeeping starts over once (version 2) and adopts what the hub holds by prefix hash; the hub must run C19r1 before its machines upload with completion marks (an older hub refuses `final=1` on files). Hubs and machines are upgraded together, as before.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Router: `curl -H 'Origin: http://evil.example' -X POST http://3000.<project>.<realm>.localhost:7480/` (403) beside a plain `curl` (routed). Proxy: a box at level allowlist with `devc net allow pkg.example.org:443 <ref> --for 1m`, a `CONNECT` held open in the box, then `devc net revoke <id>` (closed at once) or wait a minute. Pushes: `[git] relay_quota = "8m"` and push 60 MB from the box; the daemon log says "a push was ended". Archive: `devc sync status` (incomplete passes, waiting files); on the hub `ls $DEVC_HUB_DIR/archive/<machine>/<realm>/files/...` shows `~meta` records; a restore reports incomplete generations. Web app: `web/check.sh` (the dev session `home/blog · history`). Tests: `./dev test ./internal/netpolicy/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/hub/ ./internal/fleet/ ./internal/gitx/ ./internal/archive/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/config/ ./internal/box/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestLocalURLs|TestNetworkExceptions|TestRelayService|TestFleet$|TestHub$|TestWebApp' -v`.

**Tests.** New unit tests: `netpolicy.TestProxyResponseHead`, `TestProxyTunnelsEnd`; `daemon.TestPushWatchdog`; `hub.TestArchiveAdmission`, `TestArchiveDownloadStall`, `TestArchiveIndexPages`, `TestArchiveGenerations`, `TestSecretsQuota`; `fleet.TestRestoreRefusesReplaceRefs`, `TestFileClasses`, `TestSnapshotConsistency`, `TestCompleteGenerations`, `TestRefLogChunks`, `TestIncompletePass`; `archive.TestMatchGlob`. Extended: `daemon.TestRouterRefusals` (seventeen origin cases), `TestNetProxy` (a tunnel ends at revocation), `TestRelayStreams` (free-space reserve), `TestReceiptStore` and `TestDeliveryChecks` (directory sync), `netpolicy.TestGather`, `TestInstall`, `gitx.TestHostCmd`, `TestServeCall`, `boxside.TestArchiveWorker` (modes, listing limit), `fleet.TestContainerWorkers` (cut capture), `config.TestSecretStatePatterns` (transcript patterns) and a broken `mutable_interval`. Integration: `TestLocalURLs` (WebSocket from another origin), `TestNetworkExceptions` (host alias with a `lan` exception), `TestRelayService` (a 300 MB push lands; a push beyond an 8 MiB budget is ended by the watchdog and leaves nothing). `web/check.sh`: a fourth action run (the long history: bounded tool runs, paging back to the start within 1,000 events, back to the latest) and logic checks in the page's modules (tool runs, moving window, unreadable answers, command identity, summary versions).

**Changed tests of earlier chunks.** `TestArchiveRatchet` expected a file made final to be refused (now it marks the generation complete; a ref log made final is refused) and three index entries with the journal's completion; `TestArchiveBudgets` uses the renamed admission counter; `box.TestInternetLevel` expects slirp4netns's gateway in the rejected set and the canary; `gitx.TestHostCmd` and `TestServeCall` expect the new arguments; `daemon.TestDeliveryChecks` counts one more delivered paste; `deadlineRecorder` accepts a write deadline.

**Test results.** `./dev test` passes (40 packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 56 tests: 55 passed, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (`TestFleet` 55 s, `TestRelayService` 78 s, `TestWebApp` 76 s with the browser step). That run used a build from before the hub's per-directory cache of the newest generation; `./dev test ./internal/hub/ ./internal/fleet/` and `./dev itest -run 'TestFleet$|TestHub$' -v` passed again with a fresh build. An earlier targeted run found the push watchdog using the daemon's cached configuration (the hooks saw the new budget, the watchdog the old one); `serveGit` now reloads it, and `TestRelayService` passed. `web/check.sh` passes: 36 screens, 4 action runs and the logic checks, no errors or policy violations; the screenshot of the paged-back history was looked at. Push storage was measured with git 2.43 on the host before choosing thresholds (see D1-05 in the response). The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, images, daemons, hubs, browsers, loops or directories were left behind.

### C19r2 Fixes after the final review, part 2

All fifteen findings of `docs/dev/reviews/D2-review.md` and the unconfirmed D2-16 are resolved as the work order's decisions table directs; `docs/dev/reviews/D2-response.md` has one paragraph per ID with its tests. `docs/dev/LEFTOVER.md` was rewritten as one document for the user (see the end of this section). What changed:

- **Desktop limits (D2-01; `internal/config`, `internal/gui`, `assets/desktop/devc-desktop`).** Global `[gui] memory = "2g"`, `pids = 512`, `cpus = 2` (`config.GlobalGUI`, validated, in `devc config show` and the example configuration). `gui.LimitArgs` gives the sidecar `--memory`, `--pids-limit`, `--cpus`; a limit without its delegated controller fails `gui up` before anything changes; `"none"`/`-1` switch limits off. `box.Manager.HostFacts` exposes the cached host facts. The image's socat serves at most 64 X connections and websockify runs under a launcher with at most 16 handler processes (the image is rebuilt on the next `gui up`). The engine fake records `--memory`, `--pids-limit`, `--cpus`; `engine.HostConfig` gained `NanoCpus`.
- **Browser probe (D2-05; `internal/cli/guicmds.go`).** `browserAnswers`: no proxy, no redirects; a redirect means no browser.
- **GitHub (D2-02, D2-03, D2-09; `internal/box/capabilities.go`, `internal/github/mint.go`, `internal/daemon/github.go`).** Start records hold `github_upstream`, `github_app_id`, `github_installation`; `Capabilities.GitHubToken(r, web)` returns a `GitHubGrant` (mode, permissions, repository) only while mode, repository and App are those of the start. `Minter.Token(ctx, api, scope, app, key, repo, perms)`: key parsed before any cache lookup; caches keyed by API, project scope, App ID, key fingerprint (`KeyFingerprint`, SHA-256 of the public key), configured installation and repository; the installation looked up for the repository must belong to its owner (and be the configured one); a metadata-only probe token shows the one repository via `GET /installation/repositories` and is revoked; tokens are minted with `repository_ids`; a token whose answer lists other repositories is revoked and refused.
- **Service streams (D2-04; `internal/daemon/services.go`, `netproxy.go`).** After the head check a stream's limit is `limits.timeout` + `serviceGrace` (5 s): `relayConn.limit` clamps every deadline (the HTTP server's and the per-write stall), and a timer closes the stream at the limit.
- **Credentials and logins (D2-07, D2-08, D2-16; `internal/svclogin`, `internal/config/services.go`, `internal/cli/svccmds.go`).** `MinCredential = 8`; `ScrubValues` (scheme word not secret, Basic decoded only after `Basic`), `CheckCredential`, `HeaderCredential`; the proxy refuses a short part (503) and scrubs every value without a threshold; cookie values under 8 characters are refused in login output and stored jars; `devc service status` reports `invalid-secret`. `Login.Run` redacts every error against all secret values (longest first); output errors name cookies by position and JSON errors by kind and offset (`jsonProblem`). `NormalizeTarget` and `CheckRulePath` refuse percent-encoded reserved characters (`IsReserved`). The example `login.sh` leaves short cookies out.
- **Data sync (D2-06, D2-10, D2-11, D2-12; `internal/data`, `internal/boxside`, `internal/paths`).** `ParseItems` returns an error for any line that is not an item or one of rsync's two known messages (`ErrPreview`); `Totals` refuses overflow; a cut dry run is refused (`maxPreview`, 256 MiB). Backup directories `.devc-backup/<stamp>-<12 hex>` (`Service.Rand`). `paths.SyncLockFile` (`local/locks/sync-<realm>-<project>-<remote>.lock`) is held for a whole pull or push (3 s wait, then a clear refusal). The pull-in worker reports `remote_digests` (the staged tree); pull records carry `remote_digest`; `data status` compares remotes by it and marks a remote last pushed with the entry as it is now as `sent` (`>` in the table). The execx fake applies the capture cap.
- **Dependency sources (D2-13, D2-14; `internal/depsrc`).** Candidates without a checksum need `unchecked` (CRAN archive, GitHub tarballs); PyPI needs a 64-hex sha256, npm a usable SRI integrity (strongest of sha512/384/256/1, lengths checked; present-but-unusable is an error) or a 40-hex shasum, CRAN's current release a 32-hex MD5 (`PACKAGES` is read for every CRAN download). Concrete cached versions (registry versions, GitHub commits) are answered before any network access; `.alias-<asked>` files keep registry normalisations. The stand-in gained `PyPIDigests`, `NPMDist`, `CRANNoMD5`, `Requests()` and a normalised npm version.
- **Test searches (D2-15).** `credentialSearch` (service tests): positive control in a file and a process environment, exit status and an end marker required; GitHub's `boxSearch` requires its end marker.
- **Documentation.** Guide: desktop limits and relay bounds, the browser probe, App token checks and per-project caches, repository and App as start capabilities, service credential lengths, encoded reserved characters, the exchange deadline, login error redaction, pull preview refusal and the sync lock, backup directory names, remote observations in `data status`, checksum rules and offline cache of `devc src`. `examples/services/README.md` and `login.sh`.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **websockify has no connection limit of its own**; the image runs it through a short Python launcher that caps its handler processes (`multiprocessing.Process.start` waits while 16 run). If a future websockify stops using `multiprocessing`, the cap silently disappears while the container's PID and memory limits remain; `TestGUI` would notice (it counts the handlers).
2. **D2-03 uses a metadata-only probe token** to learn the repository ID, since no JWT-authenticated endpoint gives it; the probe never leaves the daemon and is revoked. The verification is cached (24 h, forgotten when a mint fails).
3. **D2-12: push records carry no remote observation.** devc never deletes on a remote and does not list it after a push, so a push can only claim that the remote received the entry as it is now (`sent`, `>`), never that the remote matches. A post-push listing is in LEFTOVER as deferred work.
4. **D2-13: CRAN's package list is now required for every CRAN download** (before, a named version skipped it when it could not be read): without it devc cannot tell whether a version should have a checksum.
5. **D2-07 also refuses short credentials at the proxy** as a last check, and the login secrets (passwords) are redacted from errors whatever their length (before: four characters or more).
6. **D2-11's lock lives in `local/locks/`**, not beside the staging directories, so staging listings stay clean; a second operation is refused after 3 s rather than queued (a pull can take hours).
7. **TigerVNC's blacklisting** (a sixth unauthenticated VNC connection from 127.0.0.1 is refused for a while) was found while testing D2-01; it lets a box keep the viewer out of its own desktop. Recorded as an accepted limit; raising `BlacklistThreshold` is listed as deferred.

**Upgrade notes.** Restart every box: token modes refuse boxes whose start record lacks `github_upstream`. Restart the daemon. Cookie jars with values shorter than 8 characters need a new `devc service login`; header credentials shorter than 8 characters need `devc secret set` again. Service rules with percent-encoded reserved characters are configuration problems now. Older pull records have no `remote_digest` (status shows their remote with `*` until the next pull). The desktop image is rebuilt on the next `devc gui up` (network).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Desktop: `bin/devc gui up <ref>`, `podman inspect devc-<realm>-<project>_gui --format '{{.HostConfig.Memory}} {{.HostConfig.PidsLimit}} {{.HostConfig.NanoCpus}}'`; set `[gui] cpus = 1.5` and `gui up --restart`. Services: `bin/devc secret set <realm> WIKI_TOKEN` with seven characters, then `bin/devc service status <realm> wiki` (`invalid-secret`). Data: two `bin/devc data push nas -p <ref> --yes` in a row and `ls <remote>/.devc-backup`; a pull while another runs is refused; `bin/devc data status <ref>` after pulling a zone with a sealed entry that differs. Sources: `devc src npm:<pkg>@<v>` twice, the second time with the network off. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/gui/ ./internal/config/ ./internal/github/ ./internal/box/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/svclogin/ ./internal/data/ ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/depsrc/... ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestGUI|TestGitHub|TestService|TestDataSync|TestDepSources' -v`.

**Tests.** New unit tests: `gui.TestLimitArgs`; `cli.TestBrowserProbeNoRedirect`; `daemon.TestServiceStreamDeadlines` (fails without the fix, checked); `svclogin.TestProxyShortCredential`; `data.TestSyncPreviewAndLock`, `TestPullRemoteObservation`; `depsrc.TestFetchChecksums`, `TestFetchCachedOffline`. Rewritten or extended: `gui.TestSidecarArgs`, `TestUpDownStatus`, `TestUpFailures`; `config` (five `gui.*` broken cases, defaults, reserved characters in rule paths); `github.TestMinter`; `box.TestGitHubCapabilities`; `daemon.TestGitHubToken`; `svclogin.TestScrubValues`, `TestNormalizeTarget`, `TestParseLoginOutput`, `TestLogin`; `data.TestParseItems`, `TestRsyncArgs`, `TestServicePush`, `TestStatusAndVerify`. Integration: `TestGUI` (cgroup limits read in the sidecar; 16 websockify handlers and 64 socat children with 24 and 66 connections held; counts fall back), `TestGitHubTokens` (mints restricted by repository ID through the fake's probe flow), `TestServiceHeader`/`TestServiceCookie` (credential searches with controls), `TestDataSync` (a push shows as sent).

**Changed tests of earlier chunks.** `TestDataSync` expected a push to make the remote "same"; it now accepts `sent`. The fakes of `daemon.TestGitHubToken`, `TestMinter` and `TestGitHubTokens` serve the installation's account, the probe and ID-restricted mints. Login and jar tests use cookie values of at least 8 characters. `TestSidecarArgs` expects the limit arguments.

**Test results.** `./dev test ./...` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 56 tests: 54 passed, `TestHarnessNetwork` was skipped as before, and `TestWebApp` failed once at the step that types into a control-mode terminal ("timed out after 30s waiting for the typed prompt"); run alone with the same build it passed (nothing in this chunk touches that path; recorded as a flake). Before the full run, targeted runs of `TestGUI`, `TestGitHub`, `TestGitHubTokens`, `TestServiceHeader`, `TestServiceCookie`, `TestDataSync` and `TestDepSources` passed (`TestDataSync` after its status expectation was updated for D2-12; `TestGUI` after its relay check was changed to partial HTTP requests, since WebSockets reach Xvnc's blacklisting). The desktop image was rebuilt for the new `devc-desktop`. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, daemons, hubs, forwards or directories were left behind; the desktop and installer images stay as caches.

**LEFTOVER.md** is now organised in six parts: to verify with a real login (per harness, then GitHub, services, registries and SSH remotes, each step with what a correct result looks like), to verify on another kind of machine, to set up before first real use, decisions waiting for the user, accepted limits by area, and deferred work. Items closed by later chunks were dropped (for example the hub-decision path, periodic context commits, the `extension` adapter, the old seccomp gap); nothing is marked as verified that was not.

### C19r3 Fixes after the verification of the last round, part 1

All fifteen findings of `docs/dev/reviews/E1-review.md` and the unconfirmed E1-16 (measured, confirmed) are resolved as the work order directs, including the redesigned retention of mutable files; `docs/dev/reviews/E1-response.md` has one paragraph per ID with its tests. What changed:

- **Hub archive (E1-02–E1-05, E1-09, E1-10; `internal/hub/archive.go`, new `retention.go`).** Admission: counters under their own lock (`admMu`, `inUse`, `per`), both places reserved together, refusals at once; `Server.admitted` returns a writer with the write-stall deadline for every admitted response (upload, file, hash, index, summary, secrets). New files go through `planNew` (names with every suffix against `statfs` `f_namelen` and 4096 bytes; file, missing directories and a new realm against the quota) and `create` (directories made and counted one by one; the returned undo removes files and directories and uncounts them); `appendNew` serves ref logs, bundle parts and secrets bundles. Usage counts directories (`max_files` covers both) and is guarded by `machineArchive.umu`, so summaries never wait on writes; a prune starts an epoch instead of locking every machine. `genCache`: bounded listing cache (64 directories, 100,000 generations, LRU), entered only after a successful upload into an existing directory, quota checked first. Records (`genMeta`) carry `completed` (hub clock, `completionTime`: never before an older generation's); `writeMeta` returns every error and syncs the directory; `appendFile` syncs the directory of a created file and cuts a failed append back; bundle completion syncs its directory. Classes: mutable only below `files/harness/` (400 otherwise, `archive.MutableAllowed`), a file keeps its first generation's class (409 with `class`), a complete mutable generation takes no more bytes (409 `complete`), completing a generation after a newer one is refused. Retention: pure `retentionDrops` (newest three complete, the state at each checkpoint of `keep_daily`/`keep_weekly`, nothing while the clock is behind the file's records, only generations recorded as mutable), applied by `retain` after a durable first completion with `unlinkat` beneath the file's directory. `hub.toml [archive] keep_daily = 7`, `keep_weekly = 4` (validated, 0–366 and 0–520). `Options.Now` (tests); the archive uses the server's clock. The index carries `completed`.
- **Archive package.** `MaxFileName` (238: the hub's longest suffix `~1000000~meta.tmp` fits in 255), `MutableAllowed`, `ErrorReply.Class`, `Entry.Completed`.
- **Uploader and restore (E1-01, E1-04, E1-06–E1-08; `internal/fleet`).** `snapshot(f, src, max)`: cap after every stat, bounded copy, returns the accepted copy's length and stat, which the generation uses. `send` uploads and completes; a completion conflict is reconciled through `fileConflict` (new generation when the stored one cannot represent the source, now also for a complete generation of another length) and the upload goes on, at most four rounds; `fileRefusal` adopts a class the hub names (`FileState.Fixed`, `adoptClass`), sends a 409 without a length back to the index without failing the pass, and skips a file the hub refuses with 400 (counted). New bookkeeping takes the class the hub holds from the index. `chooseGenerations` decides per generation (record and completion; no record; record without completion) and supports `Restorer.AsOf`; `RestoreReport` gained `as_of`, `as_of_current`, `as_of_later`, `as_of_grown`. `devc state pull --as-of <RFC 3339 | date>`.
- **Proxy (E1-12, E1-15; `internal/netpolicy/proxy.go`).** `watch` (replacing `guard`) registers the connection, checks the policy synchronously and starts the watch from that state; CONNECT and plain requests (new and reused upstreams) call it before acknowledging or forwarding anything. `headBudget` limits each head to 64 KiB minus what is buffered, for response heads and for the client's request heads.
- **Push watchdog (E1-16; `internal/execx`, `internal/gitx/host.go`, `internal/daemon/relay.go`).** `execx.Cmd.KillGroup`: own process group (or the command's session), cancellation SIGTERM to the group, the rest of the group killed as soon as the leader exits and before it is reaped (`waitid(WNOWAIT)`), then waited for until no member runs (zombies not counted, `/proc`), `ErrGroupLeft` after 30 s. `gitx.Host.Serve` and every command of a background host (the daemon) use it. `Daemon.runPush` (factored out of `serveGit`) runs receive-pack under the watchdog and removes quarantines only once the group is gone.
- **Web app (E1-11, E1-13, E1-14).** `api.command` keeps uncertainty across attempts (option `unsure`); `retryPending` keeps a refused retry pending unless the machine said the ID never arrived. `timeline.detach`/`newer`: paging back detaches the window; live events are counted (`newest`), not appended; older pages apply when the window's first event is unchanged and no full reload started; events go to the loading buffer before the detached check, so **Back to the latest** merges what arrives meanwhile. The session screen says how many newer events are not shown. `sw.js` cache `devc-shell-4`.
- **Documentation.** `docs/hub-deployment.md` section 10 (classes decided by the hub, retention window and its cost, names, directories in `max_files`, deadlines on every answer, new keys); guide (archive versions and the retention window, `state pull --as-of`, push ending with every git process, proxy rules checked again after connecting).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **The name limit is the hub file system's** (`statfs`), not a fixed 255: the toolchain container's `/tmp` (fuse-overlayfs) allows 251, which a fixed limit would have missed. The archive's own limit (238) assumes 255; a hub with a lower limit refuses a few more names with 400, and the uploader counts such a file as skipped instead of failing the pass (any 400 on a file is treated so).
2. **Checkpoint rule uses "at or before"** the checkpoint, the same rule as `--as-of`, so the state `state pull --as-of <checkpoint>` restores is the one retention keeps.
3. **Retention pauses while the hub's clock is behind a time it recorded for the file**; otherwise a clock stepping back would move the window back and could drop a state still at a checkpoint of the real window.
4. **A complete mutable generation is sealed** (no more bytes) and **completing a generation after a newer one is refused**: both keep completion times non-decreasing with the generation number and a checkpoint's state fixed. Neither happens with an honest uploader.
5. **`--as-of` for append-only files** restores the generation in use at that time with what was appended since (generations keep no history of their length); repositories are taken as they are now. Both are reported.
6. **KillGroup only for the daemon's git** (and every relay service). Git run by a command-line command stays in the terminal's foreground group so that prompts work and an interrupt reaches it (the CLI does not turn SIGINT into a cancellation); recorded in LEFTOVER.
7. **Paging back always detaches the window**, also when the page fits; the screen shows how many newer events there are.

**Upgrade notes.** Hubs and machines are upgraded together. Generations completed on an older hub have no completion time: retention keeps them and `--as-of` treats those files as current. The uploader's bookkeeping keeps version 2 (the new `fixed` field is optional). Restart the daemon for the proxy and push changes; reload the web app (new service worker cache).

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Hub: `[archive] keep_daily = 2` in `hub.toml`, upload a harness database from a box several times a day for a few days, then `ls $DEVC_HUB_DIR/archive/<machine>/<realm>/files/harness/...` (newest three plus one per checkpoint; `~meta` records hold `completed`). `bin/devc state pull <realm> --from <url> --as-of 2026-11-20` on a second machine. Push: `[git] relay_quota = "8m"`, push 60 MB from a box; the daemon log says the push was ended and `ps` shows no index-pack. Web app: `web/check.sh`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/hub/ ./internal/fleet/ ./internal/archive/ ./internal/netpolicy/ ./internal/execx/ ./internal/gitx/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestFleet$|TestHub$|TestRelayService|TestNetworkExceptions|TestWebApp' -v`.

**Tests.** New unit tests: `hub.TestCheckpoints`, `TestRetentionDrops`, `TestRetentionHostile`, `TestArchiveNamesAndDirectories`, `TestArchiveListingCache`, `TestArchiveClasses`, `TestArchiveRetentionConfig`, `TestArchiveDurableCompletion`, `TestArchiveAdmissionNoWait`, `TestArchiveStalledResponses`; `fleet.TestSnapshotLimits`, `TestEmptyReplacement`, `TestClassAdoption`, `TestRestoreMixedFormats`, `TestRestoreAsOf`; `archive.TestFileNameRoom`; `netpolicy.TestProxyTunnelStart`, `TestProxyHeadBudget`; `execx.TestKillGroup`; `daemon.TestPushEndedDuringIndexPack` (real git). `web/check.sh` logic checks for E1-11, E1-13 and E1-14. Checked against the old code: the proxy tests, the push test (thin-pack case) and the web logic checks fail there.

**Changed tests of earlier chunks.** `TestArchiveBudgets` (directories count against `max_files`: limit 9 with the counts explained; a refused upload leaves no directory; per-machine places taken through `admit`), `TestArchiveAdmission` (fills the global places through `admit` with other machines; checks the counters), `TestArchiveDownloadStall` (counter instead of channel), `TestArchiveGenerations` (a mutable generation of an append-only file is refused with the class; an incomplete mutable generation older than a complete one is dropped; usage compared with directories), `TestSecretsQuota` (a bundle counts one file and one directory; `max_files` 2).

**Test results.** `./dev test ./...` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 56 tests: 55 passed, `TestHarnessNetwork` skipped as before (`TestFleet` 53 s, `TestHub` 35 s, `TestRelayService` 82 s, `TestNetworkExceptions` 42 s, `TestWebApp` 78 s). After that run only the uploader's handling of a 400 changed (its snapshot is dropped); `./dev test ./internal/fleet/` and `./dev itest -run 'TestFleet$'` passed again with a fresh build. `web/check.sh` passes: 36 screens, 4 action runs and the logic checks, no errors or policy violations. The push measurement (git 2.43, a 200 MB push, before and after) is in E1-16 of the response. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, daemons, hubs, browsers, git processes, loops or directories were left behind.

### C19r4 Fixes after the verification of the last round, part 2

All nine findings of `docs/dev/reviews/E2-review.md` are resolved as the work order's decisions table directs; `docs/dev/reviews/E2-response.md` has one paragraph per ID with its tests and what was found in the neighbouring paths. The flaky `TestWebApp` step was a product bug in the web terminal, found in this chunk's full run and fixed (see below). What changed:

- **Desktop (E2-01; `internal/gui`, `internal/toolbox`, `internal/cli/guicmds.go`).** Labels `devc.gui.limits` (`gui.LimitsSpec`, from the creation arguments) and `devc.gui.image` (the image's `devc.aux.hash`, read back after `EnsureAuxImage`; `toolbox.LabelAuxHash`). `Manager.outdated` compares both with what `gui up` would create now; a running sidecar of the current box start that differs, lacks the labels, or has lost its record is refused with the difference and `--restart`, never replaced; `gui status` state `outdated`. `SidecarOptions.Generation`.
- **Credentials (E2-02, E2-07; `internal/svclogin`, `internal/daemon/services.go`, `internal/cli/svccmds.go`).** `HeaderCredential(header, stored) (Credential, error)` normalises once (trailing line ending, spaces and tabs around; control and non-ASCII bytes refused), splits words, treats a leading scheme from `authSchemes` as public, requires 8 characters of every other word and of Basic's password, and returns value and scrub list together (`ScrubValues`, `CheckCredential` removed). `Credential.check` is the proxy's last check (normal form, value scrubbed, no short or empty scrub value). `Jar.Credential(host, path, now)`: empty header means no `Cookie` and no empty scrub value. Cookie names and values trimmed where loaded (`normalizeCookie`). Login secrets also redacted without surrounding white space.
- **Sources (E2-03, E2-06; `internal/depsrc`).** `ParseDCFAll`/`ParseDCF` strict (CRLF, 1 MiB lines, scanner errors, malformed lines, repeated fields); `cranCurrent` parses all of `PACKAGES` (no stanza without a name, no package twice) and any error fails the download; a non-current CRAN version is fetched only from the archive. `concrete` accepts only exact semver for npm (`npmExactRE`). Stand-in: `CRANIndex`, `NPMLatest`, `mypkg_1.3.0` in `src/contrib`, `plain@1.1.0`.
- **Data sync (E2-04, E2-05; `internal/boxside`, `internal/data`, `internal/cli`).** `EntryDigest.Remote` and `.Outcome` (`merged`, `skipped`, `partial`) replace `DataResult.RemoteDigests`; `Record.Outcome`; status matches a remote only for `merged`. `boxside.MaxReport` (64 MiB) is capture (`RunOptions.MaxOutput`, truncation is an error) and decode limit; `MaxSyncEntries` (100,000) and `CheckPullEntries` (per-entry overhead plus JSON path size against the report's room) checked by the host on the preview (`previewEntries`, `PrintedPathCost`), by the worker before merging and before staging a push. `MarshalReport` (compact, no HTML escaping) for `devc box data-worker`; `capReport` caps skipped texts (512 bytes) and displaced paths (100, `DisplacedTotal`).
- **Lock names (E2-08; `internal/paths`).** `SyncLockFile` = `local/locks/sync/<realm>/<project>/<remote>.lock`.
- **Tests (E2-09).** `TestSyncPreviewAndLock` gives each refusal its own capture limit and message.
- **Web terminal (the flake; `internal/tmux`, `internal/daemon/terminal.go`).** Observing clients are no longer tmux read-only clients (see below).
- **Documentation.** Guide: outdated desktops, credential normal form and schemes, requests without cookies, CRAN index and archive, exact versions and moving names, pull entry limit, pull outcomes in `data status`. `LEFTOVER.md` updated for C19r3 and C19r4 (upgrade notes of both in part 3).

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **A running desktop without its record is refused too** (the work order names limits and image only): replacing it would also cost the programs on its display their display, so the same rule (`--restart`) applies.
2. **The image generation is the build files' hash**, as `EnsureAuxImage` labels it; `gui build --rebuild` (fresh base image, same files) does not make a running desktop outdated (recorded in LEFTOVER).
3. **The entry limit is checked three times**: on the preview (before the transfer, as the decision asks), on the staged tree before merging (a continued pull may hold more than its dry run lists), and before a push is staged (a push report has the same entries). The size part of the check uses a bound per name (JSON's worst case for bytes rsync escapes), so very long or escape-heavy names lower the number of entries a pull may have.
4. **The worker's tests lower its limits** (`DataWorker.syncEntries`, `reportBudget`): 100,001 files on the toolchain container's file system took minutes. The real values are tested without files: `CheckPullEntries` at 100,000 and 100,001 entries, the host's preview check at both, and decoding a 100,000-entry report.
5. **Schemes that are public** are a fixed list (`Basic`, `Bearer`, `Token`, `Bot`, `Key`, `ApiKey`, `Api-Key`, `SSWS`, `OAuth`); with any other first word that word is treated as a secret.

**Upgrade notes.** Desktops running from before are refused by `gui up` until `--restart`. Pull records from before have no outcome (their remote does not match until the next pull). Header credentials with control or non-ASCII bytes are refused. Restart the daemon.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Desktop: `bin/devc gui up <ref>`, change `[gui] memory`, `bin/devc gui up <ref>` (refused, names both specs), `bin/devc gui status <ref>` (`outdated`), `bin/devc gui up <ref> --restart`. Services: `printf '  Bearer <token> \n' | bin/devc secret set <realm> WIKI_TOKEN`, then a request from the box to an endpoint that reflects `Authorization`. Sources: `devc src npm:<pkg>@latest` twice, the second time it asks the registry. Data: pull a zone with a sealed entry whose file differs but has the same size, then `bin/devc data status <ref> --json` (`"outcome": "skipped"`, `"same": false`). Tests: `./dev test ./internal/gui/ ./internal/svclogin/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/depsrc/... ./internal/boxside/ ./internal/data/ ./internal/paths/ ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestGUI$|TestServiceHeader|TestServiceCookie|TestDataSync|TestDepSources' -v`.

**Tests.** New unit tests: `gui.TestOutdatedSidecar`; `svclogin.TestHeaderCredential` (replaces `TestScrubValues`), `TestProxyPaddedCredential`; `depsrc.TestParseDCF`, `TestFetchCRANIndex`, `TestFetchMovingNames`; `boxside.TestReportBounds`, `TestPullInEntryLimit`, `TestPullInSpecialEntry`; `data.TestPullEntryLimits`; `paths.TestSyncLockNames`. Extended: `tmux.TestObserve` (no read-only flag); `gui.TestSidecarArgs`, `TestLimitArgs`; `svclogin.TestProxyShortCredential`, `TestProxyCookieLogin`, `TestParseLoginOutput`, `TestJar`; `daemon.TestServiceStreams` (padded token reflected by the upstream, a 7-character token padded to 8 bytes, a path without cookies); `depsrc.TestFetchCachedOffline`; `data.TestPullRemoteObservation` (same-length sealed entry, special file), `TestSyncPreviewAndLock` (E2-09); `paths.TestLayout`. Integration: `TestGUI` (outdated desktop refused, untouched, replaced by `--restart` with the new memory limit); `TestWebApp` step 4 (a prompt sent while the terminal observes, after the observer has attached, is delivered and arrives). Checked against the old behaviour: listing-only matching fails `TestPullRemoteObservation`; ignoring `Totals`' error fails `TestSyncPreviewAndLock`.

**Changed tests of earlier chunks.** Credentials in the svclogin tests are built with `HeaderCredential` (`staticCred`, the long-key control); `TestSidecarArgs` expects the two labels; the data tests' worker fake prints with `MarshalReport` and applies the capture limit like the engine; `TestPullRemoteObservation` has five entries.

**The flaky `TestWebApp` step (product bug, fixed; `internal/tmux`, `internal/daemon/terminal.go`).** Not reproduced in 8 separate runs, 40 and 3 repetitions in temporary tests (removed) or 90 direct pseudo-terminal trials; the full integration run reproduced it, and its daemon log showed the send of step 4 ending `unknown` with "tmux: client is read-only". tmux refuses `send-keys` from the command line while a read-only client is attached to the most recently active session (reproduced every time with the static tmux 3.8), so a prompt sent while the web app observed was pasted without its submit key and stayed in front of the next input. `tmux.Observe` no longer uses `read-only`; the daemon (only writer of the client's terminal, refuses input to observing streams) and the hub enforce observing. `TestWebApp` step 4 waits for the observer's first screen, then requires `delivered` and the prompt event; it fails every time against the old flags (checked).

**Test results.** `./dev test ./...` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -v` ran all 56 tests: 54 passed, `TestHarnessNetwork` was skipped as before, and `TestWebApp` failed at the typed-prompt step, which led to the fix above (`TestGUI` 73 s with the new step, `TestDataSync` 37 s). After the fix `TestWebApp` passed five times in a row (each with `web/check.sh` in a headless browser, one while the unit suite ran); against the old observer flags its strengthened step 4 failed. The only other change after the full run (an entry that arrives as no data is reported as partial) was followed by `./dev itest -run 'TestDataSync|TestData$'` (passed) and the unit suite. Before the full run, `./dev itest -run 'TestGUI$|TestServiceHeader|TestServiceCookie|TestDataSync|TestDepSources'` passed. The desktop image did not change (same ID) and was not rebuilt. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, daemons, hubs, browsers, loops or directories were left behind; the temporary reproduction tests were removed.

### C19r5 Closing fixes

The four confirmed findings of the closing check (`docs/dev/reviews/F1-review.md`, `F2-review.md`) and the unconfirmed F1-U1 are resolved as the work order's table directs; `docs/dev/reviews/F-response.md` has one paragraph per ID with its tests. What changed:

- **Process groups (F1-01, F1-U1; `internal/execx`).** With `KillGroup` the leader is reaped last: `waitEnded` learns that it has ended without reaping it (`waitid(WEXITED|WNOWAIT)` on the pidfd from `os.Process.WithHandle`, else on the process ID), the group is killed and watched through `/proc` while the leader holds its ID, and only then does `group.reap` call `Cmd.Wait`. Every signal to the group (also `Cmd.Cancel`'s SIGTERM) goes through `group.signal`, which sends nothing once reaping has started. A group still there after 30 s: `*GroupLeftError{PGID, Gone}` (wraps `ErrGroupLeft`) joined with the other failure (the exit status from the `waitid` record, or the context), also from `Interactive`; a watcher goes on killing the group, reaps the leader and closes `Gone`. `capped` is locked (members of a left group may still write). Test seam `signalGroup`.
- **Held relay (F1-01; `internal/daemon/relay.go`).** `runPush` takes `release` (end the streams, the push note, the report files, the box lock): called after the quarantine is removed, or, for a group left, by `holdRelay` once `Gone` closes (quarantine removed, mark cleared, then `release`). Meanwhile `serveGit` refuses pushes to the relay with `heldRefusal`'s message (before taking the lock and after a lock timeout). `Daemon.heldRelays`.
- **`--as-of` (F1-02; `internal/fleet/restore.go`, `internal/cli/fleetcmds.go`).** Untimed generations count as older than every timed one: when every timed generation is newer, the file is chosen among the untimed ones (`chooseUsable`, the rules without `--as-of`) and listed in `RestoreReport.AsOfUntimed` (`as_of_untimed`), printed as "restored from a generation without a recorded time". Left out as newer only with neither.
- **Redirects (F2-01; `internal/depsrc/fetch.go`).** `candidate.redirect` limits redirects of downloads without a checksum, at every hop (`client`'s `CheckRedirect`); an unchecked candidate without a rule follows none. CRAN's archive: the same path (`samePath`). GitHub's tarball: the same owner, repository (without case) and commit (`sameTarball`). Checked downloads are unchanged.
- **npm versions (F2-02).** `npmExact` replaces `npmExactRE`: the bare form only, numbers without leading zeros up to 9007199254740991 (also numeric prerelease identifiers), identifiers of `[0-9A-Za-z-]`, at most 256 characters. `v1.2.3` is no longer exact. PyPI and CRAN checked: no change (see F-response).
- **Stand-in** (`depsrctest`): `NPMTags`, `CRANArchiveRedirect`, `GitHubRedirect`; `1.0.0+build.5` is normalised to `1.0.0`; everything below `/codeload/` serves the tarball.
- **Documentation.** Guide: a push whose processes cannot be ended holds the relay; `--as-of` and untimed versions; redirects of unchecked downloads; exact npm versions. `LEFTOVER.md`: the entries on unchecked downloads, exact npm versions, untimed generations, the upgrade notes of C19r3 and C19r4, and a new decision entry for the held relay.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **A member that survives SIGKILL cannot be made in a test.** `TestKillGroupLeft` uses real processes but has the runner send SIGTERM where it sends SIGKILL (`signalGroup`), so a member that ignores SIGTERM plays one in uninterruptible I/O. The relay's test (`TestPushGroupLeft`) gets the left group from a fake runner.
2. **The push note and the report files move with the lock**: in the held case they are handled when the group is gone (the hook may still write the report); the note may then fail to be recorded if the box's connection is gone (logged).
3. **A leader that cannot be waited for without reaping it** (never seen; it would mean something else reaped it) is reaped, nothing is signalled, and the error is a `GroupLeftError` whose `Gone` never closes, so the relay stays held until the daemon restarts (fail closed; LEFTOVER).
4. **Without a readable `/proc`** a group counts as running (the held leader keeps it alive for `kill(-pgid, 0)`), so `KillGroup` commands would report it left; Linux hosts always have `/proc`.
5. **CRAN's "same path" is the exact path** (host and scheme may change, within the existing rules); a mirror that redirects below another prefix would be refused.
6. **Numeric prerelease identifiers above 2^53-1** are not exact for devc although npm accepts them (as strings): stricter is still a subset.

**Upgrade notes.** Restart the daemon. Aliases stored for `v`-prefixed npm versions are ignored.

**How to exercise.** `./dev build`. Tests: `./dev test ./internal/execx/ ./internal/daemon/ ./internal/fleet/ ./internal/depsrc/... ./internal/cli/`; `./dev itest -run 'TestRelayService|TestFleet$|TestDepSources' -v`.

**Tests.** New unit tests: `execx.TestKillGroupLeft`, `daemon.TestPushGroupLeft`, `fleet.TestRestoreAsOfUntimed`, `depsrc.TestFetchRedirects`. Extended: `depsrc.TestFetchMovingNames` (moving tag `9007199254740992.0.0`, planted alias, `v1.0.0` online, boundary table), `TestFetchCachedOffline` (alias via `1.0.0+build.5`, `v1.0.0` needs the registry), `daemon.TestPushEndedDuringIndexPack` (`release` called once).

**Test results.** `./dev test ./...` passes (all packages); `./dev vet` (with the integration tag) and gofmt are clean. `./dev itest -run 'TestRelayService|TestFleet$|TestDepSources' -v`: all three passed (`TestRelayService` 89 s, `TestFleet` 57 s, `TestDepSources` 39 s); the rest of the integration suite was not run (work order). The new tests were not run against the old code (they use the new seams and types); by construction the old runner reaped the leader first and dropped the group error behind an exit status or cancellation, the old restore left the reviewer's file out, and the old client followed the redirect. The race detector was not run (see LEFTOVER). No test containers, daemons, processes, loops or directories were left behind.

### X1 Explorer: the tool

**What exists.**

- `explorer/tool` (Go, `package main`, about 7,000 lines with tests; standard library, BurntSushi/toml, and through `internal/cli` cobra and pflag). `load.go`: `go list -e -json -deps -test -export -tags integration ./...`, then every module package parsed and type-checked from source in one universe (each package with its in-package test files, external test packages as leaves after everything), external packages imported from export data with `go/importer.ForCompiler("gc", lookup)`. `extract.go`: symbols (functions, methods, types, vars, consts, struct fields including nested anonymous ones, interface methods), every use with its enclosing declaration, the call graph (calls and mentions; a package variable whose initializer mentions functions is a node in between; calls through a module interface fan out to every module type implementing it, marked dynamic), implementations. `source.go`: line counts, file and package comments, highlighting tokens and links with UTF-16 offsets, the other files (`web/` without `vendor/`, `assets/`, `examples/`, `test/`, `dev`, `go.mod`), chunks. `commands.go`: the real command tree through the new `cli.CommandTree`, built with every `DEVC_` variable unset and the roots, HOME and the XDG directories pointing at an empty temporary directory; run functions mapped by program counter to file, line and the declared function. `tables.go`: RPC methods with classified uses, events, routes (constant expressions evaluated), configuration keys, environment variables. `reach.go`: where code runs; path search. `docs.go`: documents, headings, findings with responses, work orders, text patterns. `model.go`, `refs.go`, `check.go`: the model, references with near-miss suggestions, the checks, flow connections. `output.go`: the site. `query.go`, `comments.go`: see below. `schema.go`: the data contract, every type and field commented.
- `explorer/model`: `packages.toml` (complete), `system.toml` (6 zones, 27 actors, 30 channels), `interfaces.toml` (15 surfaces: 4 rpc, events, routes, commands, 7 configuration files, env), `layout.toml` (configuration, state and runtime trees to two levels; 12 mounts), `glossary.toml` (15 terms), `concerns/sessions-and-events.md` with 6 invariants and one diagram, `flows/send-prompt.toml` (27 steps over 7 actors), `flows/box-channel.toml` (a tour), `overview.md` (placeholder). Everything was checked against the code.
- `explorer/app`: a placeholder (`index.html`, `app.js`) that lists the packages with their roles and shows a file with highlighting and links.
- `./dev explore [args]` runs the tool in the toolchain container; `/explorer/dist/` is ignored by git. `internal/cli.CommandTree` is the one change outside `explorer/`, `dev`, `.gitignore` and the documents.

**Layers** (bottom to top): embedded files, foundations, shared vocabulary, areas, assembly, services, entry points, tests. **Groups**: configuration and layout, boxes, harnesses and sessions, git and shared trees, data, network and brokered access, protocol, machine daemon, hub and archive, utilities, command line, tests. No package imports from a higher layer.

**How to run.** `./dev explore` builds `explorer/dist/` and prints its `file://` URL; `./dev explore --check` only checks; `--strict` fails on warnings; `--model` and `--out` take other directories below the repository. `./dev explore query help` lists the questions (outline of a package or file, a symbol, callers, calls, uses, a path between two functions, find). `./dev explore comments-only [commit]` reports Go files whose code, comments aside, differs from the commit (exit 1 if any).

**Build time and size.** The tool takes 2.8 s on this host (about 5 s for `./dev explore` with the container and a cached compile). Output: core 4.4 MB, 70 chunks 22.2 MB (the largest, `internal/hub`, 2.4 MB), 64 documents 1.1 MB, 27.7 MB in all. Extracted: 47 packages, 11,099 symbols, 74,216 uses, 231 commands, 51 RPC methods, 31 routes, 12 event types, 210 configuration keys, 51 environment variables, 138 findings, 34 work orders; no type errors.

**Warnings that remain** (`--check`: 0 errors, 464 warnings): 251 non-test Go files belong to no concern and 213 files of multi-file packages have no file comment; both shrink as the content chunks write concerns and comments. No flow, layering or type-error warnings.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. **Test variants.** Instead of type-checking `go list`'s test variants separately, each package is checked once with its in-package test files (they cannot import a package that imports the package under test, so nothing cycles), and external test packages come last. Every object then has one identity, which the call graph, implementations and references rely on. The repository has no external test packages; the loader supports them.
2. **Additions the later work orders and the content guide rely on**: `query` and `comments-only` (named in `X-content-guide.md`), `--model` (X3).
3. **Format additions, all in `explorer/README.md`**: the reference kinds `diagram:`, `surface:`, `layer:`, `group:` and whole documents (`doc:<path>`); finding IDs `<review>/<label>` where two reviews use the same label (review A has B1, B2, C1, C2 and D1 of its own; code comments mean review B's B1); a command in an actor's `commands` stands for its subcommands too unless another actor names them (`Command.Actor` gives each command one actor); `tests` on invariants; `via = "cmd:<command>"` and `"reply"`; the rules of the flow check (paths from any earlier step of the actor, shortest first, at most 8 calls, a sender check for RPC methods and events, what needs `unchecked`); the extraction fields of surfaces, including `listed_in` (the hub link's methods come from the `hubMethods` allowlist); type errors as warnings.
4. **Reach of a command** starts from what its run function's literal mentions, not from the whole constructor, which often builds several commands (`newBoxCmd` holds every `devc box` command).
5. **File comments** count only above the package clause, as the README says. Some files describe themselves just after it (`internal/hub/front.go`, `internal/config/schema.go`); they are warned about until the content chunks move those comments.
6. **Files excluded by build constraints** (two in `internal/boxside`) are shown without annotations and declare no symbols.
7. **"README rule N"** links to the section of `docs/dev/README.md` that lists the rules (its rules are a list, not headings).
8. **The model found an outdated sentence in the plan**: plan 3.11 names the agent's socket `/run/devc/helper.sock`; the code uses `/run/devc/agent.sock` for the host and `@devc-agent` for clients in the box. The model follows the code.

**Tests.** Unit tests beside the tool with a synthetic module in `testdata/mod`: symbols and implementations, the classification of RPC uses (map keys and index assignments, factories, cases, struct fields, arguments, allowlists), paths (static, dynamic, through dispatch variables, to fields, never through test doubles), flow connections (paths, earlier steps, RPC with sender check, unchecked, unfollowable messages), references (every kind, near misses), strict model loading and front matter, document patterns (and that their expressions are portable to JavaScript), headings, findings and responses. `internal/cli.TestCommandTree`.

**Test results.** `./dev test ./explorer/tool/ ./internal/cli/` pass; `./dev vet` and gofmt are clean. The site was opened from `file://` in headless Chrome: no console errors; a source file renders with highlighting, every link's text matches its target (checked over four files, including ones with non-ASCII comments), and following a link shows the definition. The integration suite was not run (nothing it covers changed).

### X2 Explorer: the app, part 1 (shell and the views of extracted facts)

**What exists.** `explorer/app/` (about 5,200 lines of plain HTML, CSS and JavaScript; classic scripts, no library, nothing fetched; the tool copies the directory as it is). Everything hangs off one global `X`; each file adds one member and starts with a comment saying what it is.

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| `index.html` | The page: theme set before the first paint, `explorerRegister`, the SVG icon sprite (`#i-<name>`), the scripts in load order. |
| `css/theme.css` | Tokens for both themes, the zone colours (`--z-<trust>`, classes `.z-<trust>` set `--z`), base elements. |
| `css/shell.css`, `parts.css`, `source.css`, `views.css` | Shell and popups; shared parts; the source view; the views of this chunk. |
| `js/util.js` | `X.util`: `esc`, `h` (build elements), `icon`, `load`/`save` (storage that never throws), `firstSentence`, `fmt`, `copy`, `toast`, `debounce`. |
| `js/settings.js` | `X.settings`: theme, tests shown, inspector width, editor link (presets VS Code and Cursor), `editorURL(file, line)`. |
| `js/data.js` | `X.data`: the Core indexed (`sym`, `pkg`, `file`, `cmd`, `rpc`, `route`, `event`, `config`, `env`, `document`, `finding`, `workOrder`, every model object), `children`, `symbolsOfFile`, `implementationsOf`, `zonesOfActors`; loaders returning promises: `chunk`, `chunkOfPackage`, `source(path)`, `doc(path)`, `allChunks(progress)`. |
| `js/refs.js` | `X.refs`: reference syntax, `resolve`, `chip`, `href` (full view), `card` (hover card), `define(kind, spec)`, `badge`, `zoneMark`, `actorList`; the shared click behaviour. |
| `js/doc.js` | `X.doc`: Go doc comments (`render`), inline text (`inline`), the build's patterns (`findRefs`, `linkify`), `highlightGo`, `plain`. |
| `js/router.js` | `X.router`: `#/<view>/<rest>?k=v`, `register`, `href`, `hrefKeep` (keeps the inspector), `go`, `replace`, `setParams`, `onChange`, `remount`. |
| `js/hovercard.js`, `js/inspector.js`, `js/connectors.js`, `js/table.js`, `js/source.js` | The shared parts below. |
| `js/symbol.js` | `X.symbolPanel.render(el, ref, {excerpt})`: signature, doc, where it may run, members, implementations, used by, calls; the inspector's symbol view. |
| `js/search.js` | `X.search`: names and text in code. |
| `js/views/placeholder.js` | "Not built yet" pages for overview, runtime, concerns, flows, state, glossary, docs; the page for an unknown route. |
| `js/views/code.js` | Code view (`#/code/<path>?sym=&line=`), tree, outline; the inspector's file view. |
| `js/views/packages.js` | Package map (`#/packages`, `#/packages/size`, `?color=group|zone|tests&all=1`); `X.pkgTiles`. |
| `js/views/package.js` | Package page (`#/pkg/<path>`); the inspector's package summary. |
| `js/views/interfaces.js` | `#/interfaces/<surface>?q=&sel=`; the inspector's views of commands, RPC methods, routes, events, configuration keys, environment variables. |
| `js/main.js` | The top bar, settings popover, start. |
| `explorer/check.sh` | The check (below). |

**Shared parts and how X3 uses them.**

- **References.** Write `X.refs.chip(ref)` (HTML string) for any reference; options `label`, `cls` (`inline` in prose, `flat`, `small`), `plain` (no icon), `act: "full"`, `noZone`. Any element with `data-ref` gets the hover card, click to inspect, Ctrl/Cmd+click to open in full; a container that handles a click itself calls `preventDefault()`. Model kinds (actor, zone, channel, concern, flow, term, diagram, surface, layer, group) resolve, label and colour already, with a simple card; X3 calls `X.refs.define(kind, {card, href, label})` to give them their real card and full-view URL (the current `href`s point at the X3 routes: `#/runtime?sel=actor:x`, `#/concerns/<id>`, `#/flows/<id>`, `#/glossary?sel=<id>`, `#/docs/<path>?h=<anchor>`, `#/docs/findings?sel=<id>`; change them freely).
- **Hover cards** need nothing: `data-ref` is enough; `data-nocard` suppresses one.
- **Inspector.** `X.inspector.register(kind, function (info, body, ctx) { …; return cleanup; })` for actor, term, finding, concern, flow, channel, zone; `X.inspector.open(ref)`; its target is the URL parameter `i`. `body.classList.add("fill")` makes the body a flex column for a renderer with its own scroller (see the file view in `code.js`). The `.insp-pad` class gives the usual padding.
- **Source view.** `X.source.view(scroller, {path, from, to, range, line, scroll, wrap, onIdent, onLine})` → `{ready, setLine, setRange, scrollTo, firstVisibleLine, expandAll}`; `X.source.header(path)`; `X.source.declStart(sf, sym)`. For the flow player, show the step's code with `X.symbolPanel.render(el, ref, {excerpt: true})` or open it in the inspector. Margin marks: `X.source.addMarks(function (path) { return [{line, ref, cls, title}]; })`, called whenever a file is rendered (style `.mkx.<cls>`).
- **Connector layer.** `var L = X.connectors.create(host, {above})`; `L.set([{from, to, cls, arrow, curve, label, sides: "auto"|"v"|"h"}])`; it redraws on resize; `L.destroy()` on unmount. Endpoints on one side of a box are spread; classes `imp`, `by`, `hl`, `dim`, `all`, `faint` exist in `parts.css`; add your own as `.cx.<cls> .cx-edge`. The host must be positioned and should be the scrolling element or inside it. Self-loops and parallel edges between the same pair are not separated yet (X3's diagram renderer must offset them, for example with per-edge `sides` or by adding a curve offset to `connectors.js`).
- **Doc renderer.** `X.doc.render(text, {pkg})` for Go doc comments; `X.doc.inline(text, ctx)` for one paragraph: patterns, `[[ref]]`/`[[ref|text]]`, `` `code` `` resolving to one symbol, package or file, Go `[Name]` doc links, package and file paths, URLs. X3's Markdown renderer should reuse `inline` for its text runs.
- **Table.** `X.table.create(container, {columns: [{id, title, html, sort, width, cls}], rows, key, text, query, onQuery, sort, tree: {depth, parent}, selected, onSelect})`; a cell link with `data-row` selects its row instead of inspecting by itself; columns with `cls: "opt"` hide below 860 px.
- **Search.** `X.search.addSource({kind, title, order, items: () => [{ref, name, keys, detail, exported, weight}]})` adds concerns, flows, terms and headings.
- **Views.** `X.router.register(name, {nav, title, render(el, route), update(route, prev), unmount})`; a later registration replaces the placeholder of the same name, so X3's files load after `placeholder.js` and before `main.js`.
- **Colours.** Zone colours only through `.z-<trust>`/`var(--z-<trust>)`; `X.refs.zoneDot(zone)`, `zoneMark(zones)`, `actorList(ids)`.

**Design choices.** Zone colours were validated as a categorical palette with the dataviz validator (six colours, all pairs, both themes: worst normal-vision ΔE 16.8, worst colour-blind ΔE 9.3; external is a deliberate neutral); one accent (blue) for interaction; syntax colours restrained. Group colours are evenly spaced muted hues (12 groups exceed what hue alone can separate), so every tile also prints its group and the legend highlights a group on hover. The source view renders whole files (2,042 lines in under 0.1 s), so the browser's find works and no find-in-file was needed; line numbers are generated content (not found, not copied). Back and forward restore the source's scroll position. Ligatures are off in code.

**Deviations.** None from the work order. No change to the tool or `schema.go` was needed: every view works from the data X1 emits. `explorer/README.md` gained one line (`explorer/check.sh`). The X1 placeholder `app.js` is gone.

**How to check.** `explorer/check.sh [--no-build] [dir]` builds the site, opens it from `file://` in headless Chrome, visits 40 routes (every view of this chunk, inspector views of each kind, every surface, extremes chosen from the data: the largest file, the package with most files, the most used symbol, an interface, a file that is not Go) in light and dark at 1280 pixels and a set at 1920, saves 94 screenshots plus the search and hover card, fails on any console error or exception, and asserts: search finds the most used symbol through the dialog and opens it; text search finds lines; a link in the source lands on the definition with its symbol panel, and back returns; a hover card appears; the inspector's back button returns and the URL follows; the largest package page lists all its files; 291 references (every model reference plus one of each kind and three that do not exist) render as chips, cards and inspector views; the app starts within 1.5 s and the largest file opens within 1.5 s. Result: all pass; start 135 ms (first contentful paint about 50 ms), the largest file 80–150 ms, text search over all code 8 ms once loaded.

### X3 Explorer: the app, part 2 (the views of the model)

**What exists.** Every view of the explorer is built; `explorer/app/` grew by about 3,700 lines (plain HTML, CSS, JavaScript; classic scripts, nothing fetched).

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| `js/markdown.js` | `X.md`: Markdown for the model and the documents (headings with `data-anchor`, emphasis, code, fenced code highlighted for go/toml/sh/json/…, nested lists, tables, quotes, links to documents and files as references, `[[ref]]`, the build's patterns through `X.doc.inline`); `headings`, `slug` (as the tool's), `plainText`, `cardText`. Raw HTML is escaped. |
| `js/model.js` | `X.model`: step numbering (`steps`, `stepAt`, `noOf`), the channel of a message (`channelOf`), whether it crosses a trust boundary (`crosses`: the channel's `boundary`, else the trust of the two zones), who takes part in what, the reading order, writer colours, `refsIn`, `symbolName`. |
| `js/backlinks.js` | `X.backlinks`: one index from every reference to where the model mentions it (concern packages, files, key symbols, invariants, tests, text; flow steps, checks, messages, text; terms; layout entries and mounts; diagram nodes and edges; actor entry points; channel checks; surfaces); `of`, `inPackage`, `section` (the symbol panel's "In the model"), `packageSection`; the source margin marks (flow step ●, key symbol ○, enforcement ◆). |
| `js/graph.js` | `X.graph.render(el, diagram, {concern})`: concern diagrams on their grid, edges routed orthogonally, labels off the boxes, loops, hover lights a node's edges. |
| `js/views/runtime.js` | Runtime view, `X.runtimeMap` (also small on the overview), Boundaries table, filters by concern or flow, the inspector's actor, channel and zone views, their cards. |
| `js/views/flows.js` | Flows index, player, `X.seq` (sequence diagram), compact step list, step panel with connections; the flow card and inspector view. |
| `js/views/concerns.js` | Concerns index and page (diagrams placed where the text names them, invariants, side column with a small layer picture), concern and diagram cards and inspector views. |
| `js/views/overview.js`, `state.js`, `glossary.js`, `docs.js` | Overview (reading order with checkboxes in localStorage, small map, legend, numbers); State (folding trees, writer marks, mounts as two sides); Glossary (filter, letters, term cards); Documents (list, document with outline, findings table; inspector views of a document section, a finding, a work order). |
| `css/model.css` | Styles of all of the above. |

**Shared parts changed.**

- `connectors.js`: kept its API and added an orthogonal router (`router: "grid"`, for edges with `avoid`): A* over a grid of 6-pixel cells around the obstacles, a cost for turns and for cells earlier edges took, so parallel edges run side by side; loops on the least used side; hit areas with `data-ref` (`hit`), HTML labels placed where they cover no obstacle (`labels: "html"`, `labelWhen`, `labelAway`), `onDraw`, `edgeOf`. `setClass` now changes classes in place instead of redrawing.
- `source.js`: margin marks take `href` (a click goes to the flow step or the concern). `symbol.js` shows "In the model"; `package.js` has an "In the model" section and the summary lists the package's concerns. `data.js` turns a document's `null` headings into `[]` (the tool writes nil slices as null). `placeholder.js` keeps only the not-found page.
- A CSS fix for X2's chips: `.chip + .chip` matched across the text between two inline chips and put a gap before the second; inline chips have no margin now.

**Tool and schema.** `Finding.Cited` and `WorkOrder.Cited` (`[]Site`): the places in Go comments, tests included, that mention a finding or work order as the patterns link them, with the declaration the comment documents or sits in (`citeInComments` in `docs.go`, test `TestCiteInComments`; the synthetic module's `server.go` gained two comments on existing lines). The findings table needs them and the app cannot compute them without loading all 22 MB of chunks. No change to the model's format.

**Deviations and choices.**

1. The runtime map's actors are placed by the app inside each zone (slots of the zone's grid, a local search that shortens channels); the model gives only the zones' grid. Channels are routed through the gaps; boundary channels are heavier with a gate where they enter the receiving zone. The small map on the overview shows zones and actors without channels: at that size they were noise.
2. The reading order is the concerns and flows in the order `overview.md` first names them, then the rest in concern order with their flows; there is no reading-order field in the format (documented in "Writing for the explorer").
3. A message that crosses a trust boundary is the channel's `boundary` where a channel joins the two actors (a reply uses the channel of its call), else ends in zones of different trust.
4. Full views of model objects: an actor, zone or channel is the runtime map with the inspector on it (`#/runtime?i=actor:x`); a diagram is its concern page (`?diagram=`); a heading is `#/docs/<path>?h=<anchor or number>`.
5. The package map's import lines stay as X2 drew them; routing them was tried and looked worse (see LEFTOVER).
6. `explorer/check.sh` gained `--site <dir>` (check a site built with `--out`) and `--shot '<route>'` (four screenshots of one page, both themes, 1280 and 1920, and its console errors) for the authors of the model.
7. The temporary large model lived under `bin/` (git-ignored), not in a `/tmp` directory: `--model` must be below the repository, the only directory the toolchain container mounts.

**How to check.** `explorer/check.sh [dir]` now also visits the overview, runtime (with an actor, a channel and a flow filter), concerns (index, page, diagram), flows (index, player, tour), state, glossary, documents, a document, the findings, and the inspector views of a flow, concern, term and finding, in both themes (138 screenshots), and asserts: pressing j/k steps through a flow and the diagram, step panel, URL and inspector follow; a reference in a concern's text opens the inspector; the concern page shows all its diagrams; the runtime map draws a connector for every channel (all routed) and a gate on every boundary channel, in about 100 ms; hovering a `[[term:…]]` shows its definition; the findings table lists where a finding is cited and a citation opens the code at its line; a flow step's declaration carries a margin mark and its symbol panel lists the backlinks; search finds a concern; the largest document renders in under 100 ms. Result: all pass on the model and on the large temporary model (40 concerns, 60 flows, a 40-step and a ten-actor flow, a 15-node diagram, a concern three times as long, 100 terms). `./dev test ./explorer/tool/` passes.
